## 2026-08-14: P3-2b — kappa-scaled theory, continuous-m sampling, and the attribution result (D51-D52)

**D51 — the acceptance criterion compares against a kappa-SCALED theoretical slope, decided before any P3-2b result existed.** The P3-2b spec specified `a_theory = c/(4 pi D)` (6.066 Hz per unit m at L=4.5). That is the RAW Lorentzian slope. Our estimator measures a **calibrated** -3 dB width carrying the P3-2 gate's T5 fit `BW = 0.302 + 1.6608*(gamma/pi)`; the intercept cancels in a paired delta but **the slope does not**. Correct value: `a_theory = kappa*c/(4 pi D)` = 10.073 Hz per unit m, with kappa = 1.6607564051417665 frozen from the P3-2 gate and hash-pinned. **Confirmed independently after the fact**: fitting the 240 simulated ground-truth m-response points across 12 (geometry, wall) cells gives mean rho = 1.0050, range [0.947, 1.084], every r^2 >= 0.9996; against raw theory the same GT fits read rho = 1.669. The failure mode this averted was NOT a false negative but a **false positive**: on a mid-training checkpoint the model read rho = 0.590 kappa-scaled and **0.980 raw**, i.e. the raw formula would have made a clearly unconverged model look like a near-perfect physics match. Both numbers are reported (`rho_vs_raw_theory_median`). Thresholds live in a frozen mapping whose sha (`a8479c5e1dcc...`) is written into `verdict.json` and pinned by a test, so they cannot be softened after seeing results. **Revisit if** the estimator's calibration changes (a new gate fit) — kappa must be re-derived, not carried over.

**D52 — P3-2's compositional failure was caused by the TRAINING DISTRIBUTION, not by the fit and not mainly by the conditioning parameterization; adopt continuous sampling in m, with the m-coordinate as the recommended encoder.** The 4-arm ablation isolates it: A (P3-2's 440 preset configs) and B (960 continuous configs) share an encoder, a renderer (`n_pts_per_ray=64`) and an eval, and differ ONLY in the sampled absorptions — S2 edit slope goes **0.153 -> 1.147**, Pearson 0.499 -> 0.871, edit_gain 0.868 -> 1.084. P3-2 sampled alpha at 3 preset values on single walls, i.e. **11 distinct alpha-vectors in a 4-D space**, and the model memorized (wall, alpha) pairs. Three further findings: (a) the **m-coordinate is not necessary but is materially better calibrated** — C's slope 0.959 vs B's 1.147 and rho 0.971 vs 1.045, with **rho = 0.99991 outside the held-out slab**, so it recovers the physical law essentially exactly while B overshoots ~15%; (b) **multi-wall training is not necessary** (arm D passes on single-wall configs alone, at a cost in in-distribution fit); (c) **fit quality is ANTI-correlated with edit transfer** — arm A has the best in-dist val LSD (0.931) of all four arms and is the only failure, which is the second independent refutation of P3-2's "a sharper recipe, not a different conditioning design" recommendation (written by this agent; wrong). The sharpest single diagnostic is rho inside vs outside the held-out slab: **A 1.060 -> 0.509**, i.e. arm A learns the law correctly everywhere EXCEPT the region the holdout defines, while B/C/D pass straight through (1.039 / 0.947 / 1.031). Also verified: P3-2's two holdouts were never comparable tests -- `(west,0.50)` interpolated that wall's trained range {0.05,0.15,0.70} while `(north,0.70)` EXTRAPOLATED beyond north's maximum of 0.50; P3-2b's slabs are both strictly interior. **Adopt arm C.** **Revisit / generalize**: the binding constraint was sampling density in the *physical parameter* space, which suggests the same audit for P3-1's 3D geometry conditioning (trained on a comparably sparse grid) before concluding anything about its conditioning mechanism.

## 2026-08-15: P3-2c — derivation sampler, designated S2, realized-gap axis, and the control failure (D53)

**D53 — P3-2c's cross-arm density sweep is CONFOUNDED by its own repair mechanism; the reportable result is the within-run extrapolation curve.** (a) **Derivation, not resampling.** Arms must differ only in the west hold-out band, which rules out re-running the sampler: `draw_alpha` rejects in a loop and `WALLS_2D` puts west first, so a west rejection shifts east/south/north in every four-wall config. Each arm's manifest is therefore DERIVED from the frozen W015 rows using an arm-independent, position-keyed repair stream `default_rng([REPAIR_SEED, geom_id, row_i, wall_slot])`: an arm's value is the first entry of `[W015_value] + repair_stream` its own predicate accepts, so two arms differ **iff** one rejects the other's value. Verified: W015 re-derives with **0** changes (so reusing the trained P3-2b arm C is exact, not approximate), deltas are exactly 31/120/236/156, north draws are byte-identical across all five manifests, and 479 new sims replace a naive 5850 (92% reuse). (b) **S2 membership is DESIGNATED, not inferred.** W100's slab `[0.193, 1.193]` contains alpha=0.30 (m=0.3567), so an "is it in THIS arm's slab" rule would migrate 10 `west@0.30` rooms S4->S2 for W100 alone, giving it a 30-room headline against everyone else's 20 — five well-formed slopes, no error, and a curve confounding gap width with population change. Every arm therefore reuses `p3_2b_splits.classify`, which keys on the frozen W015 slabs; arm-specific facts attach as annotations (`d_support_m`, `arm_holdout`). That annotation immediately caught that **XTRAP's `west@0.50` is TRAINED** (m=0.6931 < its threshold 1.10, `d_support` 0.0001) — unflagged it would have read as a spectacular pass at the widest exclusion in the sweep. (c) **The x-axis is the REALIZED gap, never the nominal width**, and for the edge-excluded arm it is beyond-edge distance, never a gap: XTRAP's `realized_gap_west` is 0.0262 (the ordinary sampling gap of a contiguous region), and plotting that beside the interior gaps would place the extrapolation arm at the dense end of the curve. (d) **THE RESULT: the design does not identify what it was built to measure.** The pre-registered north control — identical slab, byte-identical draws in all four arms — moves *perfectly monotonically* with the manipulation (Spearman **1.000**, spread **0.316** vs a 0.15 tolerance) while west does not (Spearman **-0.400**, spread 0.350). The mechanism is the repair stream itself: repairing a rejected west draw pushes it OUT of the slab toward the extremes of m, so a wider slab does not merely remove a band — it re-shapes the marginal distribution of west absorptions and with it the model's global allocation. North's rooms are the same rooms; their west walls are not. The two gated metrics agree by disagreeing: rho never crosses 0.80 (bound > 1.0033), slope crosses at 0.7542, and **49.5%** of paired bootstrap resamples show no crossing. **No west-specific gap effect is identifiable from this design.** (e) **What IS reportable** is XTRAP's within-run curve — one model, immune to cross-arm realization noise: edit slope 0.917 / 0.597 / 0.313 at +0.106 / +0.288 / +0.511 beyond the training edge, monotone, crossing 0.80 at **Δm ≈ 0.173**, and cleared by a selection-bias check (93 of 93-94 modes always-valid; full-pool and always-valid slopes agree to 3 dp) with GT effect size *rising* (6.162 -> 8.204 Hz) while the model's response falls. **Alternatives rejected**: inferring S2 per arm (rejected, (b)); nominal slab width as x (rejected, (c)); reporting either breakpoint metric alone (rejected — the disagreement is evidence, not noise to be resolved by picking). **Revisit**: a future sweep must hold the training marginal fixed BY CONSTRUCTION (resample so the out-of-slab m distribution matches across arms) **and** use replicate seeds; one seed per arm cannot separate the two explanations even in principle.

## 2026-08-15: FT-1 — FDTD over per-bin Helmholtz, and the limits of a one-geometry validation (D54)

**D54 — the wave solver is FDTD, it is correct and affordable, and its GO is conditional.** (a) **FDTD, not per-bin Helmholtz**: Helmholtz needs a sparse factorization per frequency (~49 ms x 601 bins ~ 30 s/room real, 60-90 s complex) while node-centred SLF leapfrog measures **0.83 s per 2 s room** on one pinned core and yields all 601 bins, all receivers and the full IR in one run. Projected **0.231 CPU-h per 1000 configs** against a 12 CPU-h budget (52x headroom), and interior structure — divider, aperture, absorber patch — costs **nothing measurable** (2.8% spread) because it runs the same dense code path as an empty box. Frozen: dx=0.05 (divides 4.5/4.0/8.0/3.0 exactly; dx=0.06 does not and its ~1% frequency shift would consume the entire A1a budget), fs_sim=12288, N=24576, T=2.000 s exactly, lambda_CFL=0.5583 (bound 0.7071), giving rfft bins at exactly 0.5 Hz so `H_complex` is a slice with zero resampling. Source must satisfy S(0)=0 — a DC component drives the undamped (0,0) mode of a rigid room without bound. (b) **Gates assert against the WAVE law and merely report ISM.** ISM and a locally-reacting wall disagree by 30-44% on uniform alpha (1.972/2.218/2.968 vs 2.843/2.957/3.867 Hz) because of grazing-incidence absorption — real physics, not solver error — so A2 asserts within 10% of Kuttruff. Measured worst +8.37%. (c) **A3's stated invariance was FALSE and the gate passed for the wrong reason.** The claim "BW(1,0)/BW(0,1) = 2 exactly, independent of L, W, alpha, c" does not hold; the exact ratio is `2*artanh(xi)/xi`, which is alpha-dependent. Measured 2.1267 at alpha=0.7 against an exact 2.0516 — inside the +/-0.20 band, but not for the reason claimed, and at higher alpha it would fail a correct solver. Additionally the two-room same-alpha framing in the 4.5x4.0 reference room gives `2W/L = 1.7778`, not 2.00. **This is the third gate in this project that would have mis-scored correct physics and the first that PASSED on a wrong target; the standing rule is to derive each gate's expected value from the governing law at the exact configuration being run and to check claimed invariances symbolically.** (d) **kappa must be re-derived per simulator but the reported comparison is not the right one.** `kappa_fdtd = 1.0208` vs the frozen `kappa_ism = 1.6608` compares slopes fitted against DIFFERENT regressors — a change of damping law, not an estimator property. The constant downstream actually consumes, `d(BW)/d(m_wall)`, measures ~1.005 on FDTD. Do not carry "a factor of 1.63" forward as a recalibration. (e) **GO-WITH-CHANGES, and FT-B/FT-C were not run.** All ten gates pass and three independent adversarial lenses agree the solver itself holds, but every gate ran L=4.5, W=4.0 — the ONLY on-grid geometry — while **39 of 40 train and 9 of 10 test geometries are not integer multiples of dx**, and `_grid_count` merely warns before snapping (L=3.68 -> 73.6 cells -> 0.5-0.8% dimension error, 10-16x the tolerance the solver was validated to). dx=0.01 divides every 2-dp dimension but costs 125x (28.8 CPU-h/1000), so the affordable direction is to snap the ROOMS and define the wave-track geometry family on the grid. Two further blockers bear on the edit axes directly: the absorber patch's acoustic extent is exactly dx wider than requested while `_apply_patch` reports the nominal span, and **both new edit parameters are quantized to dx and cannot be sampled continuously** — which collides head-on with D52's finding that continuous sampling in the linearizing coordinate is the operative variable. **Revisit**: answer the quantization question (accept a quantized axis and test whether D52 transfers, or restore continuity via sub-cell boundary interpolation) and add a physics gate per edit axis BEFORE either is trained.

---

## 2026-08-17: P3-3-FAST Track 2b — the doorway-aperture dataset, sqrt(a) conditioning, and the sealed room (D57)

**D57 — the aperture axis is conditioned on sqrt(a), the hold-out is an exact BAND in a, and the sealed room is in the dataset but out of the law.** (Numbering note: this file jumps D54 -> D57; D55/D56 are cited in the P3-3-FAST Track A code but were never written up here.) (a) **Coordinate.** FT-B swept the doorway width a in {0, 0.1, ... 4.0} at dx = 0.01 and fitted the inter-room level difference against six candidate coordinates: **sqrt(a)** is the linearizing one (pooled r^2 = **0.9870**; raw a 0.905, a^2 0.704), so `aperture_features_2d` puts an IDENTITY channel on `sqrt(a)/sqrt(4.0)` plus 3 octaves (pi, 2pi, 4pi), exactly the role m = -ln(1-alpha) plays on the absorption axis (D52). Geometry is `(L, W, x0)` at 8 octaves = 48 dims, so the arm is **55-d** with layout `[0:16] L | [16:32] W | [32:48] x0 | [48:55] aperture`. The divider position x0 is a THIRD geometry dimension, not a material one — without it the model cannot tell which sub-room a receiver is in. Registered in BOTH `cond_dim_for` and `INR2D_AutoDecoder`'s own whitelist; those are independent checks and a new arm registered in only one of them fails at model construction time with a message that blames the config. (b) **a = 0 is a TOPOLOGICAL discontinuity, not the small-aperture limit.** A sealed one-node divider disconnects room B *exactly*: H_B is identically zero and the level difference is -inf, not merely large. Its conditioning coordinate sqrt(0) = 0 is also the limit of a vanishing doorway, so the conditioning **cannot** separate the two, and training on it would force FiLM to fit a discontinuity in its own coordinate and smear every narrow aperture nearby. The 26 sealed rooms are therefore built and kept (they are the end-point demonstration, and the dataset gate uses one to prove the divider plumbing reaches the solver), flagged `sealed: true` in the manifest, given their own `kind`, and excluded from training via `config_kinds: ["open", "aperture"]`. They must be excluded from every continuous-coordinate fit. (c) **The hold-out is a band, not a geometry set.** No training config carries a in [0.9, 1.1] — draws landing inside are redrawn, so the hold-out is EXACT rather than approximate (asserted at manifest-freeze time and again in the dataset gate) — while 18 test configs (3 per test domain: 0.95, 1.00, 1.05) sit inside it. The question is then "did the model learn the aperture law?" rather than "did it memorize 20 aperture values?". The six test domains are strictly INTERIOR to the training box in L, W and x0/L, so geometry is interpolation and the aperture is the only thing under test. (d) **dx and fs are coupled and both are forced.** FT-1b A0c measured the aperture observable moving 10.4x the estimator floor between dx = 0.02 and dx = 0.01, so dx = 0.02 is inside the un-converged regime for THIS axis; and at dx = 0.01 a fixed fs = 12288 raises a CFL ValueError from the solver. Frozen: dx = 0.01, fs = 61440, n = 122880 (lambda = 0.55827, T = 2.000 s, df = 0.5 Hz), which keeps the 601-bin 0-300 Hz slice byte-compatible with every earlier 2D loader. Cost is ~200 s/room against Track A's ~9 s, i.e. the aperture axis is ~22x more expensive per room than the absorption axis. (e) **Receivers are nudged off the divider, per DOMAIN not per config.** The 8x8 grid spans the full domain (both sub-rooms) with a 0.3 m margin; any receiver whose x-node lands on or beside the divider column is pushed two nodes clear of it. Without that, `simulate` raises "snaps onto a solid node" for the sealed and narrow-aperture configs while succeeding for the wide ones — a per-config failure on a property that must be per-domain. The nudge depends only on (L, W, x0), so all 20 configs of a domain share one receiver array and are directly comparable. **Revisit if** the aperture axis is combined with per-segment absorption (the two conditioning blocks would need a shared geometry normalization, and this arm's box — L in [7,9] — is deliberately NOT the P3-2 box).

**D59 — the Track 2b eval reports held-out vs seen SEPARATELY, and the headline test is the seen-line residual, not a within-band r^2.** (Numbering note: D55/D56/D58 are cited in code but were never written up here; this entry takes the next free number.) `scripts/p3_3fast_trackB_eval.py` scores the aperture arm on four observables (inter-room level difference per ISO third-octave band and pooled; sub-room modal peak position and splitting; band-limited Schroeder decay; band-limited LSD) and every one is split into the held-out band `a in [0.9, 1.1]` and the seen apertures, never pooled into one number. Five choices are load-bearing. (a) **A regression run INSIDE the hold-out band is near-degenerate by construction and must not be read as the answer.** The band holds three aperture values (0.95 / 1.00 / 1.05), so `sqrt(a)` spans **0.050** against **1.710** for the seen set; measured on ground truth the within-band fit gives r^2 = **0.106** while the seen fit gives **0.957** and the pooled fit **0.948** — the low number is the *x*-range, not a failure. The test that does answer the question is therefore: fit `sqrt(a)` on the SEEN apertures only, then score the held-out points against that line. On ground truth the held-out RMS residual is **0.679 dB** against the seen set's **0.851 dB**, ratio **0.798** — the held-out band sits *on* the seen line, which is the GT-side validation that the harness can see the effect it claims to measure. Both fits are still reported, with `u_range` beside each so the degeneracy is visible rather than inferred. (b) **The pooled fit carries geometry scatter the aperture law does not own**, so per-domain fits are reported alongside: GT per-domain r^2 **0.9693 +/- 0.0093**, slope **7.636 +/- 0.701** (FT-B's single-domain slope was 6.808 at r^2 0.9870, and CONTEXT's one-training-domain sanity check gave 7.62 / 0.966 — all three agree). (c) **Sealed configs are excluded from every continuous fit and every aggregate and reported alone under `topological_reference`** (D57b): `band_level_ratio` returns -inf rather than a floored dB, `dynamic_range_db` drops exact zeros before taking a log, and room-B LSD is not reported for a sealed config at all because the target is identically zero. The model's *predicted* sealed level difference is reported as the size of the gap between the continuous law it learned and the topological truth — not as an error it could have avoided. (d) **The modal observable is peak MIGRATION, not FT-B's even/odd splitting.** FT-B could resolve sub-linewidth splitting because its divider sat at x0 = L/2, making the sub-rooms exact mirror images and letting it decompose the field into even/odd branches *before* peak fitting. Track 2b varies x0, the sub-rooms are not congruent, and that decomposition does not exist; a two-peak search instead needs a separation of order the linewidth (Kuttruff median **3.83 +/- 0.26 Hz** here, and FT-B measured that splitting only exceeds the linewidth for a >= 1.66 m), so `split_hz` is legitimately 0 across most of the aperture range. The well-conditioned half of the same window — the parabolically-refined peak POSITION — is reported as `migration_*_hz` / `mean_abs_peak_error_hz` and is what any modal claim should rest on. The near-square-sub-room caveat is enforced rather than merely stated: **16.3 of 26.5 modes per config** are flagged `degenerate` (nearest neighbour in either sub-room inside one Kuttruff linewidth) and excluded, leaving ~8 usable, with `frac_modes_dropped` (**0.46-0.50**) printed next to every modal number. (e) **The 8x8 grid forces an f_max ladder on the sub-room projection.** Only 3-5 receiver columns fall inside a sub-room, so `cond(Phi)` blows to ~1e16 by 200 Hz; the harness steps `f_max` down through (160, 140, 120, 100, 80, 60) Hz and keeps the first rung with `cond <= COND_MAX = 5`, recording the chosen rung and cond per sub-room. 140 Hz clears all twelve sub-rooms. **Absolute LSD must not be compared to P3-2b's ~1.0 dB**: this FDTD corpus spans **~75 dB** of in-band dynamic range against the ISM corpus's ~22 dB, so every LSD is reported beside its own `gt_dynamic_range_db` and `lsd_over_dynamic_range`. **Revisit if** the receiver grid is widened (the f_max ladder and the degeneracy count both move, and a 16x8 grid would restore FT-B's projection quality) or if a future sweep puts more than three aperture values inside the hold-out band (the within-band regression would then become informative on its own).

## 2026-08-18: Track B2 — the aperture as TOKEN GEOMETRY, and fractional m_hat for continuity (D60)

**D60 — Track B failed to REPRESENT the aperture law, not to transfer it; the fix is to tokenize the divider and express doorway width through token geometry, with partial coverage carried by fractional m_hat rather than by `extent`.** (a) **The diagnosis is representational, and the evidence is the seen/held-out symmetry.** Track B (`aperture`, 55-d) fitted the inter-room level difference against sqrt(a) at r^2 = **0.172** / slope **2.46** against ground truth's **0.948** / **7.61** — but it was *equally* wrong on both sides of the hold-out: seen-line residual **2.463 dB**, held-out **2.298 dB** (ratio **0.933**), mean held-out residual **0.019 dB**. A model that had learned the law and failed to extrapolate would show the opposite asymmetry. It never learned the law at all. The mechanism is the same one Track A hit one axis over: a GLOBAL vector carrying a SCALAR aperture is being asked to induce a SPATIAL barrier at x0 with a gap of width a, and FiLM has no way to localize that gap. Track A2/A3 fixed the analogous absorption-axis pathology by tokenizing the boundary (held-out window recovery **-0.069 -> +1.010** at convergence), so Track B2 applies the same instrument to the same class of failure. **The dataset is reused byte-for-byte** (472 configs, `data/track_p3_3fast_B/`, schema `p3_3fast.trackB/1`, no new simulation), and the yaml differs from `P3_3FAST_trackB.yaml` in exactly two lines (`cond_source`, `cond_dim`) plus `run_id`, so the comparison isolates the conditioning. (b) **The divider is tokenized into 16 segments with A2's featurization verbatim.** Each token is `[cx, cy, nx, ny, extent, m_hat]` -> position Fourier k=0..3 (16) + normal raw (2) + extent raw (1) + m_hat identity and Fourier k=0..2 (7) = **D_TOK = 26**, identical to `segment_token_features_2d`, so `INR2D_AutoDecoder.segment_encoder` is reused rather than duplicated. `cx = x0/L` for every token and `cy = (i+0.5)/16`; the face normal is `(+1, 0)`. The only structural difference from A2/A3 is the geometry prefix, which widens **32 -> 48** because it must carry `(L, W, x0)` — a model without the divider position cannot tell which sub-room a receiver is in (D57a). Stored width **464 = 48 + 16x26**, reduced to **112 = 48 + 64** by the encoder. The 48-d geometry block is asserted BYTE-IDENTICAL to Track B's first 48 dims, so the two arms differ *only* in how the doorway is expressed. The four outer walls are deliberately NOT tokenized: they are baseline in every config of this corpus, so they carry zero information and delta-pooling would zero them anyway. (c) **THE LOAD-BEARING CHOICE: partial coverage rides on fractional m_hat, not on `extent`.** `a` is drawn continuously on [0.1, 2.5], and D52's whole finding is that continuity in the linearizing coordinate is the operative variable — so a pure integer open-COUNT would quantize the axis to W/16 ~ 0.25 m steps, *coarser than the 0.2 m hold-out band itself*, and destroy the very property the track tests. We therefore set `f_i` = the fraction of segment i's y-extent covered by the centred doorway and blend `m_hat_i = m_hat(baseline) + f_i * (1 - m_hat(baseline))`, with `m_hat = 1.0` (i.e. m = 3.0, alpha = 0.9502) as "open" — the same open value Track A uses, so "open" means one thing on both axes. `extent` stays fixed at 1/16 for every token. **`extent` was rejected** because the segments must PARTITION the divider (the P3-3 Part-A lesson: segments are a partition, not k patches), and shrinking a solid segment's extent to make room for the doorway breaks that partition while leaving the divider's total measure wrong. Verified: `sum_i f_i * (W/16) == a` exactly (the tokens conserve the doorway's measure), `sum_i m_hat_i` is strictly increasing and jump-free over 201 samples of [0.1, 2.5] with 201 distinct values, and `a = 0.26` separates from `a = 0.25` despite opening the same segment count. FT-B's measured sqrt(a) coordinate is therefore **no longer supplied**: it must be recovered by the encoder from token geometry, which is the actual claim under test. (d) **DELTA pooling, and a sealed divider aggregates to exactly zero.** The pool is A3's `sum_i [phi(t_i) - phi(t_i^baseline)]`, not A2's mean (which diluted a single edit by 1/16 and dropped the discrimination ratio to 0.222). The divider's own alpha IS `ALPHA_BASELINE`, so with the doorway closed every token sits at the baseline m_hat and the aggregate is identically zero. **That is correct, not a degeneracy**: a sealed divider is the un-edited room and the doorway is the edit. It does mean the arm cannot separate `a = 0` from a vanishing doorway — the same limitation `sqrt(0) = 0` imposed on Track B (D57b) — so sealed configs remain excluded from training via `config_kinds: ["open", "aperture"]` and reported separately. One honest caveat recorded in `tests/test_aperture_tokens.py`: `inr_2d` builds `_tok_baseline_m` with python `math` in float64 while `conditioning_2d` builds the same features with `torch.sin` in float32, so the SHIPPED model's sealed aggregate is ~**1e-7**, not bitwise zero — six orders below any open-aperture aggregate, recorded rather than papered over. `inr_2d`'s construction was left untouched because Track A3 (job 7267594) was mid-flight. (e) **Registered in BOTH whitelists.** `cond_dim_for` and `INR2D_AutoDecoder`'s independent `cond_source` tuple are separate gates and an arm registered in only one of them has already broken two launches; `aperture_token` is in both, and the model's cond_dim check now dispatches on the arm (464 for B2, 448 for A2/A3) instead of assuming `TOKEN_DIM_2D`. **Revisit if** the aperture and per-segment-absorption axes are ever combined: the two token sets would need a shared geometry normalization, and this arm's box (L in [7,9]) is deliberately NOT the P3-2 box.

## 2026-08-18: FIG 5, the zero-shot edit demo — geometry pick, synthesized GT, and the three LSDs (D61)

**D61 — the headline "edit the room, zero-shot" figure (`scripts/p3_3fast_demo_figure.py`, `outputs/p3_3fast/meeting_assets/fig5_topological_edits.png`) makes five choices, all of them stated on the figure itself.** (a) **The geometry is picked by a MEDIAN rule, not a best-of.** The five panels all live on one test geometry, and which one is chosen changes every plotted number, so the rule is fixed and auditable: the test geometry whose `t_window_holdout` energy recovery is the lower median of the ten in `outputs/p3_3fast/trackA2/DIAGNOSTIC_30K.json` — geom_id 9, `L = 5.25`, `W = 3.60`, recovery **1.0095**, rank **5 of 10**. The full ranking is written into the sidecar JSON and the manifest entry. Test geometries share no `(L, W)` with the 20 training geometries, so the geometry itself is also zero-shot. Independent check that this is not a favourable pick: the geometry's per-panel raw LSD (**6.32-6.68 dB**) is *worse* than the corpus mean over all 120 test configs at the same checkpoint (**5.460 dB**). (b) **Ground truth for the alpha = 0.50 panels is SIMULATED here, into a separate directory.** The Track A test split only enumerates alpha = 0.70 and 0.95, so panels (b) and (c) — a "curtain" at alpha = 0.50 on `west_2` and on the held-out `east_3` — have no corpus GT. They are built by calling `scripts.build_p3_3fast_trackA.build_one` (the corpus builder itself: same `fdtd_2d_slf_kw` solver, `dx = 0.02`, `fs = 30720`, `n = 61440`, same receiver snapping) into **`data/track_p3_3fast_A_demo/`**, never into the training corpus, so the manifest `rows_sha256` and every dataset gate are untouched. ~20 s per config, idempotent via `.done`. alpha = 0.50 is interior to the training alpha distribution (71 of 940 edited training alphas fall in [0.45, 0.55]) and `east_3` is edited in **0** of 400 training configs. (c) **Panel (e) is a SEPARATE forward pass, not a copy of panel (a), and the script asserts bit-for-bit equality.** That is the standing check that `renderer.eval()` took (D49 C3: `FreqRenderer2D` jitters ray azimuths while `self.training`). It passes. (d) **Three LSDs are reported, because the -40 dB floored LSD does not measure what its own docstring claims.** `scripts/p3_3fast_floored_lsd.py` sets the floor relative to each config's own peak — but in this FDTD corpus that peak is the **bin-0 (0,0) compliance term**, which sits **~46 dB above the strongest room mode**. A -40 dB floor therefore keeps only **bins 0-12 (0-6 Hz)** and measures the near-DC term, *not* the modal content it was introduced to isolate. The figure quotes raw (all 601 bins), a newly defined **modal-peak LSD** (all 64 receivers at the 27 local maxima of the baseline receiver-RMS spectrum), and the floored value, in that order, with the caveat printed. On this geometry: **6.32-6.68 / 7.00-7.28 / 1.48-1.71 dB**. The floor remains a REPORTING choice and was never used to select anything. Corpus-wide at iter 30000 over all 120 test configs: **5.460 raw / 0.703 floored** (the previously circulated 4.956 / 0.613 came from `--limit 24`, i.e. test geometries 0-1 only, at iter 28000). (e) **The plotted mode is restricted to what the 8 x 8 receiver grid can resolve.** The literal rule "argmax |GT delta vs baseline| over baseline modal peaks" is kept, but the candidate set is (i) referenced to the strongest LOCAL MAXIMUM rather than to `s.max()` — otherwise the DC term makes the candidate set empty — and (ii) restricted to modes with `n_x <= floor(L/2 dx_rx) = 3` and `n_y <= floor(W/2 dy_rx) = 4`, since a bottom-row map of an unresolvable mode is aliasing, not physics. This changes only panel (b), whose unrestricted argmax (294.0 Hz, -1.82 dB) is a **0.01 dB** tie with the plotted (1,0) at 32.5 Hz (-1.81 dB); both are recorded per panel. All five panels land on (1,0) at 32.5 Hz. **Revisit if** the receiver grid is densified (the resolvability bound moves and the maps stop being 8 x 8 blocks) or if the corpus is ever re-simulated with a source that suppresses the (0,0) term (the floored-LSD definition would then mean what it says).

## 2026-08-18: Arm C demo pack — the abort rule made real, and the two metrics kept apart (D62)

**D62 — the Arm C demo (`scripts/armC_demo_metrics.py` + `scripts/armC_demo_figures.py`, `outputs/armC_demo/`) is split into a metrics stage and a figure stage so its pre-registered abort rule cannot be quietly bypassed, and it reports spatial and magnitude agreement side by side because only one of them is flattering.** (a) **Two stages, and the ordering IS the mechanism.** The spec fixed a kill switch — if pointwise spatial Pearson came in below ~0.70, report it rather than illustrate it — and a single script that computes and plots in one pass makes that rule decorative, because the figures exist before anyone reads the number. Stage 1 renders all 12 scenarios, computes the full table, writes `metrics.json` with `proceed_to_figures`, and **exits non-zero** if the rule trips; stage 2 refuses to draw unless that flag is true. The risk was live, not ceremonial: the FDTD-corpus model scores **0.24-0.60** on this exact metric. Measured here: **worst 0.920, mean 0.951** over 12 scenarios (3 frozen test geometries x 4 scenarios), so the figures were drawn. (b) **THE LOAD-BEARING REPORTING CHOICE: spatial Pearson and magnitude correlation are both published, on the same axes.** Spatial Pearson is evaluated at modal bins and ranges 0.920-0.976; magnitude correlation pools all 601 bins including deep nulls and ranges **0.607-0.761** (mean 0.694). Quoting only the first would be the third time this chunk a null-dominated or DC-dominated metric was mistaken for a physics claim (cf. the retracted floored-LSD result, D61d, and the DC/compliance re-scoring). `figC` therefore plots them as paired bars and the manifest labels the second "ALL bins, null-dominated" rather than burying it. (c) **`mode_shape_invariance` 0.9921 is NOT this measurement and is never merged with it.** P3-2b recorded that for Arm C as agreement with the *analytic cosine shape* on the *8x8 training grid*; the numbers here are pointwise pred-vs-GT on a **64x64** grid at 0.15 m margin (training used 8x8 at 0.30 m). The two are printed as separate quantities in `metrics.json` (`prior_note`), on `figC`, and in the manifest. (d) **Scenario (c) is doubly zero-shot and this was not in the spec.** `north@0.70` maps to m = 1.204, inside Arm C's held-out slab `north (1.13, 1.28)` — that wall/material combination appears in **0** training configs, so (c) tests unseen geometry AND unseen material placement. It scored **highest in all three geometries** (0.976 / 0.973 / 0.974). Scenario (d) (east 0.50 + south 0.70) is *not* one of P3-2b's four frozen two-wall test combos, which is harmless because every GT here is re-simulated at 64x64 rather than read from the corpus — recorded so nobody looks for a stored file. (e) **Ground truth is re-simulated on the dense grid under the corpus protocol** (fs 4096, n 8192, max_order 60, source (0.5, 0.5)) so it is the same physics the model trained against; its truncation warning is the frozen protocol's own behaviour, not a new defect. `git status` confirms the frozen corpus and its manifests are untouched. The 12 cached `.npz` dumps (440 MB) are gitignored; figures, `metrics.json` and `figures.json` are pushed, so every plotted number is traceable without the bulk data. **One negative printed rather than cropped**: agreement is close to ~170 Hz and degrades above it, tracking the envelope but misplacing individual nulls — visible in `figB` and already folded into the quoted LSDs (centre 1.23-2.81 dB). **Revisit if** the demo is ever re-run on the FDTD corpus, where the DC/compliance term (87.3% of in-band power) makes both the LSD and the magnitude correlation mean something different (D61d, Q18).

## 2026-08-18: Arm C v2 — the modal hierarchy, the Delta test, and an estimator caught mid-flight (D62b)

**D62b — v2 replaces v1's single-fundamental figure with a screen over the whole modal hierarchy, adds the Delta test that actually probes editability, and records two of my own errors rather than silently fixing them.** (Numbering: this extends D62 rather than opening a new number, because it is the same demo pack on the same checkpoint.) (a) **Items 1-3 cost NO new compute.** v1's `.npz` dumps cached the full `(4096, 601)` complex spectra to 300 Hz, so the 24-mode screen, the multi-mode Fig A and the difference maps are pure CPU work on already-paid-for data; only the doorway needed simulation. One implementation trap worth recording: `NpzFile.__getitem__` decompresses on EVERY access, and the Delta table indexes `pred`/`gt` a few thousand times, which turned a 20 s job into a multi-minute timeout until the arrays were materialised once. (b) **The abort rule did not trip, and the screen produced a finding the spec did not anticipate.** 6 of the 21 modes above 60 Hz clear 0.85 (the rule required zero), highest at 198.9 Hz; 23/24 modes clear 0.70 with mean +0.822. But accuracy is **NOT monotone in frequency** -- it falls to +0.655 at (3,2) 138.4 Hz and *recovers* to +0.891 at (4,3) 198.9 Hz -- so any single "quality above X Hz" number misdescribes the result. Both dB and linear Pearson are reported at every mode because they disagree sharply (linear mean +0.681, only 9/24 above 0.70, declining monotonically 0.885 -> 0.590); the dB form rewards the nodal pattern, the linear form weights loud regions. The **large** test geometry is materially worse than the median (mean +0.735, 4/35 modes >= 0.85, 22/35 non-isolated) and is reported rather than omitted -- a slide number taken from the median geometry alone would not be representative. (c) **MY PLANNING ESTIMATE OF MODE ISOLATION WAS WRONG and the correction is load-bearing.** A 2-D Sabine calculation gave a 1.98 Hz linewidth and implied every candidate mode was isolated. The repo's Kuttruff linewidth (`modal_damping_2d(..., model="kuttruff")`) is **2.67-3.96 Hz**, so **11 of 24 modes have a neighbour inside their linewidth** -- those fields are genuine superpositions and their mode labels are nominal. This includes **(1,2) at 111.67 Hz**, the mid row of the new Fig A, which overlaps (0,2) at 107.86 Hz. Flagged per mode in `mode_screen.json`, drawn hollow on figD, and printed on figA2 itself. Resolvability is NOT a concern at 64x64 (`n_x <= 33, n_y <= 34` against a max of (6,3)), unlike the 8x8 grid case in D61e. (d) **THE HEADLINE RESULT: Delta correlation is materially below raw-field correlation at every mode, and the decay is NOT universal.** Subtracting the baseline cancels the shared room structure that raw correlation partly rewards, so `Delta = edited - baseline` is the stricter test of an *editable* representation. Over 24 modes (linear): east curtain +0.586, north absorber +0.614, two-wall +0.835, against raw-field 0.78-0.99. My first draft of figE was titled "Delta agreement decays as frequency rises, under BOTH definitions" -- **that was wrong and was corrected before publication**. Both SINGLE-wall edits fall from the lowest six modes to the highest six (+0.871 -> +0.315, +0.822 -> +0.391) while the larger TWO-wall edit holds essentially flat (+0.885 -> +0.876). **Delta recovery tracks EDIT MAGNITUDE as well as frequency**, so any editing claim must be scoped to edit size rather than stated flatly. (e) **The Delta metric leads with LINEAR, and the reason is recorded because the ordering is also the more favourable one.** Maps and the leading number are linear, normalised by baseline RMS |H|; the dB value is printed on every panel and in every table. Justification: dB differences diverge near pressure nulls, where the field is negligible and the quantity numerically unstable -- that artifact alone moves east curtain at 107.9 Hz from **+0.521 linear to +0.010 dB**. The choice was made on that argument, NOT on which number is larger, and the manifest states this explicitly along with the fact that it is the more flattering order. v1's raw-field Pearson stays dB-based, so Delta-linear and raw-dB are never compared directly. **Revisit if** a future chunk needs one headline spatial number: it must name a geometry, a frequency range and a metric definition, because all three move the answer by more than the differences anyone would want to claim.

**D62c — the doorway motivator is ground truth only, and an estimator mismatch against FT-B was attributed rather than assumed benign.** (a) **No spatial field existed on disk.** FT-B stored 192 receivers per run, Track B 8x8, and `aaf.sim.fdtd_2d.simulate` has **no whole-grid output** -- the field buffers are cyclically overwritten and only `np.take` at receiver nodes is recorded. A dense RECEIVER array is the supported route, and the binding constraint is MEMORY not time: `ir_t`, `ir`, `H_complex` and `H_deconv` are all allocated at `n_rx x n` and `H_deconv` has no skip flag. Frozen for the figure: 8192 receivers (128x64), `n = 30720` (T = 0.5 s, df = 2.0 Hz), measured peak **15.9 GB** and ~123 s per aperture. Shrink `n`, never `n_rx`. Receivers landing on the divider column must be nudged clear (reusing `build_p3_3fast_trackB.receivers`'s rule) or `_snap_nodes` raises for the sealed and mid cases while succeeding for the open one -- a per-aperture failure on a property that must be per-domain. (b) **Domain and mode are FT-B's, deliberately.** 8.0 x 4.0, divider at x0 = 4.0, alpha = 0.15 everywhere, src (0.5, 0.5), dx = 0.01, fs = 61440, and FT-B's own `PRIMARY_MODE` sub-room (1,1) at 60.71 Hz -- whose even branch ends on full-domain (2,1) at 60.63 Hz, so sealed shows two independent sub-room fields and open shows one field spanning the domain. Reusing a validated configuration is what makes a discrepancy interpretable. (c) **THE DISCREPANCY, and the wrong first explanation.** Dense-pass level differences were -5.27 dB (a=1.0) and -0.29 dB (a=4.0) against FT-B's published **-7.15** and **-1.45**. The cause is that FT-B's `band_level_ratio` (`scripts/p3_3fast_ftb.py:232`) is `20 log10(mean|H|)` -- a mean of **AMPLITUDE** -- while the dense pass used `10 log10(mean|H|^2)`, a mean of **POWER**. Switching estimators moves a=4.0 to -1.14 dB, i.e. the estimator explains most of that gap; a residual at a=1.0 is attributable to the receiver set (8192 dense at 0.20 m margin vs 16x8 at 0.30 m) and the record length (0.5 s vs 2.0 s). Both estimators are now computed and stored for every run, and `m_band` (mean |H| per receiver) is cached so either can be re-derived without re-simulating. A **second pass replays FT-B's exact protocol** (16x8 grid, 0.3 m margin, n = 122880) as a reproduction check rather than leaving the offset unexplained; the sealed case already reproduces exactly (-inf, room B identically zero, matching `DATASET_GATE.json`). **The standing lesson**: a level-difference number is not comparable across chunks unless the estimator, receiver set and record length all match -- three degrees of freedom that between them moved this observable by ~1.9 dB. **Revisit if** any future aperture claim compares against FT-B's published table; it must state which of the three it holds fixed.

## 2026-09-10: Repo sync — the untracked backlog, the per_config policy, and 10.75 GB of loose-object garbage (D63)

**D63 — five chunks of run telemetry and eval output had accumulated untracked, one published figure rested on a file the manager could not fetch, and `.git` was 98% garbage. All three fixed; the cause of the first is structural and now has a check.** (a) **Nothing was ignored — it was never `git add`ed.** 37 untracked files plus 2 modified tracked ones had built up across P3-2 -> P3-2b -> P3-2c -> P3-2d -> P3-3-FAST. `git check-ignore -v` returned either "not ignored" or a **negation** line (`.gitignore:148 !/outputs/p3_2/**/*.json`), which *un*-ignores — so for the p3_2 files the whitelist showed they were always intended to be tracked. The repo was never out of sync commit-wise (`main...origin/main`, no divergence); the backlog was purely uncommitted local state, and it is invisible in a casual `git status` because untracked directories collapse to one line each. (b) **Two entries were load-bearing, not noise.** `scripts/slurm/armC_demo_metrics.sh` was never committed, so the Arm C demo pack's metrics stage was **not reproducible from a clean clone** even though D62 and `outputs/armC_demo/FIGURE_MANIFEST.md:121` both point at it; worse, its header was a copy-paste of `p3_3fast_trackA_diag.sh` documenting an entirely different script and creating `outputs/p3_3fast/trackA` instead of `outputs/armC_demo` (the SBATCH block and command line were correct and were left untouched). And `outputs/p3_2d/eval_seed2/{G020,G030}/summary.json` is cited by a **published** manifest (`outputs/p3_3fast/meeting_assets/FIGURE_MANIFEST.md:285-286`) and read by `scripts/p3_3fast_figures.py:608` — a figure already shown to the manager rested on a file the manager could not fetch. Also incoherent: `p3_3fast/p3_3fast_trackA/scalars.json` was tracked while `trackA2/A3/B/B2` were not, so the convergence record existed for one arm and was absent for four. (c) **THE POLICY CALL: seed-2 `per_config.json` is ignored going forward; the 10 already-tracked ones stay.** D44's rule of thumb ("never commit per-room dumps — regenerable") had been applied to `p3_2` and `p3_2b` but not to `p3_2c`/`p3_2d`, where 10 `per_config.json` totalling ~36 MB are tracked at 2.2-7.1 MB each. The new `eval_seed2` dumps (8.4 MB of an 11 MB backlog) are therefore **gitignored** — `summary.json` + `verdict.json` are the tracked headline and are what the published manifest actually cites. The existing 10 are **deliberately left tracked**: that 36 MB is permanently in git history, so `git rm --cached` would reclaim exactly nothing while adding churn and breaking anything that reads them. Stopping the bleed is worth doing; retroactive purity is not. (d) **`.git` was 11 GB against 0.31 GiB of real content.** 5530 objects totalling 17.42 GiB, of which **17.11 GiB was unreachable** — 30 blobs >100 MB (14.99 GiB) plus 131 blobs 10-100 MB, all accidental `git add`s of checkpoints that were never committed (a `git add` writes the blob even if you never commit, and it survives). Verified safe before pruning: no stashes, no dangling commits (`git fsck --no-reflogs --connectivity-only`), reflog intact to the initial commit, index clean, HEAD pushed, and every blob >50 MB confirmed unreachable from `git rev-list --objects --all`. `git gc --prune=now` took 23 s and took **.git from 11 GB to 251 MB** (~10.75 GB reclaimed); 129 commits and tip hash unchanged, `git fsck` clean, old blobs still readable from history. This touches neither history nor the remote. (e) **Prevention, because the cause is structural.** Chunk-end commits reliably cover reports and figures and reliably miss run output. `scripts/repo_hygiene.sh` now reports remote sync, uncommitted tracked changes, untracked-but-not-ignored paths **with sizes**, and loose-object bloat, exiting non-zero if any need attention — one command at chunk end. **Standing rule: run it before declaring a chunk done.** **Revisit if** a future chunk's telemetry grows beyond ~1 MB per run, at which point `scalars.json` should be downsampled rather than excluded — the convergence record is exactly what the manager needs and cannot regenerate.

## 2026-09-11: P4-1 — the world-scale convention, token-only geometry, and the L-room (D64)

**D64 — positions everywhere are absolute metres over a FIXED world scale, boundary tokens carry all the geometry, and both gates pass.** (Numbering note: the chunk spec said to log the coordinate convention as D63, but D63 was already taken by the 2026-09-10 repo-sync entry; this chunk writes D64-D65.) (a) **THE SPEC'S PREMISE WAS HALF WRONG, and the half it got wrong is the half that mattered.** The spec guards against bounding-box normalization of positions. Positions already complied: the only transform was `_normalize_unit(x) = (x+1)/2` on raw metres (`inr_2d.py:490`), which is room-INDEPENDENT, and source and receiver went through the identical function. **The actual violation was in the TOKENS** — `segment_geometry` (`conditioning_2d.py:387`) returns unit-square coordinates (`cx` in {0,1}, `cy` in {0.125,...}), hard-codes `extent = 0.25` for every segment in every room, and **ignores its own `L, W` arguments entirely**, so two rooms of different size produced byte-identical token positions. A token arm therefore could not have carried geometry no matter how it was trained. (b) **`WORLD_SCALE = 10.0`** (`aaf/walls.py`), applied to tokens, source, receivers and ray sample points alike, via `normalize_position(x, world_scale)`. The map lives in `walls` rather than in the model **because `aaf.models.inr_2d` imports tinycudann and needs a GPU** — the spec's required "source and receiver use identical scaling" test has to run in the ordinary CPU pass, not only where a GPU happens to be attached. `world_scale=None` reproduces the legacy `(x+1)/2` exactly, so the switch is opt-in per checkpoint. 10 m contains the P3-2 family, Track B's 9 m box and the planned Z-corridor, so every coordinate lands in [0, 1] — which is also tinycudann's documented HashGrid domain, fixing an undocumented deviation where inputs had been occupying [0.5, 3.5]. (c) **`base_resolution` 16 -> 80 MOVES WITH the world scale, and that coupling is load-bearing.** At world_scale 10 a 6 m room spans 0.6 hash units instead of the legacy 3.0 — 5x fewer cells across the room. Without the compensation (16 * 1.5^4 = 80 restores metres-per-cell) a Gate 0 failure could not have been attributed to the tokens rather than to lost resolution, which is the entire question Stage 0 exists to answer. **Verified the change cannot disturb any existing checkpoint**: Arm C re-renders **BIT-IDENTICALLY**, 12 of 12 cached scenarios, worst |delta| = 0.000e+00. (d) **The token arm `geom_token` pads to `MAX_SEG_POLY = 12` slots with an explicit validity MASK** (stored 324 = 12x26 + 12, reduced to 64), because Stage 0 needs 4 tokens and Stage 1 needs 6 **within one chunk** and a fixed-count encoder would have needed rewriting mid-chunk. Padding is masked out of the pool rather than merely multiplied by zero: a zero token is a perfectly valid point in token space (an edge at the origin, zero extent, baseline absorption) and an unmasked mean would drag the aggregate toward that fiction by n_pad/12. `D_TOK = 26` and the `segment_encoder` architecture are reused verbatim from A2/A3. **No global geometry prefix at all** (`_tok_geom = 0`) — a Z-corridor has no meaningful (L, W). (e) **GATE 0 PASSES, and token-only geometry is STRICTLY BETTER than the arm that is handed (L, W).** Identical corpus, manifest sha, recipe, optimizer and 60000-iteration budget; the only differences are `cond_source` and the world_scale/base_resolution pair. Demo protocol, 12 scenarios: spatial Pearson mean **0.982 vs 0.951**, worst **0.969 vs 0.920**; band LSD mean **1.722 vs 2.268 dB**. In-distribution val LSD **0.9914 vs 1.0132**. T-geo wins on all 12 scenarios on both gated metrics. **Trajectory warning worth recording**: T-geo started BEHIND (+0.18 dB at iter 8000), drew level around 22000 and finished ahead — an early-checkpoint comparison would have concluded the opposite. (f) **ONE HONEST COUNTER-RESULT: the edit-response SLOPE is worse.** On the frozen P3-2b splits (thresholds unchanged, `thr a8479c5e1dcc`) T-geo passes S2 — slope 0.871, pearson 0.900, gain 1.285 — but `edit_bw_slope` is **lower than Arm C in every split**: S1 0.784 vs 0.997, S2 0.871 vs 0.959, S4 **0.464 vs 0.789**, S5 0.913 vs 1.010, while `edit_gain` is HIGHER everywhere (it overshoots edit magnitude). So the arm reconstructs the FIELD better (LSD lower in every unseen-geometry split: S1 2.315/2.979, S2 2.049/2.669, S4 2.365/3.000, S5 1.947/2.632) while responding to an EDIT less linearly. S4 (the unseen alpha = 0.30 continuity split) is the sharpest drop and sits well below 0.80, though S4 is not a gated split. **Revisit**: if Stage 2 needs edit linearity as well as reconstruction, the token m_hat channel is the first place to look — it is the only part of the token that changed representation without changing its Fourier treatment.

**D65 — `wall_segments` and `patch` cannot describe a non-convex room and FAIL SILENTLY; the L-room is cut with `mask`, which had zero test coverage until now.** (a) **The spec directed Stage 1 to use "the wall_segments partition primitive". That is wrong against this code.** `_apply_wall_segments` (`fdtd_2d.py:482-536`) and `_apply_patch` are hard-coded to the four outer edges of the bounding RECTANGLE (`wall` must be a key of `WALL_DIR`), index the FULL node count of the segmented axis, and **never consult `solid`**. Executed on the notched 6x5 room, `{"type":"wall_segments","wall":"east","alphas":[0.9]*4}` **raises nothing**, reports `tiles_exactly: True` and extents summing to 5.0 m for an east wall that physically exists for only 2.5 m, and writes half the requested absorption onto inert solid nodes where `B = 0`. That `tiles_exactly: True` is exactly what the Track A builder stores into the HDF5 attrs, so the lie would have been recorded as a passing check. (b) **The L-room is expressible today with ZERO solver changes** via the `mask` escape hatch, and it is numerically sound — verified by execution: spectral radius `1.000000000000`, a bounded full 2.0 s run, correct Kowalczyk-van Walstijn admittance on the notch faces, and a plain SLF update at the reflex corner (all four stencil neighbours fluid, no blocked face, which is the correct staircase treatment). The known `mu` self-adjointness residual (0.207) at wall tips is **PRE-EXISTING and identical in the already-shipped Track B doorway corpus**, so `record_energy` and exact reciprocity are diagnostics here, not gates. (c) **The node indices are the whole game and are not left to inspection.** The removed block is the TOP-LEFT corner, so the solid slice is `m[:i3, j25+1:]`: node `i3` (x = 3.0) and node `j25` (y = 2.5) must stay AIR because they ARE the notch's two wall faces (the reflecting plane sits one dx outside the solid block, `fdtd_2d.py:427-430`). Off by one and the walls land at 2.98/2.52 m; wrong CORNER and you simulate a different room that still looks entirely plausible. `build_p4_1_lroom.build` reconstructs the room analytically from its vertex list and asserts node-for-node against `geom.air`; **that assert fired on the first run** (37500 nodes disagreeing = exactly twice the block, the signature of a mask in the wrong corner) and caught a real error. (d) **`mask` was the only `extra_walls` spec type with no test at all**, and P4-1 makes it load-bearing, so it now has coverage: notch node indices, face admittance vs a rigid twin, reflex-corner treatment, stability over a short run, and `_snap_nodes` refusing a receiver inside the notch. (e) **The dataset gate follows the Track B pattern where one item tests the SIMULATOR rather than the bookkeeping.** A silently-dropped `extra_walls` would pass every bookkeeping item; the rigid-vs-absorbing notch check (1.513 dB apart, not byte-identical) would not. 11 of 11 pass. **Revisit**: `_apply_wall_segments`/`_apply_patch` should raise when any target node is solid — a 3-line change that turns the silent lie into an error, deferred here only because Stage 1 uses uniform alpha and never needed per-segment absorption on a polygon.

**D66 — STANDING FACT about the renderer, recorded because three separate analyses have now had to re-derive it: `FreqRenderer2D` fans rays OUTWARD FROM THE RECEIVER and never traces a source->receiver ray.** (a) **The mechanics.** `FreqRenderer2D` fans 64 azimuthal rays from each receiver out to the room AABB, samples `n_pts_per_ray` points along each, and accumulates transmittance `exp(-∫sigma ds)` along the **receiver -> point** leg only. The source enters as a network INPUT (`tx` is a per-point feature of `signal(pts, tx, .)`), not as a second endpoint of any traced path. There is no source-side leg, no shadow ray, and no occlusion test between the source and a sample point. (b) **Why it is worth a standing entry.** It is the single structural fact that determines what `sigma` CAN and CANNOT represent, and it is not visible from any config or metric. A wall between the RECEIVER and a sample point is expressible in sigma. A wall between the SOURCE and that point **has no structural representation at all** and can only be absorbed into `signal`, i.e. memorized per room. Every occlusion result in P4-1 and P4-2 turns on this, and it is why the sigma probe (`aaf/eval/sigma_probe.py`) fires from an NLOS receiver **toward** the source: that is the one direction in which sigma is structurally ABLE to place the occluder, so a flat sigma there is evidence about the representation and not about ray coverage. (c) **What it does NOT license.** It does not mean LOS/NLOS is the wrong evaluation split — that split is a property of the GEOMETRY (does the straight source->receiver segment stay inside the polygon?) and it correctly separates receivers the source reaches directly from those it can reach only by diffracting around a reflex corner. It means the split measures what the architecture has to memorize, which is precisely the interesting quantity. (d) **Consequence carried forward**: any future claim that the model "learned the occluder" must be supported by the sigma probe, not by NLOS accuracy. P4-1 Gate 1 reached NLOS Pearson +0.974 with a sigma ratio of 1.075 and a transmittance ratio of 0.83 — capacity without mechanism — and P4-2 now shows the multi-shape version of the same thing at ratio ~1.00.

**D67 — the P4-2 shape family is a one-corner rectangular notch, and all four of its parameters are QUANTIZED TO THE FDTD GRID because otherwise the conditioning and the physics describe different rooms.** (a) **The family.** Base rectangle `L in [5.0, 7.0]`, `W in [4.0, 5.5]`; a rectangular notch of depth `d` and width `w` is removed from the TOP-LEFT corner (P4-1's convention), `d in [0, 0.45W]`, `w in [0, 0.45L]`, both zero or both non-zero. Vertices CCW and ORIGIN-ANCHORED: `(0,0) (L,0) (L,W) (w,W) (w,W-d) (0,W-d)` — six edges, or four when there is no notch. Origin-anchoring is not cosmetic: the trainer hard-codes `room_min = zeros(2)`. (b) **`d_hat = d/(0.45W)`, NOT `d/W`, and the chunk would have had no held-out slab under the other reading.** The spec sets the hold-out band at `d_hat in [0.45, 0.60]` while sampling `d in [0, 0.45W]`. Under `d_hat = d/W` the band lies ENTIRELY OUTSIDE the sampled range and the slab is empty — the hold-out would have silently excluded nothing and the chunk would have reported generalization it never tested. Under `d_hat = d/d_max` the band is interior and 0.15 wide. Frozen as a manifest constant, following the `A_NORM` precedent. (c) **THE GRID-ALIGNMENT REQUIREMENT, which is the reason this entry exists.** `_fit_axis` chooses `dx` PER AXIS so that `L` and `W` divide exactly — for `L = 5.05` it returns `dxx = 0.020040`, not 0.02. Independently-drawn 2-dp values of `d` and `w` therefore do NOT land on nodes: measured displacement of the notch face was **8.4 mm in x**. The model's token would carry `extent = w/WORLD_SCALE` for a wall the solver put 8 mm elsewhere — **the conditioning and the ground truth would have described different rooms, silently**. This is the same class of bug as D64(a) and D65(a), and again only an assert caught it: the builder's node-for-node cross-check failed on **all 75 rooms** with "solver air mask disagrees with the polygon at 78 nodes". **Fix**: quantize `L, W, d, w` to multiples of `GRID_DX = 0.02`, which makes `L/0.02` and `W/0.02` integers so `dxx = dxy = 0.02` exactly; worst face displacement drops to **8.88e-16 m**. Cost is a coarser parameter grid (0.02 m steps over 2.0 m and 1.5 m ranges, still ~100 and ~75 distinct values), far finer than the sampling-gap target needs. Manifest re-frozen `caccd70943c9 -> 50e3bb164fff`. **9 of the 75 rooms had built cleanly before the fix purely because they were coincidentally aligned** — a partial pass is the worst possible signal here and is exactly what a weaker assert would have produced. (d) **Sampling target 0.125 of each normalized range, not the spec's 0.2, and the citation it rests on is corrected rather than repeated.** The spec cites P3-2d's sampling law ("interval <= 0.2 of normalized range"). Verified against the artifacts: `delta_star.point_estimate` is **null** (unratified), it rests on a `rho` definition P3-2d **explicitly escalated rather than decided** ("I am NOT choosing."), and **it does not replicate at a second seed** — G030 reads rho 1.3083 FAIL at seed 1 and 1.1753 PASS at seed 2, and G030 was the sole evidence for Delta* ~ 0.275. The absorption axis spans 1.59 in `m`, so `0.2 x 1.59 = 0.318` lands **exactly on the G030 arm whose verdict flips**. The arm that passes under both rho definitions at both seeds is G020 = **0.125**, which is what this chunk uses. Separately, P3-2d measured ONE axis (absorption) in its own linearizing coordinate; nothing establishes transfer to geometry parameters, which have no established linearizing coordinate, so the figure is used as a conservative target rather than as a law. (e) **Derived filenames and UNIQUE labels.** `PolyConfig.filename` is a plain stored string and `PolyConfig.label` is `"L[6v]"` for EVERY six-edge room — the first lets two shapes alias onto one `.h5` (the trainer would then train one room against another's field with no error) and the second makes `train_meta.json`'s `config_labels` non-identifying. Both are now DERIVED from `(L, W, d, w)` and uniqueness is asserted at manifest-freeze time. (f) **Exactly 800 receivers per room, and the limitation that buys.** The trainer stacks targets with `np.stack(H_list)`, a HARD error on a ragged corpus. Room areas span 2.41x, so a fixed COUNT means receiver DENSITY varies by that factor across the family. Recorded as a stated limitation rather than hidden; evaluation builds its own denser per-room grid and is not bound by the trainer's stacking. (g) **The 0.8 m minimum-passage rule is VACUOUS at these ranges** — minimum passage is `0.55 x W_min = 2.20 m` — implemented anyway and reported as never firing.

**D68 — Q20 RESOLVED: the entanglement hypothesis was right. `m_hat` leaves the token, material returns on its own channel, and `geom_token_m` (T-geo+m) is adopted as the conditioning for all downstream work.** (a) **The test.** Q20 option (c), exactly as written: a token arm carrying GEOMETRY ONLY — `[cx, cy, nx, ny, extent]`, `D_TOK_GEO = 19` — with material re-entering on the dedicated per-wall channel that Arm C proved (`m_hat` identity + Fourier k=0..2, 28 dims, **verified byte-identical to `m_linear_features_2d[32:60]`**), concatenated with the pooled token embedding before FiLM. Stored 268, reduced 92. Identical corpus, manifest sha, recipe, optimizer, 60000-iteration budget, `world_scale 10.0`, `base_resolution 80`; the ONLY change from T-geo is where `m_hat` lives. (b) **It works, on the gated split, essentially completely.** On the frozen P3-2b splits (thresholds unchanged, `thr a8479c5e1dcc`), `edit_bw_slope` on S2 recovers **0.871 -> 0.957** against Arm C's 0.959 — 98% of the gap closed, a 0.002 difference. S5 recovers 0.913 -> 0.997 against 1.010. Full split table: S1 0.850/0.784/0.997, S2 **0.957**/0.871/0.959, S3 0.742/0.720/0.720, S4 0.491/0.464/0.789, S5 0.997/0.913/1.010 (T-geo+m / T-geo / Arm C). S2 PASSES all four gate criteria: slope 0.957, pearson 0.871, gain 1.274, `|rho - 1|` **0.009** (the best of the three arms; T-geo 0.054, Arm C 0.053). (c) **And it costs NOTHING in reconstruction — it is the best arm on that too.** In-distribution val LSD **0.9165** vs T-geo 0.9914 vs Arm C 1.0132, best-checkpoint 0.9052, ahead of both at every matched iteration from 20000 on. Demo protocol spatial Pearson mean **0.982**, worst 0.964 — matching T-geo and clearing the >= 0.97 adoption bar. Band LSD beats Arm C in every unseen-geometry split (S1 2.45/2.98, S2 2.18/2.67, S4 2.50/3.00, S5 2.08/2.63). The spec's success condition was "T-geo's reconstruction with Arm C's edit slope"; that is what this is. (d) **ONE HONEST CAVEAT that is not optional to report: S4 does not recover.** The unseen-alpha-0.30 material-continuity split reads 0.491 against Arm C's 0.789 — a 0.027 improvement on T-geo's 0.464, i.e. ~9% of the gap. So disentangling `m_hat` from the token recovers edit linearity where the edit is a MODERATE absorption change, and does not recover it where the edit pushes absorption to the edge of the sampled range. The token encoder is therefore not the whole story for S4, and whatever else is going on there is unaffected by this change. S4 is not a gated split, so nothing is blocked, but the "entanglement explains the slope loss" claim is established for S2/S5 and NOT for S4. (e) **`token_pool` is now a plumbed config key and is in the resume guard** (`cond_source, cond_dim, n_pts_per_ray, n_azi, n_iters, world_scale, token_pool, base_resolution`), so two arms differing only in pooling cannot cross-load a checkpoint. The extent index lives in a named constant `TOK_EXTENT_IDX = 18` in both `conditioning_2d` and `inr_2d` so the layout cannot drift between the producer and the consumer.

**D69 — extent-weighted pooling is WORSE than masked mean, consistently, and the chunk spec's stated rationale for it is empirically refuted.** (a) **The hypothesis under test.** The spec proposed `sum_i extent_i * phi(token_i)` on the reasoning that a longer boundary should have a larger effect on the field, and asked for it as a two-arm comparison against masked mean. It was implemented RAW (unnormalized) exactly as specified — normalizing would have destroyed the very "longer boundary, larger effect" property that is its rationale — and verified by unit test to reduce to masked mean when all extents are equal. (b) **It loses in both loss arms, by a wide margin.** In-slab spatial Pearson: DC-masked **0.798 (mean) vs 0.576 (extent)**, a gap of 0.222; unmasked control **0.748 vs 0.568**, a gap of 0.180. Out-of-slab, band LSD, phase and RIR agree in direction. Two arms, two independent loss configurations, same ordering — this is not a seed artifact. (c) **The likely mechanism, stated as a hypothesis and not as a finding.** The raw sum's magnitude scales with the room's total boundary length, so the pooled embedding's norm varies with room SIZE as well as with room SHAPE, and the FiLM layer downstream sees a conditioning vector whose scale is confounded with the thing it is supposed to distinguish. Masked mean is scale-free in that sense. A normalized extent weighting was NOT tested and is the obvious next variant if anyone wants to rescue the idea. (d) **Standing consequence**: `masked_mean` is the default and the only pooling used downstream. `extent_sum` stays in the codebase behind the `token_pool` key because the negative result is worth being able to reproduce.

**D70 — DC-masking the loss buys a small amount of modal accuracy and DESTROYS the impulse response; it is not the primary loss.** (a) **What was compared.** Two loss arms per pooling: primary = DC-masked (`loss_band_lo_hz = 20.0`, i.e. bins below 20 Hz excluded), control = unmasked (`0.0`). The mask exists because Q18 established that the reported "floored LSD" was dominated by the near-DC term rather than by modal content. (b) **The trade, measured.** With masked mean: DC-masked reaches in-slab spatial Pearson **0.798 vs 0.748** unmasked (+0.050) and out-of-slab 0.702 vs 0.737 (-0.035), while band LSD is WORSE (5.76 vs 4.84 dB) and **RIR Pearson collapses from 0.9989 to 0.0630**. The extent arms show the identical pattern (RIR 0.0704 vs 0.9967). (c) **Why the RIR collapse is disqualifying rather than a tolerable trade.** Removing bins 0-40 removes the DC and near-DC content that sets the impulse response's baseline; the inverse transform of a spectrum with a hole at the bottom is not the room's impulse response in any useful sense, and a spatial-audio representation whose time-domain output is uncorrelated with the truth (r = 0.06) has failed at the thing it is for, whatever its modal correlation says. The modal gain (+0.05 in-slab) does not come close to paying for it. (d) **Decision**: the unmasked loss is the primary configuration downstream. DC-masking remains available and is the right tool for a frequency-domain-only diagnostic, but it must never be used for an arm whose output will be auditioned or evaluated in the time domain, and any future report of a DC-masked LSD must be accompanied by the RIR correlation. (e) **Caveat on scope**: both arms here FAILED Gate 2, so this compares two failing configurations. The ordering is consistent and the RIR collapse is unambiguous, but the conclusion is about the loss's effect on what the model learns, not a claim that the unmasked arm is adequate.

**D71 — Q19 RESOLVED by running it: Stage 2 went ahead under option (a), GATE 2 FAILED on all four arms, and the failure is the informative one Q19 predicted.** (a) **The choice made.** Q19 asked whether Stage 2 should run as specified or whether the renderer needed a geometry-aware occlusion term first. Option (a) — run it and let it answer empirically — was taken, on the grounds that it was cheapest and that a failure paired with a sigma ratio of ~1 would be a strong, cheap result that redirects the phase. That is exactly what happened, so the choice is recorded as having paid for itself rather than as a gamble that went wrong. (b) **The verdict.** GATE 2 requires in-slab test shapes to reach spatial Pearson >= 0.80 mean with an NLOS deficit <= 0.15. **FAIL on all four arms**: in-slab spatial Pearson 0.7978 (mean/masked), 0.7483 (mean/unmasked), 0.5763 (extent/masked), 0.5681 (extent/unmasked). The best arm misses by **0.0022**. The NLOS-deficit criterion PASSES on all four, every deficit negative (-0.056, -0.120, -0.126, -0.065) on 354 pooled in-slab receiver-modes. (c) **The near-miss must not be reported as a near-pass.** 0.7978 against 0.80 is 0.3% short and it would be easy to call it a pass in spirit. It is not, for a reason independent of the threshold: the sigma probe reads ~1.0 on the same checkpoint, so whatever produced 0.7978 is not an occlusion mechanism, and the number would not be expected to improve with the kind of scaling that usually closes a 0.3% gap. The threshold was frozen before the run and is not revisited after seeing the result. (d) **The NLOS pass is not evidence of shadow modelling and should not be cited as such.** All four deficits are negative — NLOS receivers are predicted slightly BETTER than LOS ones. If the model represented shadow geometry, NLOS would be the hard case. The most likely explanation is that NLOS receivers here sit deep in a corner where the field is smooth and low-variance, so they are easier for reasons that have nothing to do with the occluder. The criterion is reported with its n (354 pooled receiver-modes, 1.2% mean NLOS census, 5 of 15 test shapes with ZERO NLOS receivers) precisely so this cannot be over-read. (e) **What is now established and carried into Q21**: the architecture can FIT one non-convex room (P4-1 Gate 1, +0.974) and cannot GENERALIZE across a shape family (P4-2 Gate 2, 0.798), and on both the sigma field shows no physically meaningful occluder. The gap between those two results is the memorization D66 makes structurally available.

**D72 — a maximum-gap sampling constraint bounds the largest HOLE, not the uniformity, and the P4-2 corpus is 7.5x denser in some deciles than others while formally satisfying it.** (a) **What was specified and what was delivered.** The chunk targeted `max_normalized_gap <= 0.125` on every parameter (D67d), and the frozen manifest records a realized `d_hat` gap of **0.0703** — comfortably inside. The invariant passed, the dataset gate passed, and the corpus is nevertheless **strongly bimodal in `d_hat`**: 15 training shapes in [0.0, 0.1), then 2, 3, 5, 3 across [0.1, 0.5), zero in the deliberately held-out [0.5, 0.6), then 12, 4, 6, 10 across the top four deciles. Densest decile / sparsest sampled decile = **7.5x**. (b) **Why both things are true at once.** A maximin design maximizes the MINIMUM separation between points, which is exactly a guarantee about the largest empty interval. It places no lower bound on how many points land in any given sub-range, so a draw can be gap-legal and still clump. Part of P4-2's bimodality is even mandated rather than accidental: the `>= 8 pure rectangles` requirement pins 8 shapes at `d_hat = 0` exactly, which by itself fills over half the first decile. (c) **It is measurable and it matters.** The shape-edit sweep's accuracy curve correlates **r = +0.45** with training density in the local decile — real, but far from determinative, and the decisive counter-example is that the **zero-density hold-out decile outscores three deciles that do contain training shapes** (+0.791 against +0.742 / +0.758 / +0.763). So the curve is not a density artifact, but density is a live confound that had to be measured rather than argued away, and `training_density()` in `scripts/p4_2_sweep_figures.py` now computes it as a standing control plotted beside the headline. (d) **Consequence for future chunks**: do not read "max gap <= X" as "evenly sampled". If a chunk's conclusion depends on even coverage of a parameter — and a per-parameter accuracy curve always does — constrain the per-bin COUNT directly, or report the realized density histogram alongside the gap so the reader can see which it is. The gap invariant stays; it is necessary and not sufficient.

**D73 — `load_model` never passed `token_pool`, so P4-2's two extent arms were EVALUATED AS masked_mean. D69 is RETRACTED; the Gate 2 verdict, D70 and D71 are unaffected.** (a) **The bug.** `aaf/eval/p3_2_eval.load_model` rebuilt `INR2D_AutoDecoder` from the checkpoint cfg but omitted `token_pool`, so every polygon arm was reconstructed with the default `masked_mean`. **For `extent_sum` this is SILENT**: extent-weighted pooling adds no parameters, so its `state_dict` is key-identical to masked_mean's and `load_state_dict` succeeds without a word — the checkpoint then renders through a pooling it was never trained with, and the eval reports numbers for an arm that was never evaluated. It surfaced only because P4-3's attention arms DO add parameters (`q/k/v/o_proj`) and therefore failed loudly on an unexpected-keys error. A parameter-free variant hiding behind a parameter-ful one is the same silent-wrong-answer class as D64(a), D65(a) and D67(c). (b) **What the correct numbers are.** Re-evaluated with the fix, in-slab spatial Pearson: `extent_masked` **0.5763 -> 0.7835** (+0.2072), `extent_unmasked` **0.5681 -> 0.7916** (+0.2235). Band LSD `extent_unmasked` 8.77 -> 4.81 dB. **The control is exact**: `mean_unmasked` re-evaluates to 0.7483, a delta of **+0.0000**, confirming the fix changes nothing for a masked_mean arm and only corrects the extent ones. (c) **D69 IS WRONG AND IS RETRACTED.** It claimed extent-weighted pooling is "WORSE than masked mean, consistently", by 0.2215 (masked) and 0.1803 (unmasked), and called that "two arms, two independent loss configurations, same ordering -- this is not a seed artifact." It was not a seed artifact; it was an evaluation artifact, and both gaps were manufactured by rendering extent checkpoints through a mean-pool forward pass. The **corrected** gaps are **+0.0142 (masked) and -0.0432 (unmasked)** — i.e. under the unmasked loss that D70 makes primary, **extent_sum BEATS masked_mean**. The four arms sit within 0.05 of one another and the pooling axis is **not** resolved by this data. The mechanism D69(c) hypothesized (the raw sum's norm scaling with room size) may still be real but has no support here. (d) **What is NOT affected, checked rather than assumed.** The **GATE 2 verdict is unchanged**: all four arms still FAIL, and the best is still `mean_masked` at **0.7978** against the 0.80 threshold. The **sigma ratios are unchanged** at ~1.0 (0.9869-1.0361), so D71's mechanism finding and Q21 stand. **D70 SURVIVES INTACT and is now better supported**: corrected RIR Pearson is 0.0630 / 0.0783 for the two DC-masked arms against 0.9989 / 0.9986 for the two unmasked ones — the collapse is reproduced on four correctly-evaluated arms instead of two. (e) **The guard**: `load_model` now passes `token_pool` from the checkpoint, and a test asserts it by reading the file, so a future arm cannot be silently rebuilt with the wrong aggregator. **Standing lesson**: when two variants differ only in FORWARD BEHAVIOUR and not in parameters, a checkpoint round-trip cannot detect a mix-up — the config key must be threaded explicitly and tested, because `load_state_dict` will never complain.

**D74 — the sigma probe counted everything outside the polygon as "solid", and 82% of its samples had left the room entirely. Clipped to the bounding box; P4-1/P4-2's conclusions are unaffected, but a LOCALIZED contrast was being diluted toward 1.0.** (a) **The flaw.** `probe_sigma_occlusion` fires from an NLOS receiver toward the source out to `reach` = the bbox diagonal, then splits samples with `solid = ~polygon_contains(verts, pts)`. The ray passes the source and exits through the FAR WALL, and every sample beyond that wall is also outside the polygon — so it was counted as occluder. Measured on P4-2's ten probeable test shapes: **1707 of 2091 "solid" samples (82%) were outside the room, 99% on one shape**, and the reported crossing lengths (3.3-4.1 m) were mostly free space rather than notch. (b) **Why the published numbers survive anyway.** P4-1 and P4-2 both measured near-UNIFORM sigma fields, where the notch interior and the outside-the-room region read the same value, so the ratio is ~1.0 under either definition — confirmed by an independent volume probe: baseline `mean_unmasked` reads 1.049 (train) / 1.041 (test) sampling the removed corner directly, against 1.036 from the ray. **D71's "no occlusion mechanism" finding and Q21 stand unchanged.** (c) **Why it mattered here.** Arm S deliberately forces a contrast INSIDE the removed corner. Unclipped, the ray probe read **1.31**; volume-sampling the corner reads **8.08** on unseen shapes. The diluting 82% would have hidden a real, generalizing mechanism behind a number that looks like its absence — which is precisely the failure mode this diagnostic exists to prevent. (d) **The fix and what it reveals.** `solid` is now `(outside the polygon) AND (inside the bounding box)`, with `n_samples_beyond_room` reported so the clipping is auditable. Clipped, `n_solid` falls from 172-247 to 7-43 and crossings from 3.3-4.1 m to **0.14-0.81 m** — and Arm S reads only **1.09** there. That is not a contradiction of the 8.08: the ray from an NLOS receiver to a source that sits BELOW the notch grazes the removed corner near the reflex vertex, while volume sampling covers its interior. **Arm S raised sigma 8x in the notch bulk and left it flat on the path the renderer actually integrates along.** Both numbers are reported, with their meanings, because either alone is misleading. (e) **Standing consequence**: the ray probe answers "is there attenuation along the occlusion path", the volume probe answers "is there attenuation in the wall". They are different questions and a chunk that cares about mechanism should report both.

**D75 — the shallow-notch weakness was a DATA problem. Arm D flattens the U completely with sigma ~ 1.0, and PASSES Gate 2.** (a) **The result.** 32 extra training shapes in `d_hat` in [0.10, 0.45) — 60 -> 92 shapes, the thin band 13 -> 45 — with architecture, loss, optimizer and iteration budget all unchanged. Sweep spatial Pearson over 20 unseen depths at a fixed bounding box: **shallow half (d_hat <= 0.32) +0.8893, deep half (>= 0.74) +0.8878**, mean **+0.8811** against the baseline's +0.7017 / +0.8375 / +0.7615. The U is not merely reduced, it is **gone** — U amplitude 0.2185 -> **0.0752**, and the curve's best point is now the pure rectangle (+0.9262) with its worst at maximum depth (+0.8510), i.e. the ordering has INVERTED. (b) **Gate 2 PASSES**: in-slab spatial Pearson **0.8021** >= 0.80 with NLOS deficit -0.0875, the first pass in this phase after four P4-2 failures. Band LSD 4.42 dB, the best of any arm; RIR Pearson 0.9992. (c) **And it carries NO mechanism**: sigma ratio **0.9328** by the clipped ray probe and **1.042** by the volume probe — indistinguishable from the baseline's 1.036 / 1.041. This matches the chunk's pre-registered third branch exactly: *"If Arm D alone flattens the U with sigma ~ 1.0, the shallow-notch weakness was a data problem and the mechanism question is separable from it."* It is, and it is. (d) **The caveat, which is conservative rather than flattering**: 92 shapes at a matched 60K means ~35% fewer gradient steps per shape than the 60-shape baseline, so Arm D wins *despite* a handicap deliberately chosen to isolate data as the only variable. (e) **Consequence**: D72's sampling-density warning was not merely methodological. Corpus density along a shape parameter is a first-order driver of accuracy along that parameter, and the fix is cheap — 32 FDTD rooms, ~5 minutes of CPU, against a 12-hour training run.

**D76 — attention pooling improves accuracy and produces NO mechanism; the residual form is the one that works, and the pure replacement is worse than the baseline.** (a) **X1 (residual) PASSES Gate 2** at in-slab **0.8438**, the best Gate-2 number of any arm in the phase, against the baseline's 0.7483; band LSD 4.61, NLOS deficit -0.0015.
