Sweep mean +0.7836 vs +0.7615, with the gain concentrated at the shallow end (+0.7503 vs +0.7017). (b) **X2 (pure replacement) FAILS** at 0.7789 and is WORSE than the baseline across the whole sweep (mean +0.7011 vs +0.7615). Its U amplitude is the second-flattest in the phase (0.0819) **by uniform degradation, not by improvement** — a flatness statistic read without the level would have scored X2 as a success, which is why both are reported. The residual-vs-replacement question the user asked for was worth the extra run: the two forms do not merely differ in convergence speed, they differ in sign. (c) **The pre-registered condition is NOT met.** The chunk required *"X raises sigma meaningfully above 1.0 AND flattens the U"* to confirm the diagnosis and adopt attention. X1 flattens the U (0.2185 -> 0.1633) and does **not** raise sigma: **1.130** by the clipped ray probe, 1.180 by the volume probe. So attention helps for some reason **other than** representing occlusion, and the hypothesis that mean pooling's position-independence was the mechanism bottleneck is **not confirmed**. (d) **Measured directly, the attention barely varies with position at all**: the position-dependent fraction of the pooled conditioning, `||z - mean(z)|| / ||z||` over an interior grid, is **0.007 for X1 and 0.014 for X2**. The conditioning remains ~99% uniform across the room. The mechanism was made available and training did not use it. A plausible cause is the `1/sqrt(d)` score scaling with d = 64 keeping the logits small and the softmax near-uniform; a sharper temperature or a learned scale is the obvious next variant and was NOT tested. (e) **Adopted narrowly**: `attn_residual` is worth carrying as an aggregator because it beats the baseline on the gated metric, but it must not be described as having solved occlusion.

**D77 — injecting the mechanism did NOT help. Arm S learns a real, generalizing 8x sigma contrast inside the wall and its fields get WORSE.** (a) **The intervention worked, on its own terms.** The auxiliary hinge drove the sigma ratio above its target within ~400 iterations and held it there: the hinge was active in only **3 of 300** logged checkpoints, i.e. satisfied for essentially the whole run. And it **generalizes** — volume-sampling the removed corner on UNSEEN test shapes reads **8.08** (6.56 on training shapes), against the baseline's 1.04. This is the only arm in the phase with a genuine occluder in the wall. (b) **It made the model worse at everything it was supposed to help.** Gate 2 **FAILS** at in-slab **0.7329**, *below* the 0.7483 baseline; out-of-slab 0.6650 vs 0.7369; sweep mean **+0.5906** against +0.7615, worse at all 20 depths; the U is not flattened (amplitude 0.2273 vs 0.2185). (c) **Reported exactly as pre-registered.** The chunk said: *"If Arm S improves fields while sigma rises only because it was forced, note that mechanism was injected rather than learned."* The mechanism was injected rather than learned — and the fields did not improve, they degraded, which is the stronger version of that caution. **A learned occluder is not sufficient for shape generalization, and on this evidence it is not even helpful.** (d) **Where the injected contrast actually sits matters.** The clipped ray probe reads only **1.09** for Arm S: the source lies below the notch, so the receiver->source ray grazes the removed corner near the reflex vertex over 0.14-0.81 m while the 8x contrast fills its interior (D74d). So the term put attenuation where it was supervised and not where the renderer integrates. A sigma supervision targeted ON the occlusion path rather than in the volume is a different experiment and is untested. (e) **Consequence for Q21**: option (c) has been run and comes back negative. The evidence now points away from "the renderer cannot represent occlusion" as the binding constraint, because a model that demonstrably DOES represent it is the worst arm in the phase.

**D78 — Q22 RESOLVED: the held-out slab was never where the difficulty lived; a hold-out band is not a substitute for measuring accuracy ACROSS a parameter.** (a) **The question.** P4-2 observed that in-slab spatial Pearson exceeded out-of-slab on all four arms and asked whether the `d_hat in [0.45, 0.60]` hold-out was isolating the difficulty it was designed to isolate. (b) **The answer, on a clean axis.** The shape-edit sweep varies ONLY `d` at a fixed bounding box, so `L`, `W` and `w` cannot confound it. The baseline's in-slab vs out-of-slab deficit is **+0.0060** — nil — while the same curve varies by **0.2185** from end to end, with its minimum at `d_hat` 0.21. The binding difficulty was the **shallow-notch regime**, which the hold-out design never examined, and which Arm D then largely eliminated with 32 extra training shapes (D75). The first of the two readings offered in Q22 was right: the slab is barely wider than one sample spacing and does not bite. (c) **Standing consequence**: report the per-parameter accuracy CURVE; treat any slab statistic as secondary. The curve found a 0.22 effect that the gate criterion scored as +0.006. A gate that reads one band of one parameter can be satisfied while the model is weak somewhere the gate never looks — which is what happened for two chunks. (d) **Gate 2's criterion is not withdrawn** — it remains the frozen, comparable number across P4-2 and P4-3, and two P4-3 arms pass it. The point is that it should never again be the ONLY shape-generalization number reported.

**D79 — `enumerate_modes` is the ANALYTIC RECTANGULAR mode list and P4-1/P4-2/P4-3 applied it to non-convex rooms with no written justification. The practice is defensible, the silence was not.** (a) **What it is.** `aaf.eval.modal_projection.enumerate_modes(L, W, f_max)` delegates to `eigenfrequencies_2d`, whose modes are the separable Neumann cosines of a RECTANGLE: `f = (c/2) sqrt((n_x/L)^2 + (n_y/W)^2)`. For an L-room, a U or a T, `(L, W)` is the BOUNDING BOX, the true eigenfunctions are not separable cosines, and the true eigenfrequencies are not that formula. Every shape chunk has nonetheless called it -- `p4_1_stage1_eval`, `p4_2_stage2_eval`, `p4_2_sweep_figures`, `p4_3_demo_pack` -- and Gate 1, Gate 2, the sweep curve and the demo all key on its output. (b) **Why it is nevertheless sound, stated here for the first time.** Only `m.f` is consumed, and only to pick which frequency BINS to correlate at. Prediction and ground truth are read at the SAME bin, so a bin that does not sit on a true resonance of the notched room costs the metric sensitivity -- it may be sampling a less modal part of the spectrum -- but it cannot bias prediction against truth, because the same (possibly wrong) bin is used for both. The metric is therefore conservative rather than flattering, which is the direction an unjustified choice should err in. (c) **What it does NOT license.** Nothing in these chunks may be described as a measurement of "the modes of an L-room", and the per-mode breakdowns printed by the evaluators are per-BIN, not per-mode, once the room is non-convex. The `mode_shape_matrix` / `project_field` path -- which would genuinely require the right eigenfunctions, and whose `cond(Phi) <= 5` guard assumes them -- is never invoked on a polygon and must not be. (d) **Why this is recorded as a correction.** Three chunks reported gate numbers that depend on this function without one sentence establishing that it applies. The argument above is not new work; it was always available and nobody wrote it down, which meant a reader had to either take the numbers on faith or rediscover the reasoning. The standing rule is the general one: **when a tool is used outside the regime its docstring scopes it to, the justification goes in writing at the point of first use, not in the reader's head.** (e) **Consequence for P4-4 Task 3**: the family evaluator keeps using it, for the same reason and with the same caveat, and its report records `n_modes` explicitly so a reader cannot mistake a 3-mode demo number for a 6-mode gate number.

**D80 — THE SHAPE DATA-SCALING LAW: at matched compute, scaling the corpus 5.4x bought NOTHING on the gated metric and cost 0.22 on the depth sweep. Corpus scaling trades per-room fidelity for breadth, and which one you measure decides whether "scaling worked".** (a) **The measurement.** Three runs of one architecture (`attn_residual_extent`, unmasked, 60K) on strictly NESTED corpora -- 92 subset 250 subset 500 -- with the 15 Gate-2 test shapes and the 20 sweep depths byte-identical across all three. Nothing varies but how much data the model saw. (b) **Gate 2 is FLAT.** In-slab spatial Pearson 0.8337 (92) -> 0.8420 (250) -> 0.8336 (500). The 92->250 step is +0.0082 and the 250->500 step is **-0.0083**: the curve saturates by 250 and reverses after. Net over 5.4x more data: **-0.0001**. Band LSD likewise flat-to-worse, 4.46 -> 4.43 -> 4.51. (c) **The depth sweep COLLAPSES, monotonically.** Mean spatial Pearson over 20 unseen depths at a FIXED bounding box: **0.8276 -> 0.6857 -> 0.6036**, and the U amplitude grows 0.0619 -> 0.1027 -> 0.1206. The degradation is uniform across d_hat rather than concentrated in any regime, which is the signature of a global loss of per-room fidelity rather than a failure at some shape. (d) **The mechanism, and it is arithmetic.** The batch draws `(batch_size // rx_per_config) * grad_accum_steps = 8` configs per iteration, so 60K iterations is 480K config-draws however large the corpus is. Draws per shape: **5217 / 1920 / 960**. The sweep probes ONE bounding box across 20 depths and is sensitive to how well each individual room was fit; Gate 2 probes 15 VARIED shapes and is sensitive to breadth. Falling steps-per-shape hurts the first and not the second, which is exactly the observed pattern. (e) **This does NOT contradict D75, it bounds it.** D75 found 60->92 shapes eliminated the U-shaped accuracy curve for five minutes of CPU, and concluded density along a shape parameter is a first-order driver. That holds -- in the regime where BREADTH is limiting. By 250-500 shapes at a fixed 60K budget, steps-per-shape becomes limiting instead and the trade reverses. **The lesson is not "data does not help"; it is that corpus size and iteration budget must scale together, and P4-4 deliberately held iterations fixed to isolate data.** A 500-shape corpus at 8000 draws/shape (i.e. ~500K iterations) is untested and is the obvious next question. (f) **Reporting rule carried forward**: a scaling claim must name which metric it scaled. "More data helped" is true of Gate 2 from 60 to 250 and false of the sweep everywhere, on the same runs.

**D81 — attention-on-an-extent-base BEATS masked-mean on the gated metric and LOSES on every other metric, on an identical corpus. The two metric families disagree in sign, and the gated one was used to decide.** (a) **The ablation.** C92 and P4-3's Arm D are the SAME 92-shape corpus and the SAME 15 test shapes, differing only in `token_pool`. In-slab spatial Pearson **0.8337 vs 0.8021, +0.0316 to the new pool**; out-of-slab **0.8060 vs 0.7641, +0.0419**. Both are the largest architecture-attributable gains measured in this phase. (b) **And it loses everywhere else.** Val LSD **4.311 vs 2.508** (1.8 dB worse, on held-out receivers of the identical corpus, so directly comparable); depth-sweep mean **0.8276 vs 0.8811**; band LSD 4.46 vs 4.42. This is not a wash -- it is a consistent split along one line: the new pool generalizes better ACROSS varied shapes and fits any GIVEN room worse. (c) **The split reproduces P4-3 exactly and that is why it was believed.** There, X1 had the worst val LSD in the phase (4.273) and the best Gate-2 score (0.8438). P4-4 predicted the same inversion from the val-LSD trajectory before Gate 2 ran -- C92 converged to 4.347 against X1's 4.344 at 52K -- and Gate 2 then confirmed it. Two independent chunks, same sign, same explanation: **val LSD is in-distribution on training-corpus receivers; Gate 2 is 15 unseen shapes. They measure different things and a shape-generalization chunk is gated on the second.** (d) **The extent base is very nearly inert.** C92 (extent base + attention) lands within 0.003 dB of X1 (mean base + attention) on val LSD and within 0.01 on Gate 2. The chunk spec's premise that attention-residual and extent-weighted pooling are two orthogonal wins that combine **does not hold**: attention dominates and the base it rides on barely matters. D69's retraction already showed extent_sum's apparent advantage was an evaluation artifact (D73); this shows its real advantage, measured correctly, is small enough to vanish under attention. (e) **Adopted for Task 3** on the gated evidence, with the disagreement recorded rather than smoothed over.

**D82 — P4-4's targets were MISSED by every arm, including the best. Reported as a miss.** (a) The chunk set in-slab spatial Pearson >= 0.88 and band LSD < 3.0 dB. Best in-slab across the whole phase is **0.8420** (C250) against 0.88; best band LSD is **4.42** (P4-3 D) against 3.0. **No arm met either target, and the LSD target was missed by 47%.** (b) The LSD miss was foreseeable and was flagged before the runs finished: the three P4-4 arms converged to val LSD ~4.3-4.5 against D's 2.5, and the plan recorded "LSD < 3 is a 32% reduction and may not fall out of scale alone" as a stated risk. It did not. (c) **What did improve**: all three P4-4 arms PASS the frozen Gate-2 criterion (0.80), where all four P4-2 arms failed, and out-of-slab generalization reached **0.8359** (C250), the best measured in the phase. The gate moved; the targets did not. (d) The targets are not retroactively relaxed and the frozen 0.80 criterion is kept alongside them, so an arm that clears the old bar cannot be reported as having met this chunk's goal.

**D83 — corner count degrades accuracy MONOTONICALLY (0.847 -> 0.766 -> 0.712), and 2-corner rooms hold well enough that Z is not ruled out — but the degradation is NOT mainly about corners.** (a) **The measurement.** One model on 300 training rooms -- 60 each of rect (0 reflex corners, 4 tokens), L (1, 6), U (2, 8), T (2, 8), DN (2, 8) -- evaluated on 50 held-out rooms, 10 per family, 3 in-slab per notched family. Dataset gate 14/14 including the simulator item (an L and a U with the same bounding box differ by 4.31 dB mean). (b) **The result.** Spatial Pearson by reflex-corner count: **0 -> +0.8469, 1 -> +0.7663, 2 -> +0.7116**; band LSD 4.44 / 4.69 / 4.77. Monotone, with the first step (-0.081) slightly larger than the second (-0.055) -- i.e. decelerating, not collapsing. Per family at 2 corners: T +0.7582, DN +0.7039, U +0.6727. **U is the hardest**, which is geometrically sensible: two notches on the SAME wall leave a narrow north stem and the most severely non-convex interior of the three. (c) **What this says about Z, carefully.** 2-corner rooms retain 84% of the rectangle's accuracy and the per-step loss is shrinking, so corner count alone does not forbid a 3-corner Z. But the Z-corridor has **10 tokens**, and D84 shows token count is the dominant term -- so the right predictor for Z is the tokenization result, not this one. Z is not ruled out; it is also not licensed by this table alone. (d) **sigma is 1.0132** over 32 probeable rooms, unchanged from every measurement since P4-2 (D71, D74, D75). Adding reflex corners does not make the model discover occlusion.

**D84 — THE HEADLINE OF TASK 3: re-describing an IDENTICAL room with redundant tokens costs MORE accuracy (-0.13 to -0.24) than adding two reflex corners does (-0.135). The conditioning is not invariant to tokenization, and that -- not corner count -- is what Z will hinge on.** (a) **The control, and why it is clean.** Comparing a rectangle (4 tokens) with a U (8) confounds token count with geometry. This instead re-tokenizes the SAME room with COLLINEAR vertices: identical physics, identical `.h5`, identical receivers, identical polygon by construction (verified: `polygon_contains` agrees on 4000 random points), with only the conditioning vector changing. `MAX_SEG_POLY = 12` bounds what is reachable, which is what makes the rectangle the best control in the corpus -- one room at **4, 8 and 12 tokens**. (b) **The numbers.** rect **4 -> 8 tokens: +0.8469 -> +0.6029 (-0.2440)**; rect 4 -> 12: -0.1306; L **6 -> 12: +0.7663 -> +0.5642 (-0.2021)**. Every one of these is a room the model reconstructs well when described minimally and poorly when described redundantly. The whole corner-count effect, 0 to 2 reflex corners, is -0.1353. **The tokenization effect is larger, on rooms whose geometry did not change at all.** (c) **THE HONEST READING, and it is not "token count costs accuracy".** The model was trained with ONE tokenization convention -- one token per polygon edge, minimal representation -- so in training, 8 tokens ALWAYS meant a 2-corner room and 4 tokens always meant a rectangle. A redundantly-tokenized rectangle is therefore **out of distribution in the conditioning**, and this control measures robustness to that shift rather than an intrinsic cost per token. **The non-monotonicity proves it**: a rectangle scores +0.6029 at 8 tokens but +0.7163 at 12, and a monotone "more tokens is worse" law cannot produce that. What the data supports is the weaker and more useful claim: **the learned conditioning depends on HOW a room is described, not only on WHICH room it is.** (d) **Consequence for the Z-corridor, which is the decision this was run to inform.** A Z has 10 tokens and every training room has 4, 6 or 8. It is out of distribution in exactly the dimension this control shows the model is fragile in, and the fragility is worth more accuracy than the extra corner. **Before Stage 3, train with tokenization AUGMENTATION** -- present each room at several equivalent tokenizations so the conditioning is forced toward invariance -- and re-run this control to confirm the gap closes. That is one training run, needs no new simulation (the control reuses existing `.h5` unchanged), and it converts Z from a gamble into a test. (e) **Standing consequence**: any future claim about token-count generalization must use the fixed-geometry control, not an across-family comparison, because the across-family version confounds the two and would have reported -0.135 for an effect that is really at least -0.20.

**D85 — `figN_morph` printed `notch width 0.00 m` for two chunks because the title read a variable off the one frame for which it is undefined.** (a) **The bug.** `fig_morph` formats `cfg0.w` where `cfg0 = rooms[0]["cfg"]`. Under `--morph-full-range` frame 0 is `d_hat = 0.00`, and `build_p4_2_sweep.py:63` stores `w = 0.0 if d <= 0.0 else SWEEP_W_NOTCH` — a rectangle has no notch, so it has no notch width. Every other frame in the strip carries `w = 2.00`. The figure therefore announced "notch width 0.00 m all FIXED" over a strip whose notch is plainly present and growing, and the same value went into `demo_metrics.json["morph"]["w"]`. (b) **Why it survived.** The value is not wrong in any way the code can detect: `0.0` is a legal width, the frame it came from is a legal frame, and nothing downstream consumes the number. It was caught by a reader looking at the picture, which is the only place the two disagree. (c) **The fix and its scope.** The width is constant across every notched frame by construction, so it is read as `max(r["cfg"].w for r in rooms)`. `fig_fields` has the identical pattern but is NOT affected: the demo shapes sweep `d_hat` over 0.50–0.95 and frame 0 always has a notch. It is left alone rather than changed speculatively. (d) **The general rule this is an instance of.** **A summary statistic taken from one member of a set is only safe if the quantity is defined for every member.** `L` and `W` are fixed across the strip and reading them off frame 0 is fine; `w` is not defined for a rectangle and reading it off frame 0 is not. Where a "fixed" parameter is being printed, derive it from the set (max, or an assert that it is unique among the frames that have it), never from an arbitrary element. (e) **Cost of the correction**: none. The render cache (`render_morph.npz`, 310 MB) was intact, so the figure was re-plotted in ~30 s and every published number is unchanged.

**D86 — the demo-v2 sweeps are NEW FDTD rather than snapped to existing rooms, because no corpus on disk contains a sweep.** (a) **What was asked of the data.** A morph strip needs 8 frames in which ONE shape parameter moves and everything else is fixed. Across the 515 single-notch rooms, the largest group sharing `(L, W, d)` holds **2 distinct notch widths**; the largest sharing `(L, d, w)` holds **1 distinct `W`**; the largest U group holds **1 distinct second-notch depth**. (b) **Why snapping was rejected.** "Snap to the nearest existing rooms" would have produced a row in which three parameters move at once, which destroys the single thing the format exists to show — and would have done so invisibly, since the figure looks the same either way and only the caption would have been false. (c) **Why the cost is acceptable.** At figN's receiver density a room is 19–64 s of FDTD depending on node contention; 36 unique rooms across five sweeps completed in minutes as a parallel array, well inside the stated ~10-minute budget. The cheap-looking option was the incorrect one and the correct one was also cheap. (d) **Standing rule**: a "sweep" figure must be built from a deliberate line through the parameter space, not assembled from whatever draws happen to be nearby. If the line does not exist, simulate it or do not publish the figure.

**D87 — v2 figures print figN's THREE-mode spatial Pearson, and every place they sit beside a six-mode number says so.** The demo pack averages spatial Pearson over the first 3 resolvable modes; `p4_4_family_eval` and Gate 2 use 6. **The difference is NOT a fixed offset in a known direction.** On the P4-3 demo rooms the 3-mode number read above the gate; `s3c`'s terminal frame -- the same room `p4_4_family_eval` scored +0.7968 -- reads **+0.7052** here. Two things differ there, not one: the mode count AND the receiver set (800 scattered points in the family corpus against ~2.6k on a 0.08 m grid). Any comparison across the two protocols must therefore be refused outright rather than adjusted for. The v2 pack keeps 3 so a new strip can be read beside `figN_morph`, and states the protocol in the figure caption, in `<sweep>_metrics.json` (`n_modes`, `n_modes_note`) and in `FIGURE_MANIFEST.md`. This matters most in two places where the two protocols appear together: the family gallery's caption, which quotes the six-mode per-family held-out means for context, and `s3c`, whose terminal frame is the best-scoring held-out U room at +0.7968 (six-mode). Neither is a number the figure should reproduce, and both say so. **No number in the v2 pack may be quoted against a gate threshold.**

**D88 — `legacy43` fails every job at launch, and array retries hid it as flakiness.** 24 of 24 array tasks that landed on `legacy43` died in 3 s with exit signal 53, the batch step `CANCELLED`, and **no log file written at all** — not even the first `echo`. Every task on every other node (`legacy41`, `legacy37`, `quics00`, `tron65/66/69`) passed. Because SLURM spreads an array across nodes, the failure looked like a 60%-random flake until the failures were grouped by `NodeList`, at which point it was 100% deterministic. `--exclude=legacy43` is now in both demo-v2 launchers. **Standing rule: when array tasks fail with empty logs, group by node before touching the code** — an empty log means the job died before the payload ran, so the payload is not the suspect. Separately, `scavenger` was queueing the whole pipeline behind a backlog; `tron` with `--account=nexus --qos=default` picked up 16 tasks immediately and is the right partition for short, latency-sensitive figure work.

**D89 — the render cache was keyed on checkpoint + room filenames but NOT on the DATA DIRECTORY, and the same filename exists in two corpora at different receiver densities.** (a) **The hazard.** `p4_4_sweep_figures_v2.load_frames` cached on `(checkpoint, "|".join(filenames))`. The gallery rooms exist BOTH in `data/track_p4_4_family` (800 scattered receivers, the corpus the published family eval used) and, after the demo-v2 re-simulation, in `data/track_p4_4_sweeps` (~3-4k on a 0.08 m grid). Same filenames, same checkpoint, different arrays — so switching `--family-dir` would have silently redrawn the 800-point fields under a caption stating the dense ones, with no error anywhere. (b) **How close it came.** It did not reach a published figure only because the stale cache was deleted by hand before the dense re-render. That is not a control; it is luck, and it is the same shape as D73 — a parameter that changes the OUTPUT while leaving the cache/checkpoint identity untouched. (c) **The fix.** `data_dir` is now part of the cache blob and is checked on load; a cache written before the field existed is REFUSED rather than trusted, because "absent" cannot be distinguished from "wrong". The six existing caches were migrated by appending the member to the `.npz` (which is a zip of `.npy`s), with the value known rather than guessed, and each re-verified readable. (d) **The general rule, which is D73's rule applied to caches**: **a cache key must name every input that changes the cached value, not every input that changes the computation's identity.** The checkpoint identifies the model; it does not identify the data. Anything read from disk belongs in the key. (e) **Found by an adversarial review of the figure pipeline, not by the pipeline failing.** Three independent reviewers of three different dimensions all surfaced it.

**D90 — marker size was scaled per frame by 1/n_rx, which drew the worst-scoring columns with the fattest dots.** (a) **The defect.** `fig_sweep` set `s = clip(12000/n_rx, 3, 30)` per frame. Every frame is sampled on the SAME 0.08 m grid and every panel shares ONE axis scale, so the correct dot size is identical in every column; `n_rx` varies only because the rooms have different AREAS. (b) **Why the direction matters.** Cutting a notch removes area, so `n_rx` falls monotonically along every sweep — and in both two-phase sweeps the frames with the most geometry removed are also the worst-scoring (s3a +0.840 -> +0.671, s3c +0.813 -> +0.705). Those frames were therefore drawn with the fattest, most overlapping markers and looked the SMOOTHEST. A per-column rendering difference with no physical cause, flattering exactly the columns that deserved it least. Magnitude was modest (<= 15% in diameter) and the `clip` at 3.0 also made it non-monotone, with s2's last three columns pinned while the first four were not. (c) **The fix.** One marker size for the whole row, derived from the receiver PITCH and the panel scale: `0.6 * (pi/4) * (RX_STEP * col_w * 72 / (Lmax + 0.4))^2` -- the size at which neighbouring receivers just touch, times 0.6 for legibility. All seven figures were re-plotted from cache; every metric is byte-identical, which confirms the change is rendering-only. (d) **Standing rule**: a visual property that varies across panels must be derived from something PHYSICAL that varies across panels. Deriving it from a proxy (here, receiver count standing in for density) silently couples the rendering to whatever else moves that proxy.

**D91 — a caption must MEASURE what it describes, and a blank-figure guard set at 2% ink could not have detected a blank figure.** (a) **The caption defect, and how close it came.** The gallery's caption asserted "the FDTD was re-run at figN's receiver density (0.08 m grid, ~3-4k points)" as PROSE, while the corpus it read was chosen by a `--family-dir` flag that defaulted to `data/track_p4_4_family` (800 scattered receivers) and that no launcher passed. The five gallery rooms exist in BOTH corpora under byte-identical filenames -- `FamilyConfig.filename` is derived from geometry alone -- so no `FileNotFoundError` could fire to say the wrong one had been opened. The published figure is correct only because the dense directory was passed explicitly on the command line. (b) **The fix is structural, not a corrected sentence.** The caption now APPENDS the receiver count measured from the rooms it is drawing ("Receivers actually drawn: N-M per panel"), and the `--family-dir` default is the sweep directory. A caption that counts cannot disagree with the arrays it labels; a caption that asserts can, and did. (c) **The blank guard was theatre.** `_assert_size` rejected a figure below 2% ink. A figure whose BOTH rows are entirely NaN still renders its title, axis labels, column headers and colourbar and measures **6.8%** -- so the guard would have passed two completely empty rows. Measured, not assumed: the 800-receiver gallery rendered at 7.3%, barely above that floor. (d) **The real check looks at the ARRAYS.** `fig_sweep` now asserts that at least half of each frame's drawn values are finite, per frame and per row, and names the frame and row that fail; the ink threshold is retained as a backstop and raised to 15%, above the all-NaN floor. Both were verified to FIRE on a deliberately NaN-ed frame before being trusted -- `frame 3 (w=1.32) row P is 0.0% finite at bin 69`. (e) **Two standing rules.** **A figure's prose must be derived from the data it draws wherever the two could disagree.** And **a guard whose firing has never been observed is not known to work**: this one had a threshold three times too low and nobody would have discovered it from a passing run.

**D92 — P4-5 PRE-REGISTRATION, committed before any P4-5 job ran.** (a) **The manager's hypothesis, verbatim**: "The dominant cause is loss weighting, not model capacity. `L_spec_real` and `L_spec_imag` are linear-domain and |H| rolls off with frequency, so high bins contribute negligible gradient. ... Prediction: **bins above 100 Hz contribute under 15% of total gradient combined.** If that holds, the model is not failing at high frequency, it is barely being asked to fit it." (b) **What is scored.** The PARAMETER gradient of the total weighted training loss, on `p4_4_C250` @ 60K, decomposed by 10 Hz band G as the **projection share** s_G = <g_G, g_tot> / ||g_tot||^2 -- an exact additive decomposition (the s_G sum to 1; a band can be negative, i.e. pull against the net step). "Above 100 Hz" = bins >= 200 (f >= 100 Hz). Secondary, reported beside it: norm share ||g_G|| / sum_G' ||g_G'||. Raw gradient, before `nan_to_num_` and clipping, which is what the optimiser sees first. Also reported for a fresh seed-0 model, because the arms train from scratch and the balance at iteration 0 can differ from the balance at convergence. (c) **Two facts recorded BEFORE measuring, because they bear on how the result must be read.** (i) `_losses` (`aaf/train/multi_room_2d_mat.py:171-195`) uses **L1** for the spec terms. dL/dRe(P_b) = sign(Re P_b - Re T_b) / N: the gradient with respect to the OUTPUT has the same magnitude in every bin, however small |H_b| is. A rolled-off target therefore does NOT shrink a spec term's output gradient, and the a-priori spec-term output-gradient share of bins >= 100 Hz is **401/601 = 66.7% exactly**. `L_amp` (log10) and `L_phase` have output gradients proportional to 1/|P|, which weight LOW-magnitude bins -- the high frequencies -- MORE. So the hypothesis's stated mechanism cannot operate at the output. Starvation could still be real one step further back, through the network's Jacobian dP_b/dtheta, and the parameter gradient measures exactly that; the prediction can hold only by that route. (ii) The hypothesis cites "bins 0-39 took 23.7% of gradient". `scripts/p4_1_dc_diagnostic.py` computes that number under `torch.no_grad()` as a share of loss VALUE (`weighted_dc_share`), not of gradient. For an L1 loss these two quantities diverge exactly where this chunk is looking, so A1 reports both, plus the output-gradient share, and says which one the prediction is scored on. (d) **Consequence for B1, decided by the user before launch**: B1 as specified divides the L1 spec terms by per-bin RMS. Given (c)(i) that does not equalise gradient; it up-weights high frequency by roughly RMS_low/RMS_high. B1 runs **as written** and is read as "strong high-frequency up-weighting". A fifth arm, **B1b**, runs the same mechanism with per-band weights calibrated to A1's MEASURED spec-term gradient share, which is the stated intent ("every frequency contributes comparable gradient"). B1 and B1b share one code path -- a per-bin weight vector loaded from file -- so they differ ONLY in the weights.

**D93 — B4's premise was false: 4x receivers requires re-running FDTD.** The spec stated "FDTD already computes the full grid; this is a storage and sampling change, not new simulation". The solver keeps three rolling field buffers and records pressure ONLY at the requested receivers (`aaf/sim/fdtd_2d.py:957-998`); every corpus `.h5` holds `ism/H_complex` of shape (800, 601) and nothing else. There is no stored grid to resample. Re-simulation is nevertheless cheap -- median `loop_s` 12.7 s over the 250 training rooms, ~0.9 CPU-h in total, minutes as an array -- so B4 stays at 4x under the spec's 45-minute rule. **Construction**: the existing 800 receivers are kept VERBATIM as indices 0-799, and 2400 more are appended from the builder's own candidate pool (minimum pool 4621 in any room, so 3200 always fits). Re-drawing 3200 with the same seed would NOT have contained the original 800. Because `val_rx_indices = range(3, n_rx, 8)`, B4's training receivers are then a strict SUPERSET of the baseline's, and its validation receivers a superset of the baseline's -- B4 sees everything C250 saw, plus more. Written to `data/track_p4_5_rx4x/`, never over `data/track_p4_2_shapes`, which backs C92/C250/C500 and every published P4 eval. The trainer stacks receivers and so requires an identical count in every room; that is asserted at build time. `val_max_configs` is set to 10 for B4 only, holding validation at the baseline's 4000 rows (40 x 100) rather than 16000 (40 x 400), which would add ~2 h to a ~13 h arm.

**D94 — "the estimator floor" and κ do not transfer to shape, so A3 uses a pre-registered GT-validity criterion.** The spec asks for "the frequency above which [dx = 0.02 and dx = 0.01] disagree by more than the estimator floor". In this project the estimator is the calibrated -3 dB modal BANDWIDTH estimator (`aaf/eval/modal_bandwidth.py:measure_modes`) and its floor is P3-2 control C2, **0.040 Hz of bandwidth**; κ = 1.6608 is that estimator's calibration slope (`aaf/eval/p3_2b_slopefit.py:78`). Neither is a statement about spatial fields or spectra, and nothing in P4 uses either. Nothing in P4-5 changes them. **Criterion, fixed before A3 runs**: ground truth is VALID at frequency f where the spatial Pearson (in dB) between the dx = 0.02 and dx = 0.01 solutions, averaged over a 5 Hz window, is **>= 0.99**; the GT-validity limit is the first frequency, scanning up from 25 Hz, where that fails. Receivers sit on a 0.08 m grid so they land on nodes at BOTH resolutions -- a 64x64 linspace lattice would snap differently on the two grids and contaminate the comparison with a position error. Reported beside it: where (1 - r_conv) exceeds 10% of the baseline model's (1 - r_model), i.e. where ground-truth error starts to contaminate the model's score. **Prediction**: valid to 300 Hz. dx = 0.02 gives 57 points per wavelength at 300 Hz, and the relative phase-velocity error of the scheme at that resolution shifts a 300 Hz mode by ~0.04 Hz, well under the 0.5 Hz bin. The risk is geometric rather than dispersive: FT-1b A0c measured a 0.30 Hz modal shift at a 0.5 m aperture at dx = 0.02 (`outputs/ft1b/a0c_quantization.json`), and an L-room's reflex corner is the analogous feature here.

**D95 — B2 (coarse-to-fine hash levels) is run with a pre-registered NULL prediction.** The spec motivates B2 by spectral bias (C2F-IFWI) and says "start with the coarsest ~6 levels". C250's encoder has `base_resolution 80`, `per_level_scale 1.5`, `n_levels 20` (not 16) on positions scaled by 1/10. Its cell sizes are 0.127 m at level 0, 0.084 m at level 1, and 0.025 m at level 4. **Even the coarsest level is finer than lambda/8 at 300 Hz (0.143 m)**, and levels 4 and up are finer than the FDTD grid that produced the ground truth. No choice of starting level can therefore restrict the representable SPATIAL bandwidth below the acoustic band the loss covers: acoustic frequency is an OUTPUT dimension (601 bins), and the spatial structure it implies is already resolved at every level. **Prediction: B2 does not move the high-frequency tail** beyond the noise criterion in the evaluation plan. Run anyway, because it is free in parallel and a null result settles the C2F question for this architecture. Implementation decisions: the schedule is adapted to 20 levels as active(it) = min(20, 6 + floor(it / 2800)), which keeps the spec's "all active by ~40K" (all 20 at 39.2K; the spec's literal "+1 every 4K" would finish at 56K). The mask is a registered BUFFER on the model, so the mask state is saved in every checkpoint and a 30K B2 checkpoint reloads with exactly its 16 active levels -- otherwise evaluating it would silently include four never-trained levels, the D73 hazard class. It is applied AFTER FiLM: masking before FiLM would leave beta(z), not zero, in the masked columns.

**D96 — B1's weight vector, and the risk it carries, recorded before B1 trains.** Computed by `scripts/p4_5_bin_weights.py rms` over the 250 training configs and their training receivers only (160,000 rows; validation receivers excluded). Per-bin target RMS spans **1210 at DC** (bin 0, the FDTD compliance term P4-1 measured at 87.3% of in-band power) down to **0.46-0.58 above 200 Hz**; the minimum, 0.39, is at 176.5 Hz. The 5th-percentile floor (0.386) binds on 30 bins. Weights after the D92 scaling rule (mean_b(w_b m_b) = mean_b(m_b), verified to 1.000000): **0.0045 at DC, 1.28 at 10 Hz, 4.87 at 100 Hz, 10.8 at 200 Hz, 11.8 at 250 Hz**; 85.5% of the total weight sits on bins >= 100 Hz against 66.7% for a uniform vector. The headline max/min ratio of 3147 is almost entirely the DC bin; excluding it, the 10-250 Hz range spans ~9x. **The risk, pre-registered.** B1 cuts the DC bin's spec-term weight to ~1/280 of its 10 Hz neighbour. D70 showed that REMOVING the low bins from the loss collapsed RIR Pearson from 0.9989 to 0.0630, because the full-band inverse transform is dominated by that near-DC term. B1 does not remove DC -- L_amp and L_phase still carry it at full weight, and its spec weight is small but nonzero -- but it is the closest any P4-5 arm comes to D70. **Prediction: B1's Gate-2-style RIR Pearson (receiver 0, full band) is the first metric to show damage if any arm damages the time response**; the modal RIR (20-300 Hz) excludes DC and is the cleaner check of the high-frequency claim itself. Both are reported for every arm. B1b's ratio cap is B1's 3147, which in practice will not bind; the cap exists so B1b cannot be MORE extreme than B1, not to constrain it.

**D97 — A1 RESULT against the D92 pre-registration: the prediction HOLDS as scored, and the stated mechanism is REFUTED.** (a) **As pre-registered** (C250 @ 60K, parameter-gradient projection share of the total weighted loss, bins >= 100 Hz): **0.0092 +- 0.0044** (jackknife over 48 batches of 4 rows, 192 training rows). Prediction "< 15%": **HOLDS**, by a factor of ~16. The decomposition closes exactly (sum of shares = 1.000000) in both states. (b) **The two a-priori facts D92 recorded are confirmed to four decimals.** Spec-term OUTPUT-gradient share >= 100 Hz = **0.6672**, exactly the 401/601 an L1 loss must give; L_amp 0.843 and L_phase 0.894, both above uniform, as their 1/|P| gradients require. (c) **But at iteration 0 the same quantity is 0.657 +- 0.004** -- almost exactly the uniform 0.667. The loss does not starve high frequency of gradient; at initialisation it hands it its proportional share (via L_amp, which carries 99.8% of the gradient while the output is near zero and the log-domain error dominates). **The starvation EMERGES during training.** The hypothesis's mechanism -- "linear-domain terms + roll-off => negligible high-bin gradient" -- is therefore not what happens. (d) **What does happen, at 60K.** The linear spec terms carry 100% of the net step (spec_real 0.673, spec_imag 0.329; L_amp -0.003, L_phase +0.002). **The 0-10 Hz band alone carries ~93% of the net step and ~60% of the gradient norm**; cumulative projection share below 50 Hz is 0.977. Bins >= 100 Hz hold **74% of the remaining loss VALUE** but **9.9% of the gradient norm** (6.7x below their width share) and **0.9% of the projection** -- so their gradient is both SMALLER than proportional and largely ORTHOGONAL to the step, two separate effects. High-frequency L_amp pulls AGAINST the step (-0.016). (e) **Mechanism, labelled as inference.** Because L1 gives every bin the same output gradient, the concentration at 60K must come from the Jacobian dP_b/dtheta. The most consistent account: the trained network has learned to output ~1000x more at DC (mean |H| at bin 0 is 1210 against ~0.4-4 elsewhere), and the Jacobian scales with the magnitude it outputs; before that is learned the gradient is flat. Not measured directly here; a per-bin Jacobian norm would test it. (f) **Consequence for the arms, recorded before any of them reaches 30K.** B1 and B1b re-scale the high-frequency gradient MAGNITUDE (D96: 85.5% and 91.0% of their weight on bins >= 100 Hz). That addresses (d)'s first effect. It does not obviously address the second: a gradient that is orthogonal to the step does not become aligned by being scaled. Prediction: if B1/B1b lift the tail at all, the lift is smaller than the weight ratio suggests. B3, which removes the high bins entirely for 15K-35K iterations, attacks neither effect directly; B4 attacks neither via the loss but changes what the gradient is averaged over. (g) **Side finding: B1 and B1b converge.** Derived from unrelated principles -- target RMS and measured gradient share -- the two weight vectors agree closely in shape above 10 Hz (e.g. 100 Hz: 4.87 vs 6.96; 250 Hz: 11.8 vs 13.0) and differ mainly at 0-10 Hz, where B1 crushes only bin 0 (0.004) and B1b down-weights the whole band (0.068). Per-bin RMS normalisation is a good proxy for gradient-share equalisation above 10 Hz in this corpus.

**D98 — PRE-REGISTRATION for the energy diagnostic, committed before it was computed.** The manager's competing mechanism: the baseline may have found a DEGENERATE solution -- smooth output, accepting a small linear-domain penalty -- which would explain both A1's collapsed high-frequency gradient and A2's 0.25 correlation, and would make B1 the right fix after all. The alternative: energy is right and only the spatial pattern is wrong, i.e. a resolution or conditioning limit, which makes B2 matter more. **Measured on `p4_4_C250` @ 60K from the existing A2 render cache** (dense 3-shape and sparse 15-shape sets), per bin, then averaged over the 100-300 Hz band: (i) **energy ratio** dE = 10 log10(mean_rx |P|^2 / mean_rx |T|^2); (ii) **spatial contrast ratio** k = std_rx(dB|P|) / std_rx(dB|T|) -- how much spatial variation the prediction has relative to the truth. **Outcomes, fixed now:** **A, degenerate in energy** -- dE <= -3 dB (under half the power): B1 is the right fix. **B, degenerate in contrast** -- |dE| < 3 dB but k < 0.7: the level is right and the field is washed out toward its mean -- also a smooth/degenerate solution, but one that a per-bin loss WEIGHT does not obviously fix, because the spatial mean already carries the right energy. **C, resolution/conditioning limit** -- |dE| < 3 dB and k >= 0.7: right energy, right amount of spatial structure, wrong placement: B2 matters more. (dE >= +3 dB, over-prediction, is reported as such.) The manager's two outcomes are A and C; B is added because "output smooth" can show up as collapsed contrast at correct mean energy rather than as missing energy, and the two point at different fixes. The 0-100 Hz band is reported beside it as the control, since accuracy there is good.

**D99 — THE MODEL HAS MEMORISED THE TRAINING-RECEIVER POSITIONS. Every 800-receiver number in P4 measures it there, not as a continuous field.** (a) **How it surfaced.** D98's energy diagnostic returned OPPOSITE outcomes on the two receiver sets: dense 3-shape C (dE +0.03 dB, k 0.965), sparse 15-shape B/A (dE -2.87 dB, k 0.645) -- every dense shape C, every sparse shape B or A. Ground truth agrees across the two sets to ~0.2 dB on the three shared rooms and receiver placement is statistically matched (median wall distance 0.86 vs 0.84 m), so the difference is in the PREDICTION: the same model, same room, predicts 2.5-4 dB less 100-300 Hz energy at the 800-receiver positions. (b) **Why those positions are special.** `build_p4_2_shapes.receivers` draws every room's 800 receivers -- training AND test -- from ONE absolute lattice, x, y = 0.12 + 0.06 k, and the hash encoding is indexed by absolute position (metres / 10). The same lattice points are therefore ray origins in all 250 training rooms. (c) **The controlled test, no new simulation.** Snapped to the 0.02 m FDTD nodes, ~1 in 9 points of the dense test lattice falls back on that lattice. Same rooms, same FDTD run, same density, split only by lattice membership (`scripts/p4_5_lattice_diag.py`, C250 @ 60K, 3 frozen test shapes): spatial Pearson ON the lattice **+0.765 / +0.427 / +0.320** (25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz) against OFF it **+0.495 / +0.136 / +0.100**; band LSD 0-100 Hz **2.66 dB on vs 3.89 dB off**. (d) **It is memorisation, not physics.** Binned by distance to the nearest lattice point, the advantage is a SPIKE: r at 25-100 Hz is 0.765 at distance 0 and **0.498 at 2 cm** -- the same as at 2.8 cm (0.492) and everywhere else off it. 2 cm is 1/57 of a wavelength at 300 Hz; no acoustic quantity changes over it. The fine hash levels (cells 1.7 cm and below from level 5) have learned values specific to the exact receiver coordinates. (e) **It reframes D98.** ON the lattice the model under-predicts high-frequency energy and washes out contrast (200-300 Hz: dE -3.63 dB, k 0.59) while staying partly correlated -- the degenerate smooth solution the manager hypothesised, but only THERE. OFF the lattice it gets energy and contrast right (dE -0.76 dB, k 1.00) and the pattern almost entirely wrong (r 0.10) -- texture with the right statistics, not a smoothed or resolution-limited approximation of the field. Neither B1's lens (energy) nor B2's (resolution) is the whole story. (f) **It reframes P4-4 demo v2 §5.3 and the manager's item 3.** The "+0.22 / +0.28 sparse-sampling flattery" is NOT a density effect: dense points ON the lattice score like the sparse set. The 800-receiver sets are evaluated at memorised positions. Gate 2, the P4-2/P4-3/P4-4 sweep curves and the family eval all measure the model where it was trained to be right, and validation (`val_rx_indices`, held-out points of the same lattice) never tested anywhere else. (g) **Consequence for this chunk's arms.** B4's extra receivers come from the SAME 0.06 m candidate grid -- it densifies the memorised lattice rather than breaking it, so it can improve every 800-receiver number without improving the field. That is a pre-registered false-positive risk. Every arm evaluation now reports on-lattice and off-lattice separately (`p4_5_eval_ckpt.sh` runs this diagnostic on every checkpoint), and **the off-lattice dense number is the honest measure of a continuous field**. (h) **Scope of the parity claim.** Shape at 0-100 Hz is 2.32 dB on the 800-receiver set against 1.7-2.3 dB for the material work -- parity, but AT TRAINING POSITIONS; off them it is 3.89 dB. The material work was most likely also measured at its own training positions and has never been measured off them, so the like-for-like statement is "parity below 100 Hz at training positions", not "parity as a continuous field". (i) **The fix class this points to** is breaking the lattice -- receivers placed per room, off any shared grid, so no absolute position is privileged -- or an encoder that cannot resolve the 6 cm receiver spacing. Neither is in P4-5's arm list; raised as OPEN_QUESTIONS Q22.

**D100 — B6, off-lattice receivers: the manager chose "both, B6 first" on Q22.** (a) **Construction.** `build_p4_5_rooms.py --mode offlat`: for each of the 250 training rooms, 800 receivers drawn per room, uniformly, from EVERY in-room 0.02 m FDTD node, with the corpus builder's own 0.12 m wall and 0.15 m reflex-corner clearance, seeded [20261014, shape_id]. Node pools are 41k-61k per room (vs 4.6k-9.2k on the 0.06 m lattice). Measured: a median **11.3%** of each room's receivers land on the old lattice (range 7.4-14.9% over 250 rooms), against 1/9 = 11.1% for a uniform draw over nodes -- so the old lattice is neither privileged nor excluded. A given absolute position is now a receiver in ~3 training rooms rather than ~30. (b) **Held fixed.** 800 receivers, so the trainer's stacking, its 40 x 100 validation set and its per-iteration cost match the baseline exactly; same architecture, loss, iterations, seed. Validation receivers (`range(3, 800, 8)`) are now off-lattice too, so B6's own val curve measures generalisation off the lattice for the first time. (c) **Prediction if D99 is right**: B6's off-lattice dense curve rises toward C250's on-lattice curve and the on/off gap closes; its 800-receiver Gate-2 number may FALL, because the frozen test set's receivers sit on a lattice B6 no longer privileges. A fall there with a rise off-lattice would be the arm working, not failing, and is pre-registered as such. (d) **Scheduling.** Corpus built on scavenger as a 50-task array; training chained with `afterok` so it starts only if all 250 rooms build; it runs on scavenger with `--requeue` because tron's four GPUs are held by B1-B4 until ~09:40.

**D101 — B5, near-DC compliance subtraction, as a learned head added INSIDE the renderer.** (a) **What the term is, measured on the 250 training rooms.** Spatially uniform below 10 Hz (|spatial mean|^2 / mean|H|^2 = 1.000 at 0-5 Hz, 0.963 at 10 Hz, 0.65 at 15 Hz, 0.11 at 20 Hz); |H(0)| scales as 1/area (corr 0.936; |H(0)| x A has 7.8% spread); its decay tracks perimeter/area (corr 0.976). And **0-10 Hz holds 99.9% of all 0-300 Hz energy in the corpus.** (b) **Why not "an analytic function of room area".** A global first-order model K / (A (g + j 2 pi f)), g = c1 P/A + c0, fitted over 250 rooms, removed **0.5%** of the 0-10 Hz energy and made 10-20 Hz worse. The spatial mean's phase rotates through more than a full turn by 10 Hz and its magnitude falls roughly as f^-1.5: the spectrum of a slowly relaxing pressure step truncated by the 2 s record, shaped by the window rather than by a single room pole. An analytic form would have to model the truncation exactly; the manager's alternative, a small head, does not. (c) **Design.** `INR2D_AutoDecoder(dc_head_bins=41)` adds an MLP (raw 324-d conditioning -> 128 -> 128 -> 82) producing one complex value per room per bin for bins 0-40 (0-20 Hz), multiplied by a per-bin buffer `dc_scale` = corpus RMS of the spatial-mean target (1213.4 at DC, 0.517 at 20 Hz) so O(1) weights reach |H| ~ 1200. **Added inside `FreqRenderer2D.forward`**, so every evaluation path -- trainer, Gate 2, demo renders, the P4-5 evaluators -- includes it and none can silently drop it (the D73 hazard, closed by construction rather than by care). Built after every other layer and zero-initialised, so a model without it builds and renders bit-identically and **B5 at iteration 0 IS the baseline**. `load_model` passes `dc_head_bins` from the checkpoint explicitly. (d) **Why this is not D70.** D70 masked the band out of the loss; the prediction there was then unconstrained and the RIR collapsed (0.9989 -> 0.0630). B5 keeps every bin in the loss and PREDICTS the DC content -- just from a component built to represent it, so the field network's Jacobian no longer has to carry it. (e) **Verified.** Three behavioural tests (shared weights identical and render bit-identical at init; the head applied by the renderer, uniform in space and confined to its bins -- seen to fire with a nonzero head; a B5 checkpoint reloads through `load_model` with its head and scale and reproduces its render exactly), and the pre-change render still reproduces at 0.000e+00 with the head code in place; 41 tests pass. **First evidence it works**: B5's loss at iteration 200 is 1.44 against ~3.7 for B6 and the baseline at the same point -- the head absorbed the DC term within 200 steps. (f) **Prediction.** A1's 93% concentration in 0-10 Hz should collapse in B5 and the high-frequency tail should gain gradient share; whether that lifts high-frequency ACCURACY is the open question, since A1 also found that gradient largely orthogonal to the step, and D99's lattice memorisation is untouched by this arm.

**D102 — CORRECTION TO THE RECORD: Gate 2 re-scored at dense receivers. Four of the five published Gate-2 passes do not survive, and the memorisation comes from ATTENTION POOLING.** Requested by the manager as a correction, not an extra. (a) **Method, and why the numbers are authoritative.** The 15 frozen test shapes were re-simulated on a 64x64 lattice masked to the polygon (`data/track_p4_5_testdense`, same geometry, same solver, dx 0.02), and scored by `scripts/p4_5_gate2_dense.py`, which runs the frozen evaluator's render loop and metrics verbatim and splits receivers into on-lattice (x, y = 0.12 + 0.06 k: where every 800-receiver number was measured) and off-lattice (the model as a continuous field). Two exactness checks, both passed on ALL 14 fields: on the original 800-receiver data it reproduces C250's published GATE2.json to the last digit, including the pooled NLOS counts 354 and 792; on the dense data its "all" subset reproduces the frozen evaluator itself, run unchanged with `--data-dir data/track_p4_5_testdense`, which exits 2 -- its own FAIL verdict -- for C250. (b) **In-slab spatial Pearson (threshold 0.80):**

| checkpoint | pooling | corpus | published | dense on-lattice | dense all | **dense off-lattice** | published | **dense** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P4-3 D | masked_mean | 92 | 0.8021 | 0.8146 | 0.8066 | **0.8063** | PASS | **PASS** |
| P4-3 X1 | attn_residual | 60 | 0.8438 | 0.8430 | 0.7686 | **0.7581** | PASS | **FAIL** |
| P4-4 C92 | attn_residual_extent | 92 | 0.8337 | 0.8306 | 0.7638 | **0.7545** | PASS | **FAIL** |
| P4-4 C250 | attn_residual_extent | 250 | 0.8420 | 0.8412 | 0.6368 | **0.6109** | PASS | **FAIL** |
| P4-4 C500 | attn_residual_extent | 500 | 0.8336 | 0.8341 | 0.5951 | **0.5641** | PASS | **FAIL** |
| P4-3 X2 | attn | 60 | 0.7789 | 0.7808 | 0.6765 | 0.6627 | FAIL | FAIL |
| P4-3 S | masked_mean (+sigma aux) | 60 | 0.7329 | 0.7324 | 0.7336 | 0.7338 | FAIL | FAIL |

Out-of-slab moves the same way (C250 0.8359 -> 0.6087 off-lattice; D 0.7641 -> 0.7651). The NLOS-deficit criterion still passes everywhere; it is the in-slab Pearson that fails. (c) **The pattern is pooling.** The two masked_mean arms show NO on/off-lattice gap (D 0.8146 vs 0.8063; S 0.7324 vs 0.7338); all five attention arms lose 0.08-0.27. **D and C92 were trained on the IDENTICAL 92-shape corpus (manifest sha d9a00aba16) and differ ONLY in pooling**: on-lattice C92 leads (0.8306 vs 0.8146), off-lattice D leads (**0.8063 vs 0.7545**). Attention pooling buys accuracy at the memorised positions and loses it everywhere else. The mechanism fits: attention pooling is the one conditioning path that varies with the QUERY POSITION (`test_attention_conditioning_actually_varies_with_position`), which gives it a channel to store position-specific values; masked-mean pooling hands every point in a room the same vector. (d) **What this overturns or qualifies, entry by entry.** **D81** ("attn_residual_extent beats masked_mean by +0.0316 in-slab on an identical corpus") -- the sign REVERSES to **-0.052** as a field; D81's own unexplained puzzle, that the gated metric and every other metric disagreed in sign, is explained: the gated metric was the one measured at memorised positions. **D80** ("the scaling ramp saturates": 0.8337 / 0.8420 / 0.8336) -- as a field it DECLINES monotonically with corpus size, **0.7545 / 0.6109 / 0.5641**; the mechanism is not established (more rooms make each lattice point a receiver in more of them, which is consistent but untested). **P4-3's headline** ("Gate 2 PASSES on two arms, X1 0.8438 and D 0.8021") -- only D passes. **P4-4's headline** ("all three P4-4 arms PASS Gate 2 where all four P4-2 arms failed") -- all three FAIL as fields. **D75** (Arm D, the data lever, passes Gate 2) -- STANDS, and is strengthened: D is the only arm whose pass survives. **D82** (targets 0.88 / 3.0 dB missed) -- missed by more. **D83/D84 and the family eval** used the same 0.06 m receiver lattice and an attention arm (FAM, attn_residual_extent) and are therefore likely inflated; not re-scored, because no dense family test set exists -- flagged, not corrected. **P4-2's four arms** used mean-type pooling and their checkpoints are no longer on disk; by (c) they are probably not inflated, but that is inference, not measurement. (e) **Consequence for P4-5.** The chunk's spec fixed the conditioning to attn_residual_extent and the baseline to C250, which is among the strongest memorisers measured (0.8412 -> 0.6109). Every P4-5 arm inherits that channel; their off-lattice numbers are therefore the ones that matter, and the standing alternative -- masked_mean pooling, i.e. Arm D's recipe -- is the strongest field model the project has. (f) **Standing rule.** **An INR's accuracy must be measured at positions it was not trained at.** Validation receivers drawn from the training receivers' own lattice, as every P4 validation set was, cannot detect memorisation of that lattice, and the project ran four chunks without noticing.

**D103 — B4 ran out of GPU memory at iteration ~5000; resumed from 4K on an RTX A6000. Two separate causes, and the second was self-inflicted.** (a) **The real cause.** The trainer preloads every target onto the GPU. B4's 4x receivers make that **3.85 GB instead of 0.96 GB**, and the 8-row renderer graph already uses ~20 GB of a 24 GB A5000 -- the baseline fitted with ~2 GB to spare and B4 did not. It ran 5000 iterations before a large enough batch crossed the line (PyTorch OOM: 18.2 GB allocated, 21.6 GB reserved). This was foreseeable from D93's own numbers and was missed when B4 was set up. No other arm is at risk: all preload the baseline's 0.96 GB, which ran 60K iterations on this GPU. (b) **The fix.** Resume from `ckpt_iter0004000.pt` -- the newest, and the only one still carrying Adam state under checkpoint thinning -- on a 48 GB RTX A6000 (`--gres=gpu:rtxa6000:1`). ~1000 iterations lost; the A6000 runs at 0.720 s/it against the A5000's 0.77, so B4 still reaches 60K inside the day. GPU type changes speed, not results. (c) **The self-inflicted failure.** The first resume also set `PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF=max_split_size_mb:128` "to limit fragmentation" -- and died at its FIRST backward, on a 48 GB card, inside tiny-cuda-nn's `cuMemCreate`. tiny-cuda-nn allocates through its own arena, outside PyTorch's caching allocator; `max_split_size_mb` stops PyTorch splitting large cached blocks, so its reservation grows and starves the second allocator. A1's full run died the same way, under the same setting, until `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` was added. Relaunched with the default allocator, B4 resumed cleanly. **Standing rule: do not set `max_split_size_mb` for training jobs with tiny-cuda-nn models.** The P4-5 evaluation launchers still set it and have run cleanly under no_grad, so they were left unchanged mid-run rather than edited.

**D104 — ON AN IDENTICAL CORPUS, MEAN POOLING GIVES A 2.6-3.5x BETTER HIGH-FREQUENCY FIELD THAN ATTENTION POOLING. Much of the "high-frequency collapse" is memorisation.** The A2 evaluator and D99's lattice split, run on P4-3 D (masked_mean) and P4-4 C92 (attn_residual_extent) -- the same 92-shape corpus, manifest sha d9a00aba16, differing ONLY in pooling -- with C250 for reference. Dense, OFF-lattice, 3 frozen test shapes, spatial Pearson 25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz: **D +0.659 / +0.362 / +0.263**; C92 +0.572 / +0.111 / +0.076; C250 +0.495 / +0.136 / +0.100. (a) D shows **no on/off-lattice gap at ANY frequency** (on-lattice 0.668 / 0.393 / 0.287), confirming D102's pooling finding across the whole band, not only at Gate 2's 26-81 Hz. (b) D is not a degenerate field: energy within 1.6 dB (dE -0.74 / +0.24 / -1.55) and full spatial contrast (k 0.93 in every band) WITH real correlation -- a partial approximation of the true pattern, unlike the attention arms' off-lattice texture (k ~1, r ~0.1). (c) **What this does to the chunk's question.** The pre-registered hypothesis (D92) located the high-frequency deficit in loss weighting; A1 (D97) moved it to a gradient concentrated at DC and orthogonal at high frequency; this locates a LARGE share of it in the conditioning architecture, which the spec held fixed. The genuine difficulty remains -- even D falls from 0.66 to 0.26 across the band -- but attention pooling roughly triples it off the lattice. (d) **Consequence for the arms.** All seven P4-5 arms inherit attn_residual_extent. Their off-lattice numbers are measured against a baseline (C250, 100-200 Hz r 0.136) that a different pooling choice on fewer shapes already beats by 2.7x. The untested arm that would isolate this at the P4-5 corpus -- C250's recipe with masked_mean pooling -- is raised to the manager. **Launched as B7 on the manager's approval** (`configs/sweep_2d_mat/P4_5_B7.yaml`): C250's config with `token_pool: masked_mean` and nothing else model-related changed -- Arm D and C250 differ ONLY in pooling and corpus, so B7 is at once "C250 with mean pooling" and "Arm D's recipe at 250 shapes". Prediction from D104: B7 shows no on/off-lattice gap and beats C250 off-lattice at every frequency; whether 250 shapes help or hurt a NON-memorising arm (D80 measured the scaling curve only on attention arms) is what B7 vs D tells us.

**D105 — AMENDS D102: with 6 in-slab shapes Gate 2 cannot resolve +-0.04, so the verdicts are restated with bootstrap CIs. The robust findings are the SIZE of the shifts and the pooling contrast, not a count of verdicts.** Requested by the manager before any further claim. Across-test-shape bootstrap, stratified (in-slab mean over its 6 shapes, out-of-slab over its 9), percentile 95% CI, 10,000 resamples, seed 20261013 (`outputs/p4_5/gate2_dense/BOOTSTRAP_CI.json`). Rule, applied symmetrically: a CI that includes 0.80 reads AT THRESHOLD, whichever side the point estimate sits. (a) **Arm D's "surviving pass" is AT THRESHOLD**: dense off-lattice in-slab **0.8063 [0.7662, 0.8447]**, P(mean < 0.80) = 0.41; its six in-slab shapes span 0.736-0.857. Out-of-slab 0.7651 [0.6915, 0.8253]. (b) **X1 off-lattice is also AT THRESHOLD, not FAIL** as D102 stated: 0.7581 [0.7149, 0.8107]. C92 (0.7545 [0.7097, 0.7952]), C250, C500, X2 and S FAIL cleanly. (c) **The published verdicts were mostly unresolvable too.** Same bootstrap on the published 800-receiver numbers: C250 0.8420 [0.8042, 0.8775] is the ONLY statistically clear PASS; D, X1, C92, C500 and X2 were all AT THRESHOLD; S a clear FAIL. D102's "four of five published passes do not survive" therefore overstated what the gate could ever resolve. **Restated**: published -- 1 clear PASS, 5 at threshold, 1 FAIL; dense off-lattice -- 2 at threshold (D, X1), 5 clear FAIL. (d) **What IS robust.** The shifts: C250 0.842 -> 0.611, with NON-overlapping CIs ([0.804, 0.878] vs [0.580, 0.656]); C500 0.834 -> 0.564. And the pooling contrast, now tested on ALL 15 frozen shapes with a paired bootstrap (D104 used 3): off-lattice dense, D minus C92 on the identical corpus = **+0.122 [+0.092, +0.148] at 25-100 Hz (D better in 14/15 shapes), +0.324 [+0.279, +0.365] at 100-200 Hz (15/15), +0.237 [+0.210, +0.265] at 200-300 Hz (15/15)** (`POOLING_PAIRED_BOOTSTRAP.json`). The gate verdicts are noise-limited; the mechanism is not. (e) **Standing consequence.** A 0.80 threshold on a 6-shape mean is not a decision instrument at this margin; any future gate needs either more in-slab shapes or a CI-based criterion, and every gate number carries its CI.

**D106 — D99(h) SETTLED FROM CODE: the material work measured fields at untrained positions, so "shape at parity with material below 100 Hz" is INVERTED, not merely scoped.** Asked by the manager to be settled by reading code, not inference. **Fact:** the material builders place receivers PER ROOM -- an 8x8 `np.linspace(margin, L - margin, 8) x np.linspace(margin, W - margin, 8)` grid scaled to each room's own L and W, margin 0.3 m -- `scripts/build_2d_mat_dataset.py:39-42` (P3-2/P3-2b, `data/track_c_2d`) and `scripts/build_p3_3fast_trackA.py:32-35` (P3-3fast, `data/track_p3_3fast_A`). There is no shared absolute lattice. Checked in the data, not only the code: test geometries are disjoint from training geometries (0 of 10 shared, both corpora), and only **6.1% (P3-2b, 38 of 628) and 3.7% (P3-3fast, 23 of 627)** of test receiver positions coincide exactly with any training receiver, incidentally. **So the material work's 1.7-2.3 dB was (to within ~5%) a field measurement, while shape's 2.32 dB below 100 Hz was measured at memorised positions.** As a continuous field, shape below 100 Hz is **3.41 dB (Arm D, mean pooling) to 3.89 dB (C250)** (3 frozen shapes, dense, off-lattice). The like-for-like statement is: **shape editing is NOT at parity with material editing below 100 Hz; the earlier apparent parity was shape measured favourably against material measured honestly.** Caveat, stated: the two LSD figures are not on identical bands or corpora, so the comparison is approximate in magnitude, but its direction is not in doubt. The scope sentence the manager asked to lead the results doc is rewritten accordingly.

**D107 — PRE-REGISTRATION, k-space diagnostic, committed before it was computed.** The manager's reading of D99's off-lattice signature (dE -0.76 dB, contrast k 1.00, r 0.10): spatial NOISE at correct amplitude. A physical field at frequency f cannot carry structure much finer than its wavelength (at 100 Hz, lambda = 3.43 m against the 0.06 m receiver spacing), so noise of that kind should show up as energy at wavenumbers the Helmholtz equation forbids. **Quantity.** The COMPLEX predicted and FDTD fields on the dense 64x64 test lattice (zero outside the room mask, zero-padded 2x), 2D FFT per 0.5 Hz bin; radial wavenumber |k|; **supra-physical fraction S = energy at |k| > 1.5 k0 / total energy**, k0 = 2 pi f / c. Complex, not dB: Helmholtz constrains the complex pressure, while a log-magnitude field acquires sharp features at its nulls that would read as supra-physical even when the physics is right. **The GT field's own S measures the mask's spectral leakage; the metric is the EXCESS, S(pred) - S(GT).** On/off-lattice split: the supra-physical component in real space, R = IFFT(F x 1[|k| > 1.5 k0]), as a fraction of field energy at on-lattice vs off-lattice grid points. **Scored band 100-300 Hz.** Below ~100 Hz the room's k-space resolution (~2 pi / 6 m ~ 1 rad/m) is comparable to 1.5 k0, so S there is resolution-limited and reported but not scored. **Models:** C250 (attention), C92 (attention, same corpus as D), Arm D (mean pooling); 3 frozen dense shapes. **Prediction (manager's), operationalised now:** HOLDS if, over 100-300 Hz, C250's excess S >= 0.10 AND Arm D's excess <= 0.5 x C250's. **If it holds**, the supra-physical fraction becomes a standing metric -- it names the defect physically and points at a loss term (a Helmholtz residual at off-lattice points, or a k-space band-limit penalty). **If it does not** -- if the off-lattice error is spectrally physical and merely misplaced -- no band-limit loss will help, and a day is not spent on one. Diagnostic only; no training arm until B6 reports.

**D108 — D107 RESULT: the prediction HOLDS. The attention arms' high-frequency fields are not physical fields in the wrong place -- they lack the Helmholtz ring entirely. Supra-physical fraction becomes a STANDING METRIC.** (a) **Scored** (excess supra-physical fraction, prediction minus FDTD, 100-300 Hz): **C250 +0.272**, C92 +0.39 (+0.298 at 100-200, +0.482 at 200-300), **Arm D +0.023** -- at the FDTD's own leakage floor (-0.005 at 100-200 Hz). Criterion (C250 >= 0.10 and D <= 0.5 x C250): **HOLDS**. (b) **The radial spectrum is the stronger result.** The FDTD field's 100-300 Hz energy peaks sharply at |k| = k0 -- the Helmholtz ring -- and falls steeply beyond 1.5 k0. **Arm D reproduces the ring almost exactly. C250 and C92 have NO ring**: their energy piles up at |k| < 0.7 k0 (too-smooth large-scale structure) and sits on a near-flat floor of ~1% per bin out to 6 k0. A physical field at the wrong position would keep the ring; these fields do not have it. (c) **Where the forbidden energy sits** (real-space high-pass, fraction of field energy, 100-300 Hz): C250 **1.50 at on-lattice points** against a physical floor of 0.14 (~10x), and 0.35 off-lattice against 0.10 (~3.5x); Arm D 0.16 / 0.14, at the floor. So the defect is not only diffuse noise off the lattice: the memorised lattice-point values DISAGREE with the field around them, and those point-like discontinuities carry the forbidden wavenumbers, with a smaller elevated floor everywhere else. This refines the manager's "spatial noise at correct amplitude" reading. (d) **Consequence.** A physics penalty IS aimed at the right defect: a k-space band-limit penalty, or a Helmholtz residual evaluated at off-lattice points, would penalise exactly what the attention arms carry and Arm D does not. Not launched -- per the manager, no training arm on this until B6 reports. (e) **Standing metric**, reported for every P4-5 checkpoint from here on: **excess supra-physical fraction, |k| > 1.5 k0, complex field, dense test lattice, 100-300 Hz**, with the radial spectrum. It names the defect physically and needs no reference to the receiver lattice, so it would also catch memorisation of any OTHER sampling pattern. Implementation `scripts/p4_5_kspace_diag.py`; output `outputs/p4_5/diagnostics/kspace_diag.{json,png}`. (f) **Added 2026-10-14 at the manager's request: (c) supersedes the D107 reading.** "Spatial noise at correct amplitude" is retired. The defect is **point-like discontinuities AT the lattice points, carrying forbidden wavenumbers at ~10x the physical floor (1.50 vs 0.14), plus an elevated floor everywhere else (0.35 vs 0.10, ~3.5x)**. Use that description from here on. (g) **Added 2026-10-14: the supra-physical fraction S and off-lattice Pearson are NOT redundant, and neither is a proxy for the other. Do not substitute one for the other.** The headline pairing disagrees in rank. Off-lattice dense Gate-2 Pearson (mode bins, ~26-81 Hz) ranks C92 BETTER than C250 (0.7545 vs 0.6109). S (100-300 Hz) ranks C92 WORSE (excess +0.390 vs +0.272; +0.298 vs +0.172 at 100-200 Hz). Measured precisely, the disagreement is partly a band difference. At MATCHED band (100-300 Hz) off-lattice Pearson also ranks C92 slightly worse (0.111 vs 0.136 at 100-200, 0.076 vs 0.100 at 200-300; 3 shapes, small margins), but not in proportion: S differs 1.7x where Pearson differs by 0.025. The real-space split separates them further: C92 carries LESS forbidden energy than C250 at lattice points (0.98 vs 1.50) and MORE off them (0.50 vs 0.35). The decisive case is Arm D. S sits at the physical floor (+0.023), yet off-lattice Pearson at 100-200 Hz is only 0.36. A field can be physical and still wrong. **S measures physicality, not accuracy; Pearson measures accuracy, not physicality.** Report both.

**D109 — D80's reversal TESTED: the per-point receiver count does NOT explain it. The corpus-size decline stays OBSERVED WITHOUT MECHANISM and must not reach the deck as "more data hurts".** Asked by the manager: test the offered mechanism (a lattice point is a receiver in more training rooms as the corpus grows) or hedge it. (a) **Why three points cannot test it.** Across C92/C250/C500 the mean count of trained receivers at a test lattice point is 8.2 / 22.4 / 44.8, and the dense Gate-2 off-lattice penalty (on − off, D102) is +0.076 / +0.230 / +0.270 in-slab (+0.069 / +0.222 / +0.242 out-of-slab). That is monotone, but the count is collinear with corpus size, so the three points fit ANY corpus-size cause equally well. (b) **The within-corpus test.** Room edges lie inside fewer training rooms than centres, so the count varies WITHIN a corpus. Location is a confound, so each on-lattice point of the 15 dense test shapes (4098 points) is compared with its own four axis neighbours 8 cm away, all off the lattice (λ/14 at 300 Hz). **Memorisation advantage** = the neighbours' mean |dB error| minus the point's own. Two metrics: the six Gate-2 mode bins, and 100-200 Hz. Counts exclude held-out val receivers. (c) **Result 1: no within-corpus dose-response.** Spearman(count, advantage) is +0.04 / +0.04 / +0.13 on the mode bins and +0.08 / +0.10 / +0.11 at 100-200 Hz. (d) **Result 2, the decisive one.** At MATCHED counts (10-20 rooms, the window all three corpora share), the mode-bin advantage is **C92 +0.45 dB [+0.35, +0.54] against C250 +1.52 dB [+1.42, +1.66]** (95% CIs from a 10k bootstrap over the 15 shapes). The CIs are disjoint: at the same count, C250 carries ~3.4x C92's advantage. C500 is +1.06 [+0.80, +1.47], but only 189 points, all at edges. At 100-200 Hz the matched values are indistinguishable (+1.24 / +1.40 / +1.24 dB, overlapping CIs). (e) **The shape of the curves says the same thing.** Each corpus's within-corpus rise sits at its OWN lowest counts: below ~8 for C92, ~17 for C250, ~45 for C500. Those are each corpus's edge points, whatever their absolute count. That is a location effect, not a dose. (f) **Verdict.** The per-point-count mechanism is REFUTED for the Gate-2 metric. Memorisation strength is a corpus-level property of the trained network, not something each lattice point accumulates in proportion to its exposure. **The monotone decline of attention-pooling off-lattice accuracy with corpus size (C92 0.7545, C250 0.6109, C500 0.5641) is OBSERVED WITHOUT MECHANISM.** One candidate, untested and labelled as such: the lattice is a room-INDEPENDENT shortcut, and the harder the per-room signal is to fit, the more a shared position-indexed channel pays. **B7 vs D is the real test:** both use mean pooling, at 250 vs 92 shapes. If mean pooling does not decline from 92 to 250, the decline belongs to attention pooling, not to data. Until B7 reports, every statement of D80 carries this hedge. Script `scripts/p4_5_d80_mechanism.py`; output `outputs/p4_5/gate2_dense/D80_MECHANISM.{json,png}`. (g) **Added 2026-10-14 at the manager's request: the candidate in (f) is in TENSION with D111, and stays a candidate.** If the lattice pays more where the per-room signal is harder to fit, the hardest rooms should lose the most off the lattice. D111's five family shifts order the other way at the top: rect -0.282, DN -0.255, U -0.206, T -0.200, L -0.168. The rectangle has 0 reflex corners and was FAM's best published family, so it is the easiest room by any a-priori measure, yet it loses the most. With n = 5 and one room per family this settles nothing. There is also a mechanical coupling: the shift is published minus off-lattice, so the room with the highest published score has the most to lose. A second room per family is being added (D112), and the result will be appended as a new entry. Until then the candidate carries this tension wherever it is stated.

**D110 — FRAMING FOR THE PAPER (the manager's note, recorded as a contribution, not a caveat): a query-position-dependent conditioning path gives an INR a position-indexed storage channel, and on a fixed receiver lattice the network uses it for memorisation before it uses it for physics.** (a) **The claim.** Attention pooling (`attn_residual_extent`) was introduced to LOCALISE boundary effects: the query position selects which geometry tokens it attends to. That same dependence on query position is a channel through which the network can store values indexed by position. Trained on rooms whose receivers all sit on one absolute lattice, it used the channel to index lattice positions rather than to localise physics. Evidence: on vs off lattice 0.765 / 0.495 at 50 Hz (D99), with the gap opening at 0 cm and gone 2 cm away. The dense Gate-2 off-lattice penalty is +0.08 to +0.27 for attention arms and ~0 for mean pooling (D102/D105). Supra-physical energy at lattice points is 10x the physical floor, with no Helmholtz ring (D108). On an identical corpus, mean pooling is 2.6-3.5x better above 100 Hz (D104). (b) **The methodological point, which belongs in the paper.** The standard evaluation protocol in neural acoustic fields holds out positions drawn from the training measurement grid. That protocol CANNOT detect this failure: held-out lattice indices are still lattice points, and the memorised channel serves them. Every Gate-2 number, every family number (D83/D84) and every P4-4 headline in this repo was measured that way. Off the lattice no arm clears Gate 2: two are at threshold (D, X1) and five fail cleanly, among them C250, the only clear published PASS (0.842 [0.804, 0.878] -> 0.611 [0.580, 0.656], non-overlapping; D105). Rule 11 (CLAUDE.md) now forbids the protocol as a primary figure. (c) **What is and is not claimed.** Claimed: the channel exists, it is used for memorisation on a shared lattice, and the standard protocol is blind to it. Two candidate controls are under test, B6 (training receivers off the shared lattice) and B7 (mean pooling at 250 shapes); neither has reported. NOT claimed: that position-dependent conditioning is harmful in general, or that it cannot be used for physics when memorisation is unavailable. B6 decides that.

**D111 — FAMILY EVAL BOUNDED (manager item 3): off the lattice it moves by 0.22, not 0.02. D84's token cost partly survives as a field; D83's corner-count ordering is unsupported.** (a) **Design.** One held-out room per family: the median-d_hat test room (rectangles: median area), chosen by rule before any score was seen (`build_p4_5_rooms.family_test_rooms`). Each was re-simulated on a dense 64x64 lattice clipped to the polygon: 3637-4096 receivers, of which 395-440 fall on the 0.06 m training lattice. Scoring uses the family evaluator's OWN metric (`p4_4_family_eval._metrics`: 6 bounding-box modes, dB spatial Pearson) on checkpoint `p4_4_FAM` @ 60K. **Exactness:** the same code on the ORIGINAL 800 receivers reproduces all five published per-room values to |dr| <= 7e-10. That is not bitwise, because tcnn accumulation order differs across GPUs, so the check uses a tolerance of 1e-6, stated in the JSON. (b) **The numbers** (published 800-rx / dense on-lattice / dense OFF-lattice / shift): rect +0.8585 / +0.8817 / **+0.5765** / **-0.282**; L +0.7813 / +0.8039 / **+0.6133** / **-0.168**; T +0.7157 / +0.7175 / **+0.5154** / **-0.200**; DN +0.8486 / +0.8392 / **+0.5937** / **-0.255**; U +0.6913 / +0.6745 / **+0.4851** / **-0.206**. **Bound: mean -0.222, range [-0.282, -0.168], 5 of 5 rooms.** Dense ON-lattice points reproduce the published value within -0.017 to +0.023, so the whole shift is the lattice, not the denser sampling. The size matches C250's Gate-2 shift (0.842 -> 0.611, -0.231), as expected for the same pooling on the same lattice. (c) **D83 (corner count degrades monotonically) is UNSUPPORTED as a field.
