Not refuted: one room per family cannot rank families.** Off the lattice, the rectangle -- the best published family -- takes the largest hit and falls BELOW the L and DN rooms: 0 corners +0.577, 1 corner +0.613, 2 corners mean +0.531 (DN +0.594, T +0.515, U +0.485). U is still the lowest, consistent with demo v2's placement-and-depth finding. (d) **D84 (the token control).** Two of the five rooms are also token-control rooms, so the control re-scores directly (`FAMILY_TOKENS_DENSE.json`; published values reproduced to 7e-10). Token cost, published -> dense on-lattice -> **OFF-lattice**: rect 4->8 tokens -0.107 -> -0.103 -> **-0.038**; rect 4->12 -0.121 -> -0.128 -> **-0.035**; L 6->12 -0.304 -> -0.321 -> **-0.207**. Off the lattice the cost shrinks by about a third (L) to two-thirds (rect). Part of D84's token cost was therefore DISRUPTED MEMORISATION: re-tokenising changes the conditioning vector, and the position-indexed channel (D110) is keyed on that vector. But the L room still loses 0.21 as a field, so **tokenization fragility is REAL and smaller than D84 stated**. D84's headline comparison ("costs more than two reflex corners") is NOT established off the lattice: 2 rooms, no CI, and the corner side of the comparison is itself unsupported (c). For the Z-corridor, tokenization stays a live risk at reduced magnitude. (e) **The project saw this a chunk early and named the wrong cause.** Demo v2 (D85-D91) reported "sparse receiver sampling flatters the model by up to 0.22". Its "sparse" set was the 800 corpus receivers, which ARE the lattice; its "dense" 0.08 m grid lies mostly off it. Same checkpoint, same pooling, same magnitude (-0.04 to -0.22 there, -0.17 to -0.28 here). It is almost certainly this lattice memorisation, attributed to sampling density. Scripts `scripts/p4_5_family_dense.py`, `scripts/p4_5_family_tokens_dense.py`; outputs `outputs/p4_5/gate2_dense/FAMILY_DENSE_BOUND.json`, `FAMILY_TOKENS_DENSE.json`.

**D112 — PRE-REGISTRATION for the manager's items 1-3 (pooling on the family rooms, token invariance, the D109 tension), committed before any of them is computed.** (a) **Item 1: Arm D (masked_mean, `p4_3_D_data` @ 60K) on the dense family rooms, off the lattice, beside FAM.** The rooms, evaluator and split are D111's. Ten rooms, not five: D111's five median rooms plus a second median room per family (each family has 10 test rooms, so the sorted list has two medians; D111 took index 4, this takes index 5: rectL6.24_W4.36, LL5.64_W5.22_NWd1.28w1.10x0.00, TL5.20_W4.32_midNd0.92w0.76x2.20, DNL5.74_W4.38_NWd1.04w0.72x0.00_SEd0.28w1.18x0.00, UL5.66_W5.22_NEd1.84w0.60x0.00_NWd1.26w1.00x0.00). FAM is re-scored on all ten, with the 800-receiver values reproduced first as in D111. **CONFOUND, stated before the numbers.** Arm D was trained on 8 rectangles and 84 NW-notch L rooms (4 and 6 tokens), so rect and L are IN its training distribution. **T, DN and U are ZERO-SHOT for D**: 8-token polygons with two notches or a mid notch, none of which it has seen. FAM was trained on 60 rooms of each family. For T/DN/U the D-vs-FAM contrast therefore mixes pooling with training distribution, and it cannot say whether mean pooling "recovers the family numbers" there. It says whether a mean-pooled L-family model transfers. For rect/L it mixes pooling with corpus (92 L-family rooms vs 300 rooms over five families). **The clean test, a masked_mean arm trained on the family corpus, does not exist.** It is recommended, not launched: that is a GPU decision for the manager, and all four tron GPUs are in use. **Reading rule (per room):** recovery fraction rho = (D_off - FAM_off) / (FAM_published - FAM_off). rho >= 0.75 reads RECOVERED, rho <= 0.25 GONE (pooling does not restore the field), PARTIAL in between. Reported separately for in-distribution (rect, L) and zero-shot (T, DN, U) rooms. B7 (masked_mean, 250 L-family shapes) is added at its 20K checkpoint, with the same confound. (b) **Item 2: Arm D under re-tokenisation, off the lattice.** Comparisons: rect 4->8 and 4->12 tokens, L 6->12, on BOTH rect and BOTH L rooms (all four are D84 token-control rooms): 6 comparisons, with FAM's 6 beside them. **Manager's prediction:** D's token cost is within noise of zero, because a mean-pooled conditioning vector has no position-indexed channel whose stored values could break. **My a-priori note, which runs partly against it.** masked_mean is NOT structurally partition-invariant. Refining a wall into k collinear pieces divides every piece's EXTENT input by k and moves its centre, and mean(phi(pieces)) != phi(whole) for a nonlinear encoder phi. Arm D never saw a refined room. Uniform refinement does preserve each wall's share of the mean (every wall contributes k of the nk slots), so the only change is per-token content. Any invariance found is therefore LEARNED smoothness of phi, not a guarantee. **Criterion (fixed now): INVARIANT if all 6 of D's off-lattice costs satisfy |cost| <= 0.02.** The threshold sits below FAM's smallest off-lattice cost (0.035), so it separates the two by construction. Otherwise NOT INVARIANT, which by the manager's reading is a real representation limit. Every cost carries a paired receiver-bootstrap 95% CI (off-lattice receivers resampled jointly for both tokenisations, 2000 resamples). That CI measures receiver-sampling noise only, not seed variance, and spatially correlated receivers make it optimistic. (c) **Item 3: the D109(f) candidate against the family shifts.** Under the candidate, more geometric difficulty means a larger off-lattice loss for FAM. Test, on all ten rooms: Spearman(reflex-corner count, FAM shift); the candidate predicts NEGATIVE. Also rect vs the mean of the other families, in both rooms. Difficulty is measured a priori by reflex-corner count (D83's axis), never by a score from these rooms, to avoid the mechanical coupling noted in D109(g). n = 10, two rooms per family: a tension can be recorded, nothing confirmed. (d) **Item 4 needs no pre-registration** (it is the standing protocol), but its quantities are fixed here so an early read cannot be re-cut. **GAP** = on-lattice minus off-lattice dense Gate-2 Pearson, in-slab and out-of-slab, with a paired, slab-stratified bootstrap over the 15 shapes (10k, seed 20261013). Beside it: the off-lattice level with its CI, the 3-shape A2 on/off split per band, and the excess supra-physical fraction at 100-300 Hz. Reference gaps (in-slab): Arm D +0.008, C92 +0.076, C250 +0.230.

**D113 — D112 RESULTS. (1) On the families it was trained on, mean pooling RECOVERS the published family numbers as a field; on the other three it cannot be judged. (2) Arm D is NOT invariant under re-tokenisation: its token cost is 1.5-10x FAM's, the OPPOSITE of the prediction. (3) The D109(f) candidate's tension persists over ten rooms.** (a) **Exactness.** FAM's 800-receiver values were reproduced on all 10 rooms to |dr| <= 1.8e-9, and its dense values on D111's five rooms equal D111's exactly (0.0). (b) **Item 1, per room** (FAM published / FAM off-lattice / **Arm D off-lattice** / recovery rho): rect#1 0.859 / 0.576 / **0.807** / +0.82; rect#2 0.849 / 0.534 / **0.822** / +0.91; L#1 0.781 / 0.613 / **0.828** / +1.28; L#2 0.708 / 0.522 / **0.774** / +1.36; T#1 0.716 / 0.515 / **0.392** / -0.62; T#2 0.746 / 0.472 / **0.357** / -0.42; DN#1 0.849 / 0.594 / **0.468** / -0.49; DN#2 0.767 / 0.648 / **0.547** / -0.85; U#1 0.691 / 0.485 / **0.267** / -1.06; U#2 0.726 / 0.471 / **0.622** / +0.59. **In-distribution (rect, L): Arm D off-lattice +0.808 against FAM's published +0.799 and FAM's off-lattice +0.562: all four RECOVERED (mean rho +1.09).** A mean-pooled model trained on 92 L-family rooms reaches FAM's published rect/L numbers as a continuous field. It was trained on a different and smaller corpus, and FAM's own field is 0.25 below its published values. **Zero-shot (T, DN, U): Arm D +0.442 against FAM off-lattice +0.531: 5 GONE, 1 PARTIAL**, which is uninterpretable as a pooling result by D112(a), because Arm D has never seen these polygon types. **For the deck:** the family claim survives as a field for rect and L under mean pooling. For T, DN and U it is UNRESOLVED, not gone. The deciding experiment is a mean-pooled (or extent-pooled, D114) model trained on the family corpus. Arm D's own on-minus-off gap on these rooms is -0.007 to +0.029 (mean +0.010): no memorisation, as on the frozen shapes. **Updated family bound (D111, now 10 rooms):** FAM off-lattice minus published, mean **-0.226**, range [-0.315, -0.119], 10 of 10 rooms. (c) **Item 2: NOT INVARIANT, and decisively.** Off-lattice token cost, D vs FAM, each with a paired receiver-bootstrap CI of about +-0.01: rect#1 4->8 **-0.385** vs -0.038; rect#1 4->12 **-0.209** vs -0.035; rect#2 4->8 **-0.348** vs -0.070; rect#2 4->12 **-0.183** vs -0.064; L#1 6->12 **-0.321** vs -0.207; L#2 6->12 **-0.349** vs -0.107. All 6 of D's costs fail |cost| <= 0.02 by a factor of 9 or more. **The residual token fragility in D84 is not a memorisation artifact.** The pooling that does not memorise is MORE fragile under re-tokenisation, and the attention arm is the more robust one. By the manager's own reading this is "a real representation limit, and more interesting than D84 stated". **Interpretation, labelled as such and tested in D114.** Partition-consistency is a property of how the pooling WEIGHTS tokens, not of set encoding as such. masked_mean averages phi over tokens; refining a wall changes every piece's extent and centre, and mean(phi(pieces)) != phi(whole). FAM's attn_residual_extent base is an EXTENT-WEIGHTED SUM, sum_i e_i phi(t_i). That is a Riemann sum of a boundary integral: k pieces of extent e/k give sum (e/k) phi(piece) ~ e phi(whole) wherever phi varies slowly along the wall, so it is approximately partition-invariant by construction, as D112(b)'s a-priori note anticipated. Two confounds remain. Arm D saw only 4- and 6-token rooms (FAM saw 4, 6 and 8). And D's 4->8 cost is LARGER than its 4->12 cost, which this interpretation does not explain. (d) **Item 3.** Over ten rooms, Spearman(reflex-corner count, FAM shift) = **+0.33** (p 0.35). The candidate predicts negative. **The rectangle loses the most in BOTH rooms** (-0.282, -0.315; mean -0.299), against -0.208 for the other eight. The tension recorded in D109(g) holds at n = 10. The candidate is not supported, D80's decline stays observed-without-mechanism, and the candidate is not to be stated without this contrary trend beside it. Script `scripts/p4_5_family_pooling.py`; output `outputs/p4_5/gate2_dense/FAMILY_POOLING.json`.

**D114 — CORRECTION (mine): P4-2's four checkpoints ARE on disk. D102 and GATE2_DENSE_CORRECTION §5 said they were not, without checking. Plus a PRE-REGISTRATION of the test D113(c) calls for, committed before it runs.** (a) **The error.** D102 wrote that "P4-2's four arms used mean-type pooling and their checkpoints are no longer on disk; by (c) they are probably not inflated, but that is inference, not measurement." All four 60K checkpoints exist at `outputs/p4_2/stage2/p4_2_s2_{extent,mean}_{unmasked,masked}/ckpt_iter0060000.pt` (written 2026-09-12). The claim was asserted, never checked, and it left a measurable part of the record flagged as inference. **Remedy:** all four are re-scored now with the frozen protocol (`p4_5_gate2_dense.py`, `load_model` reading each arm's own `token_pool` per D73). The two DC-masked arms carry their RIR correlation beside every number (Rule 9). (b) **Why the unmasked pair matters beyond the record.** `p4_2_s2_extent_unmasked` (extent_sum) and `p4_2_s2_mean_unmasked` (masked_mean) were trained on the SAME corpus (`p4_2_shapes_manifest`) with the same recipe, and differ ONLY in token_pool. Neither has a query-position-dependent conditioning path. They are the clean contrast for D113(c)'s interpretation. **Predictions, fixed now:** (i) **no lattice memorisation in either**: dense Gate-2 on-minus-off gap, in-slab, < 0.03 for both; (ii) **extent-weighting is the partition-consistent pooling**: on the four rect/L family control rooms, extent_sum's off-lattice token cost is smaller in magnitude than masked_mean's in at least 5 of the 6 comparisons, AND its mean |cost| is at most half of masked_mean's. **If (i) and (ii) both hold**, extent-weighted pooling WITHOUT attention removes both defects this chunk found: lattice memorisation (D99) and tokenization fragility (D113(c)). It becomes the candidate conditioning for the family corpus and the Z-corridor. That is a recommendation for the manager, not a launch. **If (ii) fails**, D113(c)'s interpretation is wrong and is withdrawn. The family-room recovery numbers (D112(a)'s rule) are also reported for both arms, with D112(a)'s confound: their corpus is the L-family.

**D115 — D114 RESULT: both predictions FAIL. extent_sum MEMORISES the lattice, so D110's mechanism sentence is falsified as stated, and D113(c)'s extent-weighting interpretation is WITHDRAWN. The P4-2 record is corrected.** (a) **Exactness.** Both unmasked P4-2 arms, re-run by `p4_5_gate2_dense.py` on their original 800 receivers, reproduce their published GATE2.json on every field ("EXACT, every field"). extent_sum is therefore loaded AS extent_sum, the D73 hazard checked rather than assumed. (b) **P4-2's Gate 2, dense, OFF-lattice [95% CI], with on-minus-off gap (in-slab):** extent_unmasked published 0.7916 -> **0.6311 [0.569, 0.699] FAIL**, gap **+0.164 [+0.108, +0.215]**; mean_unmasked 0.7483 -> **0.7471 [0.718, 0.783] FAIL**, gap +0.007 [-0.004, +0.020]; extent_masked 0.7835 -> **0.7887 [0.746, 0.823] at threshold**, gap +0.003 (RIR 0.078, Rule 9); mean_masked 0.7978 -> **0.7982 [0.750, 0.838] at threshold**, gap -0.002 (RIR 0.063, Rule 9). P4-2's published verdict (all four FAIL) stands, but the extent arm's 0.79 was 0.63 as a field. This replaces GATE2_DENSE_CORRECTION §5's flag. (c) **Prediction (i) FAILS:** extent_sum, which has NO query-position-dependent conditioning path, memorises (+0.164). **D110's sentence "a query-position-dependent conditioning path gives an INR a position-indexed storage channel" is wrong as a mechanism.** The position-indexed channel is the HASH GRID, which every arm has (absolute position / 10, D99(b)). Pooling decides only whether the network USES it. The methodological half of D110 (the held-out-lattice protocol cannot detect this) is unaffected. **Amended framing for the paper:** "a position-indexed encoding is a storage channel; on a fixed receiver lattice, some conditioning schemes let the network use it for memorisation before physics; the standard held-out-grid protocol cannot see it." (d) **Prediction (ii) FAILS:** extent_sum's token cost beats masked_mean's in only 2 of 6 comparisons, with mean |cost| 0.221 against 0.181. Per D114, **D113(c)'s interpretation (extent-weighting is the partition-consistent pooling) is WITHDRAWN.** Every L-family model is fragile under re-tokenisation: mean |cost| D 0.299, P4-2 extent 0.221, P4-2 mean 0.181. Only FAM is robust (0.087). Of FAM's differences from them, extent-weighting alone is now excluded. What remains is either its ATTENTION delta, or its CORPUS (five families with 4-, 6- and 8-token rooms, against 4- and 6-token rooms only). That is untested and recorded as such. D84's tokenization fragility is therefore real, general across poolings, and absent only in the model trained on token-count diversity. (e) **P4-2 arms on the family rooms** (recovery rule D112(a), L-family corpus): extent_unmasked rect/L off-lattice +0.607 (mean rho +0.15); mean_unmasked +0.593 (+0.29). Both are below Arm D's +0.808. P4-2's corpus is smaller than D's (D is P4-2 plus more data, D75).

**D116 — PRE-REGISTRATION, committed before any 20K, 30K or 60K arm read and before the B1/B4 @10K reads are opened: lattice memorisation has appeared ONLY where the FIELD NETWORK fits the DC term AND pooling is extent-weighted or attention-based.** (a) **Every case measured** (dense Gate-2 in-slab on-minus-off gap). Both factors present: extent_unmasked +0.164, X1 +0.085, X2 +0.118, C92 +0.076, C250 +0.230, C500 +0.270, and FAM (D111/D113: rect on 0.882 vs off 0.576). DC fitted, mean pooling: D +0.008, P4-2 mean_unmasked +0.007, S +0.000 (masked_mean), B7 @6K +0.032. DC removed from the field network, extent/attention pooling: extent_masked +0.003 (DC-masked loss), B5 @6K/@10K -0.003/-0.004 (DC given to a separate head). Neither factor: mean_masked -0.002. **12 of 12 consistent; n is small in the off-diagonal cells, and B5 is only at 10K.** Mechanism NOT claimed. A plausible route, unmeasured: the DC term is ~1000x the rest in magnitude (D97), spatially uniform, and scales with 1/area; fitting it at fixed absolute positions with a room-dependent amplitude is the easiest thing for a position-indexed table plus a room code to store. (b) **Predictions for the running arms**, scored at 30K and at 60K on the dense in-slab gap with its paired CI. **P1: B5 gap < 0.03 at 30K AND 60K.** B5 is attention on the shared lattice, so pooling alone predicts a gap. **P2: B2, B3, B4 gap > 0.05 at 60K**: attention, DC fully in the field loss, on the lattice. **P3 (weaker): B1 and B1b, whose spec weights shrink 0-10 Hz (B1 mean 0.46, rising from 0.005 at DC; B1b 0.068 flat) while L_amp and L_phase still carry the DC, show gaps below the mean of B2/B3/B4 at 60K.** **Refutation:** if B5 opens a gap > 0.05 by 60K, the DC half is refuted. If B2/B3/B4 do NOT open > 0.05 by 60K, the pattern does not generalise beyond C250's exact recipe. Either outcome is reported against this entry. (c) **What it would mean if it holds.** B5 subtracts the DC rather than masking it, the manager's requirement, and keeps RIR (0.999 / 0.902 at 10K, against the masked arms' 0.06-0.08). It would then be the one intervention that removes the memorisation without giving up attention pooling or the time response.

**D117 — B7 @20K MEMORISES: mean pooling at 250 shapes on the shared lattice opens a +0.151 gap. This refutes the pooling half of D116's pattern and D104's "mean pooling does not memorise" beyond 92 shapes, and answers the manager's B7-vs-D test (preliminary, at 20K). A new prediction is registered before the 30K reads.** (a) **B7 @20K** (C250's recipe with token_pool masked_mean, i.e. Arm D's recipe at 250 shapes; D104): dense Gate-2 off-lattice in-slab **0.521 [0.488, 0.552]**, gap **+0.151 [+0.089, +0.209]** in-slab and +0.115 [+0.090, +0.137] out-of-slab. The gap was +0.032 at 6K, so it is growing. Distance profile 25-100 Hz: **0.552 at 0 cm against 0.417 at 2 cm**, the D99 spike. Excess supra-physical fraction **+0.165**; real-space forbidden energy 0.515 at lattice points against a physical floor of 0.14 (3.7x), 0.282 elsewhere against 0.10. A2 off-lattice 0.413 / 0.088 / 0.074 against on-lattice 0.552 / 0.319 / 0.278. Frozen 800-rx Gate-2 0.674, flattered relative to its own field. RIR 0.998 / 0.776. (b) **What it refutes.** D116(a)'s pattern ("memorisation only where the field fits DC AND pooling is extent/attention") is violated by its 13th case: mean pooling, DC fitted, and memorisation. **Pooling is not protective at 250 shapes.** D104/D105's pooling contrast was measured at 92 shapes and stands there, but it does not generalise as "mean pooling avoids memorisation". (c) **The manager's B7-vs-D test, preliminary at 20K.** B7 and D differ only in corpus (250 vs 92 L-family shapes). D shows no gap at 60K (+0.008); B7 shows +0.151 at 20K and rising. Going from 92 to 250 rooms on the SAME receiver lattice induces memorisation under mean pooling too, so **D80's decline is not attention-specific.** Stated precisely, it is NOT "more data hurts". B6 trains on the same 250 rooms with receivers OFF the shared lattice and has no gap (-0.005 at 20K). The claim is: **more rooms on a shared receiver lattice give the position-indexed shortcut more support, under either pooling.** Mechanism not established; the per-point count was already refuted within corpora (D109). Final at 30K / 60K. (d) **What does NOT memorise at 250 shapes on the lattice, through 20K:** B1 (1/RMS weights), B4 (4x receivers), B5 (DC head), all gaps <= +0.002. They share one measured property. Their training validation LSD is far below C250's at matched iterations (26K: B1 2.94, B1b 3.08, B4 3.86 dB; 20K: B5 3.31, against C250 4.96 at 26K). The arms that track C250's validation (B2 4.88, B6 4.99, B7 5.08 at 20-26K) are the slow fitters, and B7, the one of them on the lattice with no intervention, memorises. **New candidate, registered before the 30K reads: memorisation is what the network does when it is NOT fitting the field. Interventions that make it fit faster pre-empt it.** This is the D109(f) candidate restated at the level of the model rather than the room, and D113(d)'s rectangle result still counts against the room-level form. (e) **Predictions at 30K** (in-slab gap, paired CI; reads land ~03:45): **B2** (slow fitter, attention, no DC or supervision change) gap **> 0.05**. **B1, B1b, B4** (fast fitters) gap **< 0.03**. **B7** gap >= its 20K value. **B3** no prediction: its full-band validation is uninterpretable during the curriculum (9.4 dB with 200-300 Hz untrained until 35K). D116's P1-P3 (B5 < 0.03 at 30K and 60K; B2/B3/B4 > 0.05 at 60K; B1/B1b below them) stand as registered and are scored as written. Where they conflict with (e) (B4), the outcome will say which candidate failed.

**D118 — 30K reads for B1-B4, scored against D116 and D117 (B1b, B5, B6, B7 @30K pending).** Dense Gate-2 off-lattice in-slab [95% CI] / in-slab gap [CI] / A2 off-lattice r at 100 and 200 Hz (+-2.5 Hz) / excess supra-physical fraction at 100-300 Hz: **B1** 0.896 [0.881, 0.914] / -0.002 [-0.007, +0.002] / 0.682, 0.451 / +0.004. **B4** 0.921 [0.907, 0.932] / -0.001 [-0.004, +0.002] / 0.749, 0.414 / +0.023. **B2** 0.696 [0.658, 0.739] / **+0.083 [+0.059, +0.106]** / 0.210, 0.149 / +0.207 (distance spike 0.677 at 0 cm vs 0.603 at 2 cm). **B3** 0.671 [0.641, 0.695] / **+0.135 [+0.120, +0.148]** / 0.192, 0.062 / +0.042. B3's 200-300 Hz is untrained until 35K by design (off-lattice r -0.058, LSD 18.6 dB, modal RIR 0.29), so B3 is not judged before 60K. Baseline C250 @ 60K: 0.611 / +0.230 / 0.209, 0.257 / +0.272. (a) **D117(e), the slow-fitter candidate, passes 3 of 3 scored so far.** B2, whose training validation tracks C250's (4.73 vs C250's ~4.86 at 30K), opens a gap above 0.05 as predicted. B1 and B4, the fast fitters, stay below 0.03 as predicted. B1b and B7 are pending. B3 had no prediction; it memorises (+0.135), and its full-band validation (9.15 dB) puts it among the slow fitters, consistent with the candidate. (b) **D116.** P2 (B2/B3/B4 > 0.05 at 60K): B2 and B3 already exceed it at 30K; B4 is at -0.001, so P2 is on course to FAIL for B4, which is where D116 and D117 disagree. Scored at 60K as registered. P1 (B5) at 30K is pending. (c) **D95 (B2 null) holds so far.** B2's off-lattice tail at 100 Hz (0.210) equals the baseline's (0.209). Coarse-to-fine levels neither help the tail nor prevent memorisation. (d) **The tail, provisionally, before the 60K criterion read.** B1 leads at 200 Hz (0.451 against the baseline's 0.257) and B4 at 100 Hz (0.749 against 0.209). Both carry no lattice gap, forbidden-energy excess at the floor, and the best modal RIR measured (0.926, 0.918). The pre-registered "moved the tail" criterion is scored at 60K against C250 @ 60K.

**D119 — The manager's reading of B1, recorded; D110 AMENDED to a conditional (two necessary conditions, not sufficient); D104/D105 made conditional; B5 PRE-REGISTERED before its 30K read; two premises corrected; four arms launched.** (a) **The manager's verdict, recorded.** B1 @30K, off-lattice in-slab 0.896 [0.881, 0.914] with gap -0.002, is the project's first unambiguous Gate-2 pass as a field. Off-lattice r at 100 Hz goes from 0.209 (C250 @ 60K) to 0.682, which answers the question the chunk was launched to answer. **Mechanism, the manager's, read back from A1/D97:** the 0-10 Hz band carried ~93% of the net step and bins >= 100 Hz 0.9% of the projection. A loss that leaves the rest of the field without effective gradient lets the position-indexed channel substitute stored values for it. B1 is an attention arm with zero gap, which refutes the single-factor ("attention memorises") reading. (b) **D110 AMENDED (supersedes D110's mechanism sentence and D115(c)'s interim wording).** Two NECESSARY conditions are identified, and every memorising model so far has both. (1) A position-indexed encoding meeting a SHARED receiver lattice: B6, with the same 250 rooms and receivers off the lattice, does not memorise. (2) A DC-dominated gradient, i.e. the baseline loss: no arm that removes the DC from the field network's gradient memorises (B1, B1b, B5, extent_masked, mean_masked; the masked pair at 60K, the others through 20-30K). **They are NOT jointly sufficient.** Arms with both and no gap: Arm D (mean, 92 shapes, 60K), P4-2 mean_unmasked, P4-3 S (mean, P4-2 corpus), and B4 (attention, 4x receivers, through 30K). Pooling and corpus size modulate: mean pooling escapes at <= 92 shapes and not at 250 (B7). Denser supervision on the same lattice (B4) has escaped so far. The paper claim is therefore: "memorisation needs a position-indexed channel AND a loss that starves the field of gradient; given both, it appears in models that fit slowly (D117)." (c) **D104/D105 conditional.** The paired pooling bootstrap stands as measured: D minus C92, +0.12 / +0.32 / +0.24, 14-15 of 15 shapes. **What it licenses is narrowed.** It shows that, UNDER THE BASELINE LOSS and AT 92 SHAPES, attention memorised and mean pooling did not. It does not show that mean pooling is the better conditioning. That needs the comparison under a loss with no memorisation, which is B8 against B1 (item 6 below). (d) **PRE-REGISTRATION, B5, the manager's, committed before B5's 30K read.** B1 and B5 are the same intervention by two routes: B1 removes the DC from the gradient, B5 moves the DC into its own head. **Prediction: at 30K and at 60K, B5's in-slab gap is <= 0.03, AND its off-lattice r at 100 Hz (+-2.5 Hz, 3 dense shapes) rises substantially over C250 @ 60K (0.209); "substantially" is fixed now as >= 0.409, i.e. +0.20.** If both hold, the mechanism is DC gradient dominance, confirmed by two independent interventions. If B5's gap is large, the mechanism is spectral reweighting specifically and the DC account is wrong. **Disclosed, so the test is not overstated:** B5 already met both conditions at 6K, 10K and 20K (gap -0.003 / -0.004 / -0.002; r at 100 Hz 0.658 at 10K, 0.703 at 20K). So the 30K and 60K reads test whether it HOLDS through the training phase in which B2, B3 and B7 opened their gaps. They do not test whether it appears. This coincides with D116's P1. (e) **Premise corrected: B1's weight ratio is NOT "almost entirely bin 0".** B1's vector ramps across the whole near-DC band: 0.0045 (0 Hz), 0.014 (0.5 Hz), 0.031 (1 Hz), 0.079 (2 Hz), 0.354 (5 Hz), 1.28 (10 Hz). Excluding bin 0 its max/min ratio is still **1011**, and across 10-250 Hz the span is 12.8x. That matches D101, where the DC compliance term is spatially uniform across 0-10 Hz, not only at bin 0. **Consequence for the manager's ablation (B9: uniform weights, bin 0 alone at 0.0045):** it suppresses one bin of the near-DC band. Manager's prediction: B9 matches B1 within noise. **My a-priori note, against it:** bins 0.5-10 Hz carry target RMS 4-390 against ~0.5 above 100 Hz, and under L1 the gradient concentration lives in the Jacobian, which scales with output magnitude (D97(e)). So B9 should recover only part of B1. To make a mismatch interpretable, **B9b** is added: B1's weights on 0-10 Hz only (bins 0-20, normalised to 1 at 10 Hz), 1 above. B1 factorises as (near-DC suppression, B9b) x (10-300 Hz tilt). If B9 ~ B1: one weight fixes it. If B9b ~ B1 > B9: the near-DC BAND is the cause. If B1 > B9b: the tilt does real work. Neither B9 nor B9b is rescaled (D96's scaling rule is not applied), so "nothing else changed" holds literally for B9. Weights `outputs/p4_5/arms/weights/B9_bin0_weights.npy` (sha256 d79c333e993c...) and `B9b_dcband_weights.npy` (8a0696b2f4bb...), float32, matching B1's file. (f) **Premise corrected: C250 kept ONLY its 60K checkpoint** (`ckpt_keep_last: 3` and nothing else retained; directory listing: `ckpt_iter0060000.pt`). Item 3 cannot be done from existing checkpoints. **C250r** is launched instead: C250's config with only run_id and the P4-5 checkpoint-retention settings changed (`ckpt_every` was already 2000; early stopping never fired for C250, so raising its warmup is behaviour-neutral). tiny-cuda-nn is not bit-deterministic, so C250r is a replica, not a copy. **It is VALIDATED by its 60K endpoint against C250's published 60K dense numbers** (off-lattice in-slab 0.611 [0.580, 0.656], gap +0.230) before its 30K read stands in for C250's. Interim evidence that memorisation grows with training, from arms already on disk: B7 +0.032 at 6K and +0.151 at 20K. (g) **Arms launched (scavenger, requeue, 60K, every 2K checkpoint retained):** **B8** = B7 + B1's weights, the fourth cell of pooling x loss (manager's item 4). **B9** = the manager's bin-0 ablation. **B9b** = the near-DC-band ablation from (e). **C250r** = (f). Configs `configs/sweep_2d_mat/P4_5_{B8,B9,B9b,C250r}.yaml`, each generated from its parent with the diff stated in its header.

**D120 — Manager items 2, 1, 5 (in the order the manager ranked the risk): B1's time response is INTACT; below 100 Hz B1 is at material parity AS A FIELD; the slow/fast split holds at matched iterations.** `scripts/p4_5_risk_metrics.py`, all 15 frozen test shapes at dense receivers, OFF-lattice only, 10k bootstrap over shapes; output `outputs/p4_5/gate2_dense/RISK_METRICS.json`. (a) **Item 2, the pre-registered risk metric (D96), reported before B1 enters any headline.** Full-band RIR Pearson is the irfft of all 601 bins, DC included, per off-lattice receiver, averaged. **B1 @30K 0.9997 [0.9996, 0.9998]; B1b @30K 0.9996 [0.9995, 0.9996]**; C250 @60K 0.9988; Arm D 0.9996. D70's exact metric, receiver 0 of each 800-receiver shape: B1 0.9992, B1b 0.9964, C250 0.9989. **No damage.** The metric is validated on the failure it exists to catch: the DC-masked P4-2 mean arm reads **-0.037** on the same computation, D70's collapse reproduced. B1 cuts bin 0's spec weight to 0.0045 but does not remove the DC from the loss: it stays in the spec terms at small weight and in L_amp and L_phase at full weight. That is why it keeps the time response where masking lost it. Modal RIR (20-300 Hz): B1 0.926, B1b 0.916. (b) **Item 1: band LSD below 100 Hz, as a field** (definition identical to D106's source, `p4_5_lattice_diag.py`: mean |dB error| over bins 0-199 and receivers). 15 shapes [95% CI] / D106's 3-shape A2 set: **B1 @30K 1.95 [1.79, 2.13] / 2.34**; B5 @20K **1.71 [1.58, 1.87]** / 2.08; B4 @30K 2.03 [1.89, 2.18] / 2.35; B1b @30K 2.31 [2.11, 2.52] / 2.77; Arm D 2.79 [2.52, 3.11] / 3.41; C250 3.67 [3.56, 3.79] / 3.89; B2 3.15; B6 @20K 3.46; B3 3.59; B7 @20K 3.99. The three A2 shapes run 0.2-0.6 dB harder than the 15-shape mean in every model. **Against the material work's 1.7-2.3 dB (per-room receivers, a field measurement, D106): B1 is inside the range on the 15-shape measure and at its top edge on D106's 3-shape measure. B5 is inside on both.** D106's caveat carries over unchanged: the two LSD figures are not on identical bands or corpora, so the comparison is approximate in magnitude. **The results doc's lead is rewritten:** parity below 100 Hz is restored AS A FIELD by the DC-gradient interventions, measured the way material was measured. The baseline recipe still does not reach it (C250 3.67-3.89 dB), and D106's inversion stands as the account of the earlier claim. Provisional at 30K (B1) and 20K (B5); confirmed or withdrawn at 60K. (c) **Item 5: training validation LSD (on-lattice, full band, the trainer's own), at matched iterations.** At **30K**: B1 2.82, B1b 2.96, B4 3.76, B2 4.73, B6 4.98, C250 4.86, B3 9.15 (curriculum). At **20K**: B5 3.31, B7 5.08, C250 5.16. D117's split holds at matched iteration: fast fitters (B1, B1b, B4, B5) 2.8-3.8 dB; slow fitters (B2, B6, B7) track C250 within 0.15 dB. `outputs/p4_5/gate2_dense/VAL_LSD_MATCHED.json`.

**D121 — B5 @30K scored against D119(d) (the manager's pre-registration) and D116 P1: BOTH conditions HOLD at 30K. The DC-gradient-dominance account is supported by two independent interventions; 60K completes the test.** B5 @30K: dense Gate-2 off-lattice in-slab **0.920 [0.909, 0.931]**, out-of-slab 0.915 [0.892, 0.936]. **In-slab gap -0.002 [-0.007, +0.003]** against the <= 0.03 criterion: HOLDS. **Off-lattice r at 100 Hz (+-2.5 Hz, 3 dense shapes) 0.706** against the >= 0.409 criterion (C250 @ 60K 0.209): HOLDS. Standing metrics: excess supra-physical +0.009; 15-shape high-frequency gap +0.014 / +0.016 at 100-200 / 200-300 Hz, at the floor D120 identified; distance profile flat (0.854 / 0.851 / 0.847). Off-lattice LSD below 100 Hz **1.58 dB [1.42, 1.75]** (15 shapes; 1.97 on D106's three-shape set), inside the material work's 1.7-2.3 dB on both measures. Full-band RIR 0.9998 [0.9997, 0.9998], D70-style 0.9993, modal **0.932**, the best measured in the project. Training validation LSD at 30K 2.95 dB (B1 2.82, C250 4.86). **Reading, per D119(d):** B1 (DC removed from the gradient by weighting) and B5 (DC moved into its own head) are two routes to one intervention, and both give zero gap and the high-frequency lift through 30K. Spectral reweighting is not needed for either effect: B5 reweights nothing. **That supports the mechanism being DC gradient dominance and argues against spectral reweighting specifically.** The disclosure in D119(d) applies: B5 met both conditions at 6K-20K, so this read shows they HOLD through the phase in which the slow fitters opened their gaps (B7 +0.151 by 20K, B2 +0.083 and B3 +0.135 by 30K). It does not show that they appeared. The 60K read completes D116 P1 and D119(d). B9 / B9b will say how much of B1's route is the single DC bin.

**D122 — B7 @30K: the memorisation persists, but D117(e)'s B7 prediction FAILS as written. The original eight arms' 30K reads are complete.** B7 @30K (mean pooling, 250 shapes, baseline loss): off-lattice in-slab 0.664 [0.647, 0.683], out-of-slab 0.628. **In-slab gap +0.118 [+0.078, +0.158]**, against +0.151 [+0.089, +0.209] at 20K. D117(e) predicted ">= its 20K value", so it **FAILS** on the point estimate. The CIs overlap almost entirely, so the change is not resolved, but a prediction fixed in advance is scored as written. Every other memorisation measure GREW from 20K to 30K. The 15-shape high-frequency gap is +0.244 at 100-200 Hz (20K +0.239) and +0.217 at 200-300 Hz (20K +0.182). The excess supra-physical fraction rose **+0.165 -> +0.295**, with real-space forbidden energy at lattice points 0.694 against a 0.14 floor (5x) and 0.399 elsewhere. The distance spike is 0.654 at 0 cm against 0.526 at 2 cm. The frozen 800-receiver Gate-2, 0.782, is flattered relative to its field (0.664). Below 100 Hz the off-lattice LSD is 3.54 [3.41, 3.68] dB. Full-band RIR 0.9992, modal 0.819. Training validation LSD at 30K is **4.856, against C250's 4.864**: B7 is C250's fitting trajectory with mean pooling. **Reading.** The mode-band gap narrowed slightly as the field rose (0.521 -> 0.664), while the high-frequency memorisation and the forbidden energy at lattice points both grew. Memorisation in B7 is not receding. **D117(e) tally at 30K: 4 of 5** (B2 > 0.05; B1, B1b, B4 < 0.03; B7 fails as written). **B7 vs D (the manager's test) stands as D117(c) stated:** the same recipe at 250 shapes memorises (+0.118 at 30K); at 92 shapes it does not (+0.008 at 60K).

**D123 — B4 @60K (diagnostic arm, off-lattice numbers only). D116 P2 FAILS for B4; D117's fast-fitter account holds. First "moved the tail" score: B4 moved it, +0.092 [+0.077, +0.108].** B4 @60K: dense Gate-2 off-lattice in-slab 0.922 [0.913, 0.930], out-of-slab 0.921 [0.893, 0.945]. In-slab gap **+0.001 [-0.002, +0.003]**. High-frequency gap +0.013 / +0.016 at 100-200 / 200-300 Hz, at the floor. Excess supra-physical +0.013. Off-lattice LSD below 100 Hz 1.88 [1.73, 2.04] dB. Full-band RIR 0.9998, modal 0.932. Training validation LSD 3.17 at 60K. (a) **D116 P2** predicted B4's gap > 0.05 at 60K: **FAILS** (+0.001). Where D116 and D117 disagreed, D116 is the one that failed: B4 has attention pooling and the full DC term in its loss, and sits on the lattice, yet does not memorise. It is a fast fitter (validation 3.76 at 30K against C250's 4.86), as D117 predicts. Two necessary conditions plus slow fitting remains the account consistent with every case (D119(b)). (b) **The pre-registered "moved the tail" criterion** (`scripts/p4_5_tail_criterion.py`, paired receiver bootstrap, 2000 resamples): r at 200 Hz (+-2.5 Hz, 3 dense shapes), arm minus C250 @ 60K. OFF-lattice (primary, Rule 11): 0.350 vs 0.257, **+0.092 [+0.077, +0.108] -> MOVED**. All receivers (the literal pre-registered form): +0.092 [+0.077, +0.107] -> MOVED. The script reproduces the figure's 200 Hz marks exactly. Per the manager, B4 is a supervision-density diagnostic and does not enter a headline; its score is recorded for completeness. Output `outputs/p4_5/TAIL_CRITERION.json`.

**D124 — The manager's corrections after B4 @60K, recorded as given, with the record's own caveats; B4 promoted to a deliverable; the physics-penalty ask retracted (standing); the 60K reporting standard extended; B10 and B4s1 launched.** (a) **D99(g) REFUTED, a manager-side error (the manager's words, recorded).** D99(g) pre-registered B4 as a false-positive risk: its extra receivers come from the same 0.06 m candidate grid, so it would densify the memorised lattice and improve on-lattice numbers without improving the field. B4 produced the best field in the project with a gap of +0.001. The error was treating memorisation as free. Quadrupling receivers quadruples the positions a memorising solution must store, while shrinking the off-lattice gaps it could exploit (interpolation error falls with spacing squared), so the legitimate pathway becomes the cheaper one. **The instruction that B4 is a diagnostic whose numbers stay out of headlines is STRUCK. B4 is a deliverable.** (b) **D110 restated (third version; supersedes D110's sentence, D115(c) and D119(b)'s wording), the manager's:** "a hash-grid INR always carries position-indexed capacity, and when the training objective leaves part of the signal underfit the network fills the deficit by memorising the sampling positions. Held-out-position protocols drawn from the same lattice cannot detect it." The storage channel is the hash grid itself: its fine levels sit at 1.7 cm and below, finer than the 6 cm receiver spacing. Attention adds a second channel, not the only one. B7 memorises with mean pooling. **Caveat the record carries, so the paper version is not overstated:** under the baseline loss, three mean-pooled models at <= 92 shapes left their field underfit (Arm D r 0.36 at 100-200 Hz off-lattice) and did NOT memorise: Arm D (+0.008), P4-2 mean_unmasked (+0.007), P4-3 S (+0.000). "Always fills the deficit" holds for every arm at 250 shapes. At 92 shapes it held for attention and extent_sum but not for mean pooling. (c) **B7 overturns the pooling story. AMENDS D102(c), D104, D105(d), D80 and D109.** Under the baseline loss, BOTH poolings decline with corpus size. Mean pooling goes from Arm D @60K 0.806 at 92 shapes to B7 0.521 @20K and **0.664 [0.647, 0.683] @30K** at 250. Attention goes from C92 0.754 to C250 0.611, both @60K. Under B1's loss at 250 shapes it is 0.896 @30K. **The decline is a supervision pathology, not a data effect.** Iteration-matching, as the manager asks: Arm D has only its 60K checkpoint, so the matched comparison is B7 @60K (due ~11:30) against D @60K; B7 @30K is reported above so that the amendment does not harden on the 20K number. (d) **Retracted ask, standing: no training arm on a Helmholtz-residual or k-space band-limit penalty, now or later.** B1's excess supra-physical fraction is +0.004 and B4's +0.013, at the FDTD leakage floor, so there is nothing for such a penalty to remove. The supra-physical fraction stays a standing DIAGNOSTIC (D108). (e) **60K reporting standard, extended:** off-lattice level with CI; gap with CI; excess supra-physical fraction; band LSD below 100 Hz AND per band above it (100-200, 200-300); modal RIR in both on- and off-lattice form; validation LSD at a matched iteration (with item 6's de-circularised fit beside it once it exists). (f) **Launched:** **B10** = B4 + B1 combined (4x receivers + B1's weights, 250 shapes), on an RTX A6000 with the default allocator (D103). Manager's prediction: B10 beats both B4 and B1 below 100 Hz and at 200 Hz, with off-lattice r at 200 Hz > 0.50. If not, the two interventions are substitutes rather than complements. **B4s1** = B4 at seed 1, also on an A6000. Configs `configs/sweep_2d_mat/P4_5_{B10,B4s1}.yaml`.

**D125 — PRE-REGISTRATION (manager item 6): D117 re-scored on a non-circular fit predictor, committed before any of it is computed.** (a) **Why D117 as scored was circular** (the manager's point): its predictor, the trainer's validation LSD, is measured at held-out indices of the training lattice, so memorisation inflates it. Within a run, fit "improves" while the gap grows (C250r 20K: val 5.02, gap +0.090; C250 60K: val 4.42, gap +0.230). **A further problem, mine, with the manager's replacement.** Off-lattice fit on training geometries, measured at the SAME iteration as the gap, is also not independent of memorisation: a memorising field carries forbidden energy everywhere (D108(f): ~3.5x the floor off the lattice), so it fits worse off-lattice by construction. The test is therefore made a prediction in TIME. (b) **Predictor (PRIMARY):** off-lattice fit on training rooms at **10K**, before the arms open their gaps. B1, B4, B5 and B6 have gaps <= 0.009 at 10K; B7 has +0.032 at 6K; B2, B3, B7 and C250r at 10K are measured alongside, to confirm the predictor sits before memorisation. Fit = band LSD 0-100 Hz, the band every arm trains from step 0 (B3's curriculum included). Rooms and receivers: 23 training rooms (the 92-room corpus, nested in every arm's training set, filename-sorted, every 4th), at their B6-corpus receivers OFF the 0.06 m lattice (~716 per room). No new simulation. `scripts/p4_5_train_offlat_fit.py`. **Outcome:** dense Gate-2 in-slab gap at **30K** (D118, D121, D122; C250r @30K when it lands). **Arms:** B1, B1b, B2, B3, B4, B5, B7, C250r. B6 is excluded: these are its own training receivers, so the number is in-sample for it. (c) **Prediction (D117, restated):** **FULL SEPARATION.** Every arm whose 30K gap is > 0.05 (currently B2, B3, B7) has a worse (higher) 10K off-lattice training LSD_0-100 than every arm whose gap is < 0.03 (currently B1, B1b, B4, B5). Spearman(10K LSD_0-100, 30K gap) is reported beside it and expected positive. **If separation holds, D117 is re-established on a predictor that cannot be inflated by the outcome. If it fails, D117 is withdrawn.** (d) **Secondary, reported but flagged as partly confounded by (a):** the manager's literal version, off-lattice training fit at 30K against the 30K gap. Also the within-run trajectories (C250r and B7) of off-lattice training fit against the trainer's validation LSD, which show directly whether validation improved while the off-lattice fit did not.

**D126 — Manager items 8, 2, 7. The reported modal RIR was ON-lattice and memorisation inflated it by up to 0.24; B4's per-band breakdown; the density sweep's 2x point launched; 8x is infeasible on the lattice as specified (open question).** `scripts/p4_5_risk_metrics.py`, extended; 15 frozen shapes, dense receivers. (a) **Item 8: settled from code.** Every arm's `rir_pearson_modal_20_300` (`p4_5_eval.py:191-199`) is computed on the 15 shapes' 800 receivers, i.e. ON the training lattice; the dense on-lattice subset reproduces it to 0.002. **Off-lattice modal RIR [95% CI]:** C250 @60K **0.621 [0.600, 0.640]** against 0.862 on-lattice (a 0.24 inflation); C250r @20K 0.749 (0.801 on); B7 @30K 0.753 (0.819 on). Non-memorising arms read the same on and off: Arm D 0.871 / 0.870, B4 @60K **0.926** / 0.925, B1 @30K 0.931, B5 @30K 0.931. **The manager's suspicion is confirmed.** Between C250r @20K and C250 @60K (replica against original, so stated with that caveat), the on-lattice modal RIR ROSE 0.80 -> 0.86 while the off-lattice one FELL 0.75 -> 0.62: in the interval where only memorisation improved, the published time-response metric improved and the field's got worse. From here the modal RIR is reported on and off the lattice, and the off-lattice figure is primary (Rule 11). **Full-band RIR is saturated at 0.999 in every non-masked arm.** D96's pre-registered risk did not materialise, and the metric no longer discriminates. It is kept only as a tripwire for the DC-masked failure (-0.04). (b) **Item 2: B4's per-band LSD above 100 Hz, off-lattice [95% CI]:** @30K 4.56 [4.40, 4.73] / 5.35 [5.25, 5.45]; **@60K 4.35 [4.11, 4.59] / 5.16 [5.05, 5.29]** at 100-200 / 200-300 Hz. Comparisons at 100-200 / 200-300: C250 @60K 7.03 / 6.98; Arm D 5.35 / 5.68; B1 @30K 4.37 / 5.10; B5 @30K 4.31 / 5.09; B7 @30K 7.28 / 7.10. B4's training validation LSD is 3.76 @30K and 3.17 @60K (C250 4.86 / 4.42); D125's non-circular fit replaces it once computed. (c) **Item 7: the density sweep.** 1x = C250r (every-2K checkpoints), 4x = B4 (+ B4s1). **2x = B11, launched** on `data/track_p4_5_rx2x`: the first 1600 receivers of each 4x file (`scripts/build_p4_5_rx2x.py`; 1x subset 2x subset 4x; positions verified exact against the 1x corpus, H to 1e-6 relative because the 4x corpus re-simulated them, max recorded difference 1.3e-8). val_max_configs 20, so validation keeps 4000 rows. RTX A6000. Off-lattice metrics at matched iterations as the arms land. **8x is NOT feasible as specified** and is raised as Q23. The 0.06 m candidate pool holds 4,621 points in the smallest training room, fewer than 6,400, and the trainer's preload would be 7.7 GB, which needs streaming. The options trade density against lattice structure and are the manager's call.

**D127 — CORRECTION (mine): D120(b)'s "parity below 100 Hz restored" is WITHDRAWN. The material reference it used, 1.7-2.3 dB, is NOT a below-100 Hz figure.** Traced for manager item 3 (from code and saved fields, not inference). The "1.7-2.3 dB" is P4-1 Gate 0's demo-protocol pair (`tasks/CHUNK_P4_1_RESULTS.md:58-64`; `scripts/p4_1_gate0.py:28`; metric `scripts/armC_demo_metrics.py:167`): **LSD over 0-300 Hz at the CENTRE receiver only**, mean over 3 frozen test geometries x 4 absorption scenarios. Arm C (P3-2b `p3_2b_C_cont_mlinear`) 2.268 dB; T-geo (`p4_1_Tgeo`, same material corpus) 1.722 dB. Ground truth is ISM on a dense 64x64 grid. Recomputed from the saved fields (`outputs/armC_demo/fields/`, `outputs/p4_1/stage0/fields/`), the pipeline reproduces 2.268 and 1.722 exactly. **Restricted to 0-100 Hz over all 4096 receivers, the material models score Arm C 1.27 dB and T-geo 0.62 dB.** That is BELOW shape B1 1.95, B4 1.88 and B5 1.58. **D120(b) therefore compared a 0-300 Hz single-receiver figure with a 0-100 Hz all-receiver one, and its parity conclusion does not follow.** D99(h) and D106 used the same reference, so their numbers carry the same band mismatch, although D106's direction (shape behind material) is, if anything, reinforced. There is a second difference: the material corpus that figure comes from is ISM, while shape is FDTD, whose near-DC compliance term (|H(0)| ~ 1200-1500) makes 0-10 Hz hard in a way ISM does not. **The like-for-like reference exists: P3-3fast Track A is material editing on FDTD data** (`data/track_p3_3fast_A`, 0.5 Hz bins to 300 Hz, 120 test configs on 10 unseen geometries, per-room receivers). It is being scored now, with the identical 0-100 Hz definition and a bootstrap over geometries, alongside P3-2b on its unseen-geometry splits. Until it lands, the results doc and CONTEXT carry NO parity claim in either direction, only the shape numbers and this correction.

**D128 — PRE-REGISTRATION for manager item 3 (material parity, interval against interval), committed before any material number is computed.** `scripts/p4_5_material_lsd.py`. Definition identical to shape's (D120): band LSD over bins [0, 200), all receivers, floor 1e-30, raw H; per config, then mean per geometry; 10k bootstrap over geometries. **Material references, all on UNSEEN test geometries with per-room receivers:**
* **FDTD**, the like-for-like simulator: P3-3fast Track A (m_segment, 18K), Track A2 (m_token, 30K) and Track A3 (m_token_delta, 18K), each on its 120 test configs over 10 geometries.
* **ISM**, the corpus behind the withdrawn 1.7-2.3 reference: P3-2b Arm C (m_linear, 60K) and P4-1 T-geo (geom_token, 60K) on P3-2b's unseen-geometry splits.

**Primary comparison:** shape's best field arm against the BEST (lowest-LSD) FDTD material arm. That is the most demanding like-for-like reference. **Rule:** PARITY if the two 95% CIs overlap; shape BEHIND or AHEAD if they are disjoint. The ISM comparison is reported beside it and labelled cross-simulator. The caveat carried from D106 is restated rather than softened: the corpora differ (geometry vs material edits, different rooms), the bootstrap units differ (15 test shapes vs 10 geometries), and FDTD and ISM differ at low frequency. Whatever the result, the lead sentence of the results doc is written from it.

**D129 — Meeting-day plan (the manager's), PRE-REGISTERED before any read of the three arms. B4s1 and B11 cancelled.** (a) **Cancelled at 10:00, per the manager:** "no second seed yet" (B4s1, job 7829890) and "the receiver-density sweep must not take a GPU slot from the three arms" (B11, job 7829941). Both had started on tron about 3 minutes earlier and written no checkpoint. Their configs stay in the repo for after the meeting. B8, B9, B9b and C250r keep running on scavenger, where they contest no tron slot. (b) **The three arms**, all 250 shapes, attention pooling (`attn_residual_extent`), baseline recipe otherwise:
* **B10** = 4x receivers + B1's weights (item 1), launched 09:50.
* **B12** = ROUND-2 weights (item 2). The user's decision on the manager's ambiguous wording: RELATIVE-RESIDUAL weighting, `w_b = w_B1(b) * RMS(P_b - T_b) / RMS(T_b)`, from B1 @60K on 50 training configs at their training receivers. The relative residual is floored at its 5th percentile, the max/min ratio is capped at B1's 3146.8 by raising the minimum, and D92's scaling rule is applied (`scripts/p4_5_residual_weights.py`). The literal "1/RMS(residual)" would move weight TOWARD the bins B1 already fits, the opposite of the stated intent.
* **B13** = 4x receivers + round-2 weights, the stack (item 3), and the first to drop if a GPU is contested.

All three run on tron (non-preemptible); B10 and B13 on RTX A6000s with the default allocator (D103). (c) **Predictions (the manager's), scored at 20K, the binding read, and at 30K, against B1 and B4 at the SAME iteration.** **B10:** beats B4 AND B1 below 100 Hz (off-lattice band LSD, 15 shapes) and at 200 Hz (off-lattice r, A2 3-shape +-2.5 Hz protocol, the one behind B1's 0.451), AND off-lattice r at the top of the band is **> 0.42**. "Top of the band" is fixed now as bins 590-600 (295-300 Hz, one-sided), on the A2 3-shape protocol, with the 15-shape value beside it. If B10 does not beat both, the two interventions are substitutes, not complements. **B12:** improves 200-300 Hz over B1 at the matched iteration (off-lattice band LSD and band-mean r) and may cost a little below 100 Hz; a trade is a result, not a failure. **B13:** no prediction. (d) **Reading order at each checkpoint:** the gap first (dense Gate-2, paired CI), then the level. Then the excess supra-physical fraction; band LSD below 100 Hz and per band above it; modal RIR on and off the lattice; the per-frequency curve to 300 Hz; validation LSD at the matched iteration. (e) **The honest-note rule:** if B10 has not lifted the top of the band by the 16:30 report, it is said plainly then, not deferred to 30K.

**D130 — Round-2 vector: the manager's four guards and pre-flight gate, run on the launched vector. The gate FAILS (x1.19 against "about 1.2x"), so B12 and B13 are STOPPED and the slot goes to B10 at a second seed (B10s1), per the manager's rule.** The guards arrived after B12/B13 launched at 10:11; they are scored on the launched vector from its saved per-bin residual and target RMS (`outputs/p4_5/arms/weights/B12_weights.json`), on CPU. (1) **Floor consistency.** D96's target-RMS floor (0.386) binds on 30 bins at 166.5-181 Hz, and the launched ratio used the UNfloored target RMS. A floor-consistent vector changes their relative residual only from 0.382 to 0.358 on average, and moves the 200-300 Hz share from x1.189 to x1.196. (2) **DC.** Bin 0's weight is 0.0149-0.0151, **3.3-3.4x B1's 0.0045**, which fails a strict "small factor". The cause is the ratio cap raising the minimum, not the boost. The near-DC BAND is suppressed MORE than in B1 (0-10 Hz weight share 0.00013 against 0.00196, ~15x lower). (3) **Rows.** The residual came from a 25-config subset (every 10th by filename, training receivers, validation excluded; 17,500 rows), not all 250. Its target RMS differs from D96's full-corpus RMS by median 3.9%, max 17.4% across bins. **Caveat on the recipe, recorded as asked:** deriving weights from a model's own training residual would feed memorisation if applied to an arm that memorises (its training residual is deflated at the memorised positions). It is safe for B1, whose gap is -0.002. (4) **Band edge.** The per-bin residual RMS rises smoothly to 300 Hz (0.206 mean at 280-294 Hz; 0.231 -> 0.260 across 294.5-300 Hz), and the relative residual from 0.47 to 0.52. That is a trend, not a spike, so no band-edge exclusion is needed. **Pre-flight gate (both vectors from the same render):** 200-300 Hz weight share against B1's 0.471: relative-residual x1.189 as launched, x1.196 floor-consistent; literal 1/residual x0.681. **Both relative-residual forms are under the manager's "about 1.2x" bar.** Reading, the manager's: the relative residual is close to flat across the band (e 0.356 at 100-200 Hz against 0.454 at 200-300), so the boost has little to grip. **Action, per the manager's rule:** B12 (job 7830110) and B13 (7830111) cancelled about 3 minutes into training. **B10s1 launched** (B10 with seed 1; RTX A6000, default allocator; job 7830150), with 20K and 30K reads. Configs `P4_5_B12/B13.yaml` stay in the repo unrun. The manager's scoring pre-registration for the round-2 arm (trade 200-300 Hz up against below-100 Hz flat or down; lifting nothing means the static-weight family is exhausted and the next idea is a schedule, not a vector) is recorded for if it is ever run. **The gate result is itself the reading the manager attached to the "nothing" outcome:** as a static vector derived from B1's residual, round-2 has too little to grip to justify a slot. The next lever in the loss family is a schedule.

D119(c)'s "conditional on the baseline loss" is now a measurement: with the loss fixed, pooling does not matter, and if anything attention is slightly better. (b) **D119(e), scored as pre-registered.** "If B9 ~ B1: one weight fixes it. If B9b ~ B1 > B9: the near-DC BAND is the cause. If B1 > B9b: the tilt does real work."
