**Outcome: B9b ~ B1 > B9, so the near-DC BAND is the cause.** The manager's prediction that B9 matches B1 within noise **FAILS narrowly** above 100 Hz. On the band means B9 recovers ~94% (100-200 Hz) and ~90% (200-300 Hz) of B1's gain over the baseline-loss arm B7, and it beats B1 on Gate-2's band (0.913 vs 0.896) and below 100 Hz (1.86 vs 1.95 dB). My a-priori note (D119(e): B9 recovers only part) holds in direction, but the shortfall is small. **B1's 10-300 Hz tilt does no measurable work:** B9b, with flat weights above 10 Hz, matches it. (c) **The portable claim for the deck, at 30K and one seed:** "In this FDTD corpus, the near-DC compliance band (0-10 Hz, 21 bins) dominates the spectral gradient. Down-weighting it in the spectral loss terms is the whole effect: it removes the lattice memorisation and lifts the field across the band. One weight on the DC bin alone gets ~90% of it." **B9b is the best single arm measured at 30K** (LSD below 100 Hz 1.70 dB [1.52, 1.91], 200-300 Hz 5.03 dB, off-lattice in-slab 0.925, modal RIR 0.931). Confirmation at 60K (~17:30). These arms took no slot from the three meeting arms (scavenger).

**D133 — D125 AMENDED before any slow-fitter fit value was seen: the predictor moves from 10K to 4K, because the slow fitters have ALREADY opened their gaps at 10K. Also a launch bug of mine.** (a) **What D125 assumed and what was measured.** D125 placed its predictor at 10K as "before the arms open their gaps" and pre-registered checking that. The check, dense Gate-2 in-slab gap at 10K: **B2 +0.068 [+0.035, +0.102], B3 +0.078 [+0.053, +0.100], B7 +0.098 [+0.065, +0.131], C250r +0.096 [+0.067, +0.125]**, against B1 -0.004, B4 +0.001, B5 -0.004, B6 +0.009. **The slow fitters memorise from early on, so a 10K fit is not "before memorisation" and would be confounded exactly as D125(a) warned.** (b) **Amendment:** predictor = off-lattice training fit (LSD 0-100 Hz, D125's rooms and receivers) at **4K**. The 4K gaps are measured alongside, to show the predictor now precedes memorisation (B7 was +0.032 at 6K). Outcome and prediction are unchanged: full separation against the 30K gap, plus Spearman. **No slow-fitter fit value had been computed when this was written; only B1 and B5 at 10K and 30K exist.** If the 4K gaps are also non-trivial (> 0.03) for the slow fitters, the prediction-in-time design cannot be realised on these checkpoints, and the re-score reports that instead of a verdict. (c) **Launch bug (mine).** The D125 render jobs passed comma-separated `--items` through `sbatch --export=ALL,CMD=...`, which splits on commas, so each job rendered only its first item. Relaunched one checkpoint per job. No other job passed commas in its command.

**D134 — Manager item 3 settled by the D128 rule (interval against interval): below 100 Hz, as a field, shape with the DC fix is AHEAD of the FDTD material models (like-for-like simulator), at PARITY with ISM Arm C, and BEHIND ISM T-geo. Plus item 3 of the earlier list (C250 @30K, via its replica).** (a) **Material band LSD 0-100 Hz on its own unseen-geometry test sets** (`scripts/p4_5_material_lsd.py`, D120's definition; bootstrap over 10 geometries; `outputs/p4_5/material_lsd/`):
* **FDTD** (P3-3fast Track A, 120 configs): **A2 3.39 [2.79, 3.99]**, A3 3.87 [3.26, 4.47], A 4.05 [3.44, 4.61].
* **ISM** (P3-2b splits S1/S2/S4/S5 + unseen baselines, 210 configs): **Arm C 1.57 [1.31, 1.85]**, **T-geo 0.99 [0.65, 1.41]**.
* For context, 0-300 Hz: A2 5.46, Arm C 2.90, T-geo 2.24.

**Shape, off-lattice, 15 shapes:** B5 @30K 1.58 [1.42, 1.75]; B9b @30K 1.70 [1.52, 1.91]; B4 @60K 1.88 [1.73, 2.04]; B1 @60K 1.92 [1.74, 2.12]; Arm D 2.79 [2.52, 3.11]; C250 3.67 [3.56, 3.79]. (b) **Primary comparison (D128):** shape's best field arms against the best FDTD material arm (A2, [2.79, 3.99]). **Every DC-fixed shape arm's CI is disjoint from it and lower, so shape is AHEAD, by ~1.5-1.8 dB.** The baseline recipe (C250 3.67) and Arm D (2.79) OVERLAP it, i.e. parity without the fix. **Cross-simulator (ISM), labelled as such:** Arm C [1.31, 1.85] overlaps B5, B9b, B4 and B1, so PARITY. T-geo [0.65, 1.41] is disjoint from every shape arm, though only just from B5's [1.42, 1.75], so shape is BEHIND. (c) **What this comparison is and is not.** It compares PIPELINES as they stand. The FDTD material models predate the DC finding: they were trained with the same DC-dominated loss, and their 0-300 Hz LSD plateaued at 4.5-5.7 dB in P3-3fast. So shape-with-fix vs material-without-fix is not a task-difficulty comparison. **The like-for-like task statement needs FDTD material editing re-trained with the DC fix (B1-style weights or B5's head).** That is one arm, not run, and raised as a recommendation. **Lead sentence for the deck, as measured:** "Below 100 Hz, measured as a field on unseen rooms, shape editing with the DC fix reaches 1.6-1.9 dB, ahead of the existing FDTD material-editing models (3.4 dB) and at parity with the ISM material model Arm C (1.6 dB). The earlier apparent parity (D99(h)) compared a 0-300 Hz single-receiver material figure with on-lattice shape numbers (D106, D127)." (d) **C250 @30K, through its replica (unvalidated until C250r @60K).** C250r @30K: off-lattice in-slab **0.705 [0.665, 0.759]**, gap **+0.092 [+0.064, +0.116]**. Against C250 @60K (original): 0.611 [0.580, 0.656], gap +0.230 [+0.191, +0.277]. The gaps are disjoint, so the manager's D97-based prediction (gap smaller at 30K than at 60K) HOLDS provisionally. The trajectory has a SHAPE: the replica's gap is +0.096 / +0.090 / +0.092 at 10K / 20K / 30K, flat after an early onset, and the jump to +0.23 happens after 30K. The off-lattice level is higher at 30K than at 60K, so **the published C250 numbers are a worst case, and early stopping would have been a second lever for the baseline recipe.** Not needed for B1 or B4 (D131). All provisional on C250r @60K reproducing C250's 0.611 / +0.230.

**D135 — D125/D133 RESULT: D117 is re-established on a non-circular predictor, with the time-ordering clean for two of the four memorisers and at onset for the other two.** (a) **Is the 4K predictor before memorisation?** (D133's check; dense Gate-2 in-slab gap at 4K.) B3 -0.015 [-0.037, +0.002] and B7 -0.023 [-0.034, -0.012]: **clean**, no gap. **B2 +0.031 [+0.014, +0.048] and C250r +0.037 [+0.026, +0.045]: at ONSET**, just over D133's 0.03 line and well below their 30K gaps (+0.083, +0.092). Fast fitters: B1 -0.011, B4 -0.011, B5 -0.004. So, per D133's rule, the prediction-in-time design is fully realised for B3 and B7 and only approximately for B2 and C250r, where the predictor overlaps the first ~third of their eventual gap. (b) **The re-score, as pre-registered** (predictor: off-lattice training-room LSD 0-100 Hz at 4K, 23 rooms; outcome: 30K in-slab gap). 4K fit for the memorisers (gap > 0.05 at 30K) **B3 4.68, C250r 4.78, B2 4.78, B7 4.79** dB; for the non-memorisers (gap < 0.03) **B5 2.53, B1 2.82, B1b 3.26, B4 4.01** dB. **FULL SEPARATION HOLDS** (memoriser minimum 4.68 > non-memoriser maximum 4.01). Spearman(4K fit, 30K gap) = **+0.76 (p 0.028)**, n = 8. Restricted to the clean subset (B3, B7 against the four fast fitters) separation still holds (4.68, 4.79 vs <= 4.01). Secondary, confounded per D125(a): the 30K fit separates too (3.10-3.60 vs 1.09-1.80; Spearman +0.83). (c) **Reading.** How well a model fits the field OFF the lattice on its own training rooms by 4K, before (B3, B7) or at the very onset of (B2, C250r) memorisation, predicts which arms will memorise by 30K. The account the manager called "probably right but, as written, unfalsifiable" now rests on a predictor the outcome cannot inflate, with the stated qualification for B2 and C250r. Outputs: `outputs/p4_5/train_offlat_fit/*_4K.json`, `outputs/p4_5/gate2_dense/GAP_CI_4K.json`.

**D136 — B5 @60K: D119(d) (the manager's pre-registration) and D116 P1 both HOLD at 60K, completing them. DC gradient dominance is the mechanism, confirmed by two independent routes through the whole training run. B5 is the best arm at 60K.** B5 @60K, off-lattice: dense Gate-2 in-slab **0.924 [0.909, 0.939]**; **gap +0.001 [-0.005, +0.005]**, against the <= 0.03 criterion: HOLDS (30K -0.002). **r at 100 Hz on A2's +-2.5 Hz protocol: 0.672**, against the >= 0.409 criterion: HOLDS (30K 0.706). Excess supra-physical +0.010. Band LSD 0-100 Hz **1.53 [1.35, 1.72]**, 100-200 **4.24**, 200-300 **5.00** (the lowest of any arm in every band). Modal RIR off-lattice **0.935** (on 0.935). r at the top of the band (295-300 Hz) **0.396** on 15 shapes, 0.323 on A2. "Moved the tail" at 200 Hz: **+0.151 [+0.135, +0.167], the largest of any arm.** Validation LSD 2.52. **D116 P1** (B5 gap < 0.03 at 30K AND 60K): HOLDS. **Reading.** Moving the DC term into its own head (B5) and taking it out of the spectral gradient (B1, B9b) give the same structural outcome, zero memorisation through 60K and the high-frequency lift, and B5 reweights nothing. The 60K ranking on the manager's standard (off-lattice, CI): B5 ~ B4 > B1 > B1b > B6 > Arm D > B2 > B7 > B3 > C250. The material comparison (D134) updates with B5 @60K at 1.53 [1.35, 1.72]: still disjoint from and ahead of FDTD material A2 [2.79, 3.99], and overlapping ISM Arm C [1.31, 1.85].

**D137 — The manager's decisions after the 60K reads, recorded before B10's 20K read. B5 (DC head) is the provisional default; B2, B3 and the pooling question are CLOSED; B10's prediction is RESET; the default recipe for new work is fixed; and item (1) is pre-registered with thresholds.** (a) **Accepted:** D132, D134, D135, D136. **The DC head (B5) is the provisional default recipe**, to be confirmed against B9b (0-10 Hz spec-weight suppression) when B9b reaches 60K. **CLOSED as tested nulls:** B2 (coarse-to-fine levels; D95 null held, tail -0.097) and B3 (frequency curriculum). **CLOSED:** the pooling question, per D132 (under the fixed loss, mean ~ attention). (b) **The manager's reading, which changes the plan.** The four working arms (B1, B1b, B4, B5) spread over 0.8 dB below 100 Hz, but sit within 0.16 dB at 200-300 Hz (band LSD 5.00-5.16) and within 0.04 at the top of the band (0.353-0.396). They removed the same defect and now meet a SHARED CEILING with a different cause. In-band reweighting has no grip there (D130, D132), and density helps below 100 Hz but not above it. **B10's prediction is RESET, before its 20K read: 0.38-0.42 at the top of the band, not > 0.42 (D129).** A read near 0.40 confirms the defect is fully removed and is reported that way, not as a miss. (c) **Default recipe for new work (decision):** the DC head (dc_head_bins = 41) plus per-room receivers drawn off any shared lattice, evaluated OFF-lattice with CIs. **D135's 4K training-room off-lattice fit is the early kill signal** for runs that will memorise (memorisers >= 4.68 dB against non-memorisers <= 4.01 at 4K). (d) **Reporting for the deck (standing):** every number on the 15-shape protocol only, with anything from A2 flagged; the 200-300 Hz BAND value quoted, not the 295-300 Hz edge point; D134's parity wording kept, including that ISM and FDTD LSD are not like-for-like given ~22 dB against ~75-88 dB of dynamic range. (e) **PRE-REGISTRATION, item (1): training rooms vs test rooms at 200-300 Hz, @60K, for B5, B1 and B4.**
* Training rooms: D135's 23 rooms at their off-lattice B6-corpus receivers (training GEOMETRY, untrained POSITIONS).
* Test rooms: the 15 frozen shapes, off-lattice.
* Metrics: band-mean dB spatial Pearson and band LSD, per band (25-100, 100-200, 200-300).
* **Interpretation (the manager's), with thresholds fixed now:**
  * **CAPACITY** is the limit if, at 200-300 Hz, training rooms are within 0.05 r AND 0.3 dB of test rooms for all three arms. Next test: a band-specialised output head or a band-split model.
  * **CONDITIONING PRECISION** (how exactly the geometry code carries the room) is the limit if training rooms beat test rooms by > 0.10 r OR > 0.5 dB for all three arms.
  * Anything in between, or a split across arms, is reported as MIXED, with the numbers.

Caveat stated now: the two room sets differ in their geometry distribution (23 training L/rect rooms against 15 frozen L test shapes), so a small difference is not attributable to either cause.

**D138 — Manager items (2) and (3) after the 60K reads. (2) The geometry the model is conditioned on is EXACTLY the geometry the solver simulated: zero offset. (3) The upper band has plateaued for B1, is marginal for B5, and is still climbing for B4.** (a) **Item 2.** The manager's tolerance argument: a mode shifts by f.dL/L, and keeping the shift under half a mode spacing needs dL < ~1950/f^2 m, which is ~2 cm at 300 Hz, the FDTD grid spacing. That is checked from code and data over every P4 training room (250) and test room (15). `_fit_axis` (`aaf/sim/fdtd_2d.py:292`) fits each axis on its own grid. **Measured: per-axis dx is exactly 0.020000 m in every room** (every L and W is an even hundredth), and **outer walls are offset by at most 8.9e-16 m.** Notch faces: `notch_solid_mask` (`aaf/sim/polygon_geom.py:144`) puts the reflecting plane at the first AIR node, x = round(w/dx).dx and y = round((W-d)/dy).dy. **Offset between the token coordinates (w, W-d) and the simulated faces: max 0.0000 cm, mean 0.0000 cm, over all 484 training and 30 test faces.** The `.h5` vertex lists equal the manifest vertices the tokens are built from in all 265 rooms. **No systematic offset exists.** One residual, recorded rather than a defect: in a node-masked FDTD the effective reflecting plane is defined only to about +-dx/2 = 1 cm. That is a property of the solver, identical in training and test, so the model learns the simulator's plane. It is not a conditioning mismatch. This branch of the manager's explanation for the 200-300 Hz ceiling is therefore NOT supported by a geometry error. Item (1)'s training-vs-test comparison decides between capacity and conditioning precision. (b) **Item 3: upper-band trajectory, 30K -> 60K, 15 shapes, off-lattice.** Paired band-mean r at 200-300 Hz (60K minus 30K): **B4 +0.054 [+0.038, +0.068]** (14/15 shapes improve), **B5 +0.012 [+0.000, +0.023]**, **B1 -0.013 [-0.023, -0.003]**. Band LSD 200-300 Hz: B4 5.35 -> 5.16, B5 5.09 -> 5.00, B1 5.10 -> 5.06. **Longer training is a lever for the density arm (B4), marginal for the DC head, and spent for the reweighted loss** (all three under the same cosine-to-5e-5 schedule). The cheapest next-phase test is therefore B4-style density (or B10) trained longer, not B1 or B5. `outputs/p4_5/gate2_dense/UPPER_BAND_TRAJ.json`, `RISK_METRICS_TRAJ.json`.

**D139 — Manager item (4): the doorway model (Track B) carries the SAME DC pathology. A1 on its checkpoint: 0-10 Hz takes 81% of the step. Track B retrained with the DC head (TBdc) is running on the idle tron slot.** A1 (`scripts/p4_5_a1_bin_gradient.py`, generalised to the doorway corpus with the trainer's own config branch and conditioning call; projection share of the total weighted loss, 48 batches x 4 rows, closure 1.000000). **Track B @20K (aperture conditioning, the arm that failed the sqrt(a) coupling law: r^2 0.172, slope 2.46, against GT's 0.948 / 7.61):** 0-10 Hz **0.810** of the step; cumulative below 50 Hz 0.922, below 100 Hz 0.945; bins >= 100 Hz **0.055 +- 0.008**. **Track B2 @14K** (aperture tokens, D60's representational fix): 0-10 Hz 0.743, below 100 Hz 0.971. **C250 @60K** for reference: 0-10 Hz 0.931, below 100 Hz 0.991. The coupling law lives in the inter-room level difference across the doorway, a broadband quantity, and the model was handing 81-93% of its gradient to the near-DC compliance term. That fits D60's "uniformly wrong everywhere": a model with almost no gradient in the relevant band. **Meeting line (the manager's), supported:** "the same defect is present in our doorway model, which explains its earlier failure; the retrofit is running." **Launched:** **TBdc** = Track B's config (`P3_3FAST_trackB.yaml`) with `dc_head_bins: 41` and nothing else changed (`configs/sweep_2d_mat/P4_5_TBdc.yaml`), job 7832720, tron. It started cleanly: the DC head initialised on the doorway corpus (`[dc_head] 41 bins (0-20.0 Hz) | scale bin0 925.3`), 0.73 s/it, 30K iterations, done ~19:20 (after the meeting). **Prediction (the manager's), recorded before any TBdc read:** the coupling-law slope moves well toward GT's 7.61 and r^2 rises far above 0.172. It is scored with the P3-3fast Track B coupling evaluator against the D59a ground truth.

**D140 — Manager item (1), scored by D137(e)'s pre-registered thresholds: the 200-300 Hz ceiling is CONDITIONING PRECISION, not capacity. Training rooms beat test rooms by 0.08-0.28 r and 0.57-1.74 dB in all three arms.** @60K, off-lattice. Training rooms = D135's 23 rooms at untrained (B6-corpus, off-lattice) positions; test = the 15 frozen shapes. Band-mean dB Pearson / band LSD, TRAIN against TEST:
* **B5:** 25-100 0.959 / 0.88 dB vs 0.887 / 1.53; 100-200 0.812 / 2.84 vs 0.631 / 4.24; **200-300 0.648 / 3.83 vs 0.467 / 5.00** (train ahead by +0.181 r, 1.18 dB).
* **B1:** 0.939 / 1.22 vs 0.854 / 1.92; 0.836 / 2.61 vs 0.609 / 4.39; **0.727 / 3.32 vs 0.448 / 5.06** (+0.279 r, 1.74 dB).
* **B4:** 0.948 / 1.37 vs 0.890 / 1.88; 0.758 / 3.32 vs 0.622 / 4.35; **0.527 / 4.59 vs 0.442 / 5.16** (+0.084 r, 0.57 dB).

**Rule** (> 0.10 r OR > 0.5 dB in all three arms -> CONDITIONING): **holds for all three.** B4 qualifies on LSD alone (+0.57 dB) and is the narrowest; density narrows the train-test gap, consistent with D138's finding that B4 is still climbing. **Reading, per the manager's pre-registered interpretation:** at 200-300 Hz the networks represent their training rooms well, including at positions they never saw, but carry less of that accuracy to unseen rooms. The limit is how precisely the conditioning carries geometry to new rooms, not model capacity, so a band-specialised head or a band-split model is NOT the next test. D138 rules out the trivial version (a coordinate offset between tokens and solver). What remains is generalisation in geometry space: corpus density along the shape parameters (D75), and/or the resolution of the token encoding at the ~2 cm scale the 300 Hz modes resolve (manager's D137 physics). Caveat as pre-registered: the training rooms (23 L/rect) and test shapes (15 frozen L) differ in distribution. The size of the gap at 200-300 Hz, against a much smaller one at 25-100 Hz, is the evidence, not any single number. `outputs/p4_5/TRAIN_VS_TEST_60K.json`.

**D141 — B10 @20K (4x receivers + B1's weights), the binding read, scored against D129 and the D137 reset. The defect is fully removed (gap 0, forbidden energy at the floor, top of band 0.385 inside the reset range). But at 20K B10 is INDISTINGUISHABLE from B1: the two interventions read as substitutes at this checkpoint, not complements.** 15 frozen shapes, off-lattice; matched-iteration comparators B1 @20K and B4 @20K. **Gap first:** in-slab **+0.001 [-0.004, +0.006]** (B1 -0.003, B4 +0.002). The high-frequency gap at 100-200 / 200-300 Hz is +0.015 / +0.012, at the D120 floor. Excess supra-physical +0.010. **Level:** Gate-2 off-lattice in-slab **0.883 [0.870, 0.895]** (B1 0.887 [0.873, 0.900]; B4 0.904 [0.884, 0.919]). Band LSD 0-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz: **B10 2.04 [1.87, 2.22] / 4.45 / 5.19**; B1 2.01 / 4.42 / 5.17; B4 2.19 / 4.86 / 5.50. Band-mean r 25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300: **B10 0.853 / 0.620 / 0.466**; B1 0.854 / 0.620 / 0.470; B4 0.862 / 0.547 / 0.322. Top of band (295-300 Hz, 15 shapes): **B10 0.385**; B1 0.399; B4 0.282. Modal RIR off-lattice 0.918 (B1 0.924, B4 0.902). Validation LSD 3.33 (B1 3.16, B4 4.06). (a) **D129's original prediction** (beats BOTH B4 and B1 below 100 Hz AND at 200 Hz): **FAILS.** B10 beats B4 above 100 Hz (200-300 Hz +0.144 r, 0.31 dB) but equals B1 within CI in every band. (b) **D137's reset prediction** (top of band 0.38-0.42; "a read near 0.40 confirms the defect is fully removed"): **HOLDS at 0.385**, with gap and forbidden energy at the floor. On A2, flagged per D137(d), 0.328. (c) **Reading.** At 20K, adding 4x density to B1's loss buys nothing over B1, so on this checkpoint the interventions are SUBSTITUTES. But density's own gain in B4 arrived mostly AFTER 20K (D138: B4's 200-300 Hz band r went 0.322 @20K -> 0.389 @30K -> 0.442 @60K), so 30K (~16:25) is the fairer test of complementarity. B10s1 (seed 1) @20K at ~14:50 replicates this read. `GAP_CI_B10.json`, `RISK_METRICS_B10_20K.json`, `FREQ_CURVE_B10_20K.json`, `HF_GAP_B10_20K.json`.

**D142 — B10s1 @20K (B10 at seed 1) REPLICATES D141.** Gap +0.002 [-0.002, +0.005]; Gate-2 off-lattice in-slab 0.877 [0.856, 0.897] (B10 0.883); band LSD 0-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz 2.04 / 4.39 / 5.10 (B10 2.04 / 4.45 / 5.19; B1 2.01 / 4.42 / 5.17); band-mean r 200-300 Hz 0.473 (B10 0.466, B1 0.470); top of band (295-300 Hz, 15 shapes) 0.411 (B10 0.385), inside D137's reset range 0.38-0.42; modal RIR off-lattice 0.922. The two seeds agree within their CIs, and both equal B1 @20K. **D141's reading stands: at 20K, density added to B1's loss reads as a substitute, not a complement. 30K (~16:25) is the test of complementarity.**

**D143 — INCIDENT: the project directory hit its 500 GB quota at ~15:03. Every running training job died (exit 1, no traceback), and resubmissions failed at launch (signal 53, no log). Recovered by deleting regenerable caches; all seven jobs resumed from verified full checkpoints.** (a) **What happened.** `/fs/nexus-projects` is a 500 GB per-project export (`df` on the project directory: 500G size; the filesystem-wide 90T figure in earlier checks was misleading). Today's runs filled it: per-arm checkpoints every 2K, all retained (~84 GB under `outputs/p4_5/arms`); ~1 GB dense render caches per evaluated checkpoint (~30 GB); A2 render caches (~8 GB); and corpora. Between 15:03:00 and 15:05:46, B9, C250r, TBdc, B10, B9b, B8 and B10s1 failed with exit code 1 and no traceback (the stderr write itself failed). Resubmissions failed in 0-2 s with signal 53, the same signature as D88's legacy43, here caused by SLURM being unable to create the job's log file. A login-node `touch` returned "Disk quota exceeded". (b) **Recovery.** Deleted only regenerable, gitignored caches whose results are committed in JSONs: 34 dense 15-shape caches for early checkpoints (4K/6K/10K/20K except B10 and B10s1 @20K, the P4-2 re-scores and their verify800 runs, the family-pooling renders), 16 GB; 12 early A2 render caches, 2.6 GB; the 2x corpus `data/track_p4_5_rx2x` (B11 was cancelled; it rebuilds in a minute), 1.9 GB. No checkpoint was deleted. Before resuming, every arm's newest checkpoint was verified loadable and FULL (optimizer present): B10 24K, B10s1 22K, TBdc 8K, B8 48K, B9 48K, B9b 48K, C250r 46K. All seven were resubmitted on their original partitions and cards; watchers and the health monitor were re-pointed. **Cost:** up to ~2K iterations per arm plus queue time. B10's 30K read slips to ~16:45. (c) **Standing risk, raised for a decision.** Headroom after recovery is ~18-20 GB. With seven arms checkpointing (net ~0.25 GB per arm per 2K after thinning, plus transient full checkpoints) and ~1 GB per dense evaluation, that is ~3-4 hours. The manager's "retain every 2K checkpoint" policy costs ~84 GB at 227 MB per thinned checkpoint. Pruning thinned intermediates to a sparse set (e.g. every 10K plus the D135 4K points) would recover ~60 GB, but it is the manager's call. CLUSTER_INFO.md now records the 500 GB project quota and how to check it.

**D144 — PB3 (per-band signal heads) and W3 (parameter-matched wide control) on B5's recipe: design, tests, parameter counts, and the manager's PRE-REGISTRATION, recorded before either trains.** (a) **PB3**: B5's config (DC head, 1x receivers, same loss, schedule and seed) with only the signal pathway changed. There are THREE copies of the signal MLP (3 hidden x 512 plus output layer), each with its OWN FiLM (gamma, beta) from the shared room code, each producing ONLY its band's bins. The split is at equal 2D mode count (N(f) ~ f^2): 300/sqrt(3) = 173.2 Hz and 300 sqrt(2/3) = 244.9 Hz, rounded to bins at 173.0 and 245.0 Hz. Adjacent heads are blended by a raised-cosine crossfade over +-10 Hz, so the weights sum to one in every bin. Supports in bins: [0, 366], [326, 510], [470, 600]. The DC head still adds 0-20 Hz on top. Shared and unchanged: hash grid, sigma MLP, token encoder and pooling, DC head, renderer aggregation. **W3**: B5's recipe with ONE signal MLP, restricted to the supervised band as PB3's heads are, and widened to 896 neurons. (b) **Implementation** (`aaf/models/inr_2d.py`, `aaf/train/multi_room_2d_mat.signal_kwargs`, used by both the trainer and `load_model` so an evaluation cannot rebuild a different pathway, D73). The alternative pathway is built ONLY when configured, so the default model builds and runs exactly as before. Crossfade weights and head supports are BUFFERS, so they travel in every checkpoint. **Band restriction:** B5's single MLP outputs all 4097 bins (to 2048 Hz), but the loss sees bins 0-600 only, so the rows for bins 601-4096 never receive gradient. Restricting outputs to the band is a training no-op that makes a parameter count meaningful; bins above 300 Hz are returned as exact zeros. **Behavioural tests** (`tests/test_p4_5_pb3.py`, 6, with the 16 P4-5 arm tests; 22/22 pass): the default build has no alternative modules; the crossfade weights sum to 1 in every bin and are zero outside each support; perturbing head 3 changes ONLY bins 470-600; head 2's own FiLM, once woken, changes ONLY bins 326-510 (the vacuous-test trap); bins >= 601 are exactly zero; a PB3 checkpoint round-trips through `load_model` bit-identically. (c) **Parameter counts, signal pathway (MLPs + their FiLM layers):** B5 raw **4,993,856**, active (rows that can reach 0-300 Hz) **1,413,952**; **PB3 3,086,784** (raw = active; 2.18x B5's active); **W3 3,121,984** (within 1.1% of PB3, the closest multiple-of-16 width; `scripts/p4_5_pb3_param_probe.py`). (d) **PRE-REGISTRATION (the manager's), baseline B5 @60K:** training-room 200-300 Hz r 0.648 / LSD 3.83 dB; test-room band r 0.467 / LSD 5.00 dB.
* **BAND SEPARATION is the lever** if PB3 (i) lifts training-room 200-300 Hz r to >= 0.75 OR cuts its LSD by >= 0.5 dB, AND (ii) lifts test-room band r to >= 0.52, AND (iii) W3 recovers less than half of PB3's gain.
* **CAPACITY, not separation,** if W3 matches PB3 within CI. The next step is then widening, not splitting.
* **NEITHER** if both stay within CI of B5 on training rooms. The fit deficit is then optimisation-limited or partly metric-intrinsic, and the next lever is a longer schedule.
* **THE SPLIT COSTS SOMETHING** if the seam statistic (mean |delta dB| between adjacent bins across 173 and 245 Hz, against the same statistic elsewhere in the band) exceeds 2x the in-band value at either edge, or if the full-band RIR drops. This is reported whichever way the accuracy goes.

Evaluated at 4K (D135's kill signal), 20K, 30K and 60K on D140's protocol (training vs test rooms per band) plus the off-lattice 15-shape metrics with CI, gap, excess supra-physical, per-band LSD and off-lattice modal RIR. (e) **Launched:** PB3 (job 7835132) in B10s1's slot (B10s1 stopped per the manager); W3 (7835133). Both on tron, A5000.

**D145 — AMENDS D140's consequence (a manager-side error, the manager's words): "a band-specialised head is not the next test" is SUPERSEDED. Standing rule added: a diagnostic that splits two causes must also check the absolute level of the reference condition.** D137(e)'s rule asked only whether training rooms beat test rooms. It never asked whether training rooms are GOOD at 200-300 Hz, and they are not: training-room r is 0.53-0.73 and LSD 3.3-4.6 dB at 200-300 Hz, against 0.9-1.4 dB at 25-100 Hz. Training-room LSD rises 2.1-3.2 dB across the band (B1 +2.10, B5 +2.95, B4 +3.22); transfer adds 0.6-1.7 dB on top (B4 +0.57, B5 +1.18, B1 +1.74). **Fit is the larger share, about 70% for B5.** D140's numbers stand; its conclusion is withdrawn. The ceiling is MOSTLY fit and partly transfer, which is why PB3 (fit) and W3 (capacity) are the tests (D144). **Standing rule (CLAUDE.md Rule 12):** a diagnostic that splits two causes must also check the absolute level of its reference condition.

**D146 — Manager item (3), the doorway check: the near-DC term is uniform across the doorway only below ~5 Hz. At 10 Hz uniformity collapses with aperture, so a B9b-style doorway arm (TB9b) is queued. The coupling-law level difference is measured over 20-300 Hz.** (a) **D101's statistic** |spatial mean|^2 / mean |H|^2 over both sub-rooms, per bin, doorway training corpus (400 configs), by aperture (`scripts/p4_5_doorway_uniformity.py`):

| stratum | 0-2 Hz | 5 Hz | 10 Hz | 15 Hz | 20 Hz |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| a <= 0.5 m (74) | 1.000 | 0.963 | **0.395** | 0.037 | 0.077 |
| 0.5 < a <= 1.5 m (110) | 1.000 | 0.983 | **0.696** | 0.067 | 0.027 |
| a > 1.5 m (176) | 1.000 | 0.990 | **0.827** | 0.188 | 0.008 |
| fully open (20) | 1.000 | 0.993 | **0.884** | 0.280 | 0.011 |
| sealed (20) | 0.512 | 0.512 | 0.505 | 0.450 | 0.277 |

Sealed rooms: one sub-room gets no source energy at all. The mean |level gap| between sub-rooms at 10 Hz is 7.8 dB for small apertures against 4.4 dB fully open. (b) **Reading.** The DC head predicts one value per room per bin over 0-20 Hz, so between ~5 and ~15 Hz on narrow doorways it can carry only the spatial MEAN; the rest falls back on the field network. By the manager's condition ("if uniformity falls at small apertures"), **TB9b is queued**: Track B's config with B9b's 0-10 Hz spectral-loss suppression (`outputs/p4_5/arms/weights/B9b_dcband_weights.npy`), job 7835134, tron. (c) **The coupling-law observable** (`scripts/p3_3fast_trackB_eval.py:13`) is `20 log10(<|H|>_roomB / <|H|>_roomA)` per ISO third-octave band inside **20-300 Hz**, pooled over the band. It never touches the DC head's 0-20 Hz, so the DC head can affect the coupling law only through the gradient balance (D139). TBdc's A1 gradient share is reported at 30K beside its coupling-law result.

**D147 — TBdc (Track B + DC head, nothing else changed): the doorway coupling law, which Track B "never learned", is now largely learned. Pooled r^2 0.172 -> 0.757, slope 2.46 -> 4.57 (ground truth 7.61). The manager's D139 prediction HOLDS.** (a) **Run.** TBdc early-stopped at 14K under Track B's own unchanged rule (warmup 10K, patience 10K, 0.3%): best validation LSD 5.20 dB at 6K, against Track B's 5.64 at 10K (Track B itself early-stopped at 20K). TBdc's validation LSD sits 0.4-0.5 dB below Track B's at every checkpoint. **Evaluated at its final checkpoint (14K)**, as Track B was at its final (20K). (b) **Exactness first.** `scripts/p3_3fast_trackB_eval.py`, run unchanged on Track B @20K, reproduces the published `outputs/p3_3fast/trackB/EVAL.json` (pooled slope 2.4568, r^2 0.1718; differences <= 5e-8). (c) **Coupling law** (inter-room level difference, 20-300 Hz third-octaves, against sqrt(a)), Track B -> **TBdc** (GT):
* pooled prediction slope 2.457 -> **4.574** (7.611);
* pooled prediction r^2 0.172 -> **0.757** (0.948);
* prediction-vs-GT slope 0.354 -> 0.583, r^2 0.218 -> **0.753**;
* seen-aperture r^2 0.215 -> 0.772 (GT 0.957);
* held-out-band r^2 0.001 -> 0.029, against GT's own 0.106 on that narrow band.

**Prediction (D139):** "the coupling-law slope moves well toward 7.61 and r^2 rises far above 0.172". **HOLDS:** the slope recovers 41% of the distance to ground truth, and r^2 rises 4.4x to 0.76. (d) **Reading.** The doorway model's failure (D60: "did not represent the aperture law at all, uniformly wrong everywhere") was largely the same DC gradient pathology as the shape models (D139: 81% of its step in 0-10 Hz), not only the representational problem D60 diagnosed. One change, a DC head, takes it from "never learned" to r^2 0.76. The slope is still short (4.57 vs 7.61), which leaves room for D60's tokenized-divider fix and for TB9b's 0-10 Hz suppression (running), which addresses the non-uniform near-DC term across narrow doorways (D146). The A1 gradient share on TBdc @14K is running and is reported beside this. Output `outputs/p4_5/trackB_dc_eval/EVAL.{json,md}`; reproduction `outputs/p4_5/trackB_eval_repro/`.

**D148 — PB3 and W3 @4K: both PASS D135's kill signal, neither memorises, and PB3's seams are mild (well under the 2x flag).** (a) **Kill signal** (4K off-lattice training-room LSD 0-100 Hz; memorisers >= 4.68, non-memorisers <= 4.01): **PB3 2.52, W3 2.28** (B5 2.53). Both run on. (b) **Gap** (dense Gate-2 in-slab, paired CI): PB3 -0.006 [-0.013, -0.000], W3 -0.008 [-0.014, -0.002], B5 @4K -0.004. Off-lattice in-slab level: PB3 0.812 [0.790, 0.836], W3 0.845 [0.821, 0.866], B5 @4K 0.824. Excess supra-physical +0.016 / +0.019. Full-band RIR (D70-style) 0.9987 for both. (c) **Training rooms, 200-300 Hz, @4K** (D140's protocol): PB3 r 0.358 / LSD 5.36 dB; W3 r 0.285 / LSD 5.53 dB. That is early; the pre-registered comparison (D144(d)) is scored against B5 @60K at 20K, 30K and 60K. (d) **Seam check** (`scripts/p4_5_seam_check.py`; mean |dB step| between adjacent bins inside each +-10 Hz crossfade window, against the rest of 100-300 Hz; 15 shapes, off-lattice): **PB3 1.30x at the 173 Hz window and 0.75x at 245 Hz**; W3 1.05 / 1.01; ground truth 0.98 / 1.02. So PB3 shows a mild roughening at the lower seam and a smoother upper window, both well under the manager's 2x flag. Tracked at every later read. `GAP_CI_PB3_W3.json`, `seam_check_4K.json`.

**D149 — TBdc's A1 gradient share, reported beside its coupling law (D147): the DC head takes the 0-10 Hz step off the FIELD; the all-parameter share hid that.** The manager asked for the share at 30K; **TBdc early-stopped at 14K** (D147(a)), so there is no 30K checkpoint, and the share is measured at 14K against Track B at the same iteration (`trackB2` @14K). (a) **Caveat first.** The all-parameter projection includes the DC head's own parameters (34,258 of 44.46M for TBdc; 68,690 of 56.94M for B5). The head outputs the ~1000x DC level, so its gradient dominates the total step. With it included, TBdc's 0-10 Hz share RISES (0.743 -> 0.883) and B5 @60K reads 0.997, which would wrongly suggest the head worsens the pathology. `--exclude-prefix dc_head` restricts the projection to field parameters (hash grid, sigma/signal MLPs, FiLM, token encoder). Closure is 1.000000 in all five runs. (b) **Parameter-gradient projection share** (192 training rows, 48 batches of 4, jackknife SE):

| model | params | 0-10 Hz | 10-20 Hz | >= 100 Hz (+-SE) | cum < 50 Hz | grad-norm share >= 100 Hz |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Track B @14K (no head: all = field) | all | 0.743 | 0.091 | 0.029 +- 0.004 | 0.931 | 0.165 |
| TBdc @14K | all | 0.883 | 0.023 | 0.037 +- 0.002 | 0.942 | 0.238 |
| **TBdc @14K** | **field only** | **0.033** | **0.197** | **0.305 +- 0.020** | **0.522** | **0.431** |
| C250 @60K (D97, no head) | all = field | ~0.93 | -- | 0.009 +- 0.004 | 0.977 | 0.099 |
| B5 @60K | all | 0.997 | 0.000 | 0.001 +- 0.000 | 0.999 | 0.074 |
| **B5 @60K** | **field only** | **0.039** | **0.016** | **0.349 +- 0.081** | **0.496** | **0.529** |

(c) **Reading.** On the field, the DC head cuts the 0-10 Hz share of the step from 0.74 to 0.03 (doorway) and from ~0.93 to 0.04 (shapes). The >= 100 Hz share rises 10x on the doorway (0.029 -> 0.305) and 38x on the shapes (0.009 -> 0.349). That is the same mechanism in both corpora, and it matches TBdc's coupling-law recovery (r^2 0.172 -> 0.757, slope 2.46 -> 4.57 against GT 7.61). The 10-20 Hz band keeps 0.197 of TBdc's field step. That is the band where the head's support (0-20 Hz) overlaps the field, and where D146's narrow-aperture non-uniformity sits, which TB9b's 0-10 Hz suppression does not touch. Labelled inference: the residual slope shortfall may live partly there. (d) **Standing correction for A1 reporting.** Any arm with a DC head reports its A1 share field-only, with the all-parameter figure beside it, labelled. Files `a1_{TBdc_14K,TBdc_14K_fieldonly,B5_60K_all,B5_60K_fieldonly,trackB2_14K}_C250_60K.json` (the `C250_60K` suffix is the script's historical label for "trained checkpoint", not the model).
*[D149 correction, see D150(f): the row labelled "Track B @14K (no head)" is **Track B2** @14K (aperture tokens, cond_dim 464), a different architecture. Track B's matched figure is @20K: 0.810 / 0.055 (D139).]*

**D150 — Speckle thread opened, PRE-REGISTERED before any speckle number is computed; doorway label correction; TBdc and TB9b resumed to 30K.** (a) **Diagnostics** (no training; B5 @60K, C250 @60K, B6 @60K; 15 dense test shapes; `scripts/p4_5_speckle.py`, `scripts/p4_5_speckle_render.py`, `scripts/p4_5_speckle_2cm.py`). The manager specified each of these; the operationalisations below are mine, and I fixed them before computing any number.
* **d1 speckle ratio.** 4-neighbour pairs on the 64x64 grid with both nodes in the room. Per bin and per shape: mean |dB step| of the prediction divided by the same for FDTD. PRIMARY uses pairs with both nodes off-lattice (Rule 11); all pairs are secondary. **Quintiles** (200-300 Hz): the pair's FDTD level is min(dB|T_a|, dB|T_b|), so a pair straddling a null counts as near-null. Quintiles are formed per shape per bin. Each quintile reports its ratio and its share of the excess sum(|dB step_P| - |dB step_T|).
* **d2 coherence.** Mask-aware high-pass: HP = f - LP(f) on in-room nodes. LP is the least-squares projection of the in-room samples onto fields band-limited to |k| <= 1.5 k0 (k0 = 2 pi f / 343), solved by CG on the 2x zero-padded grid. That respects the boundary like normalised convolution, but passes |k| <= 1.5 k0 at unity, so it does not blur the physical band. The literal normalised convolution (Gaussian applicability, half-power at 1.5 k0) is reported beside it. Coherence is the complex correlation of HP_P with HP_T over off-lattice nodes, reported as |c| and Re c, with the HP energy ratio.
* **d3**: `model.level_mask[N:] = 0` for N = 6, 5, 4 (cells 1.65 / 2.5 / 3.7 cm at the finest kept level). This is the existing c2f mechanism (point and tx encoders, after FiLM), the inference analogue of a2's 6-level cap.
* **d4**: the 64-ray fan rotated by 8 angles drawn uniformly in [0, 2 pi/64) (seed 20261014; larger offsets only permute the rays), complex outputs averaged.
* **d5**: the d2 LP applied to the prediction. Report off-lattice r/LSD before and after, with the FDTD self-projection ceiling (LP(T) against T) beside it.
* **d6**: shape 1000 on every in-room 2 cm FDTD node (0.12 m wall inset as the dense grid), B5 @60K against a fresh FDTD re-simulation with identical settings, which must reproduce the stored h5 at shared nodes.

(b) **Gates for the gated arms, fixed now.**
* **a3 (floored log-amplitude loss, eps_b = 0.05 x per-bin target RMS from `B1_weights.json`) RUNS IF** at B5 @60K, 200-300 Hz, primary pairs, the lowest FDTD-amplitude quintile holds >= 40% of the excess |dB step| (2x its 20% share) AND has the largest ratio of the five.
* **a2 (hash grid capped at 6 levels) RUNS IF** at lv6 (else lv5), the 200-300 Hz speckle excess (ratio - 1) falls >= 50% while off-lattice band r drops <= 0.02 in every band. **Ambiguous** (excess falls 20-50%, or falls >= 50% at an r cost of 0.02-0.05): Zip-NeRF-style level-weighted weight decay instead. **Negative** (otherwise): no a2, recorded.
* **Helmholtz residual (tomorrow).** The manager reverses the earlier "no Helmholtz arm, now or later" for a stated reason: S4 shows the right AMOUNT of fine-scale energy, not that it sits in the right PLACE. It is queued IF d2's |c| <= 0.1 at 200-300 Hz AND neither B5r nor a2 cuts B5's 200-300 Hz speckle excess by >= 50% at 60K.
* **a1 B5r (ungated; `P4_5_B5r.yaml` = B6 + `dc_head_bins: 41`, i.e. B5 on B6's corpus).** Manager's prediction: the lowest speckle ratio of any arm, with accuracy at or above B5.

(c) **Every arm in this thread** reports d1 and d2 at 60K, beside off-lattice r/LSD with CIs and the gap.

(d) **TBdc's stop criterion.** `_early_stop` at 14K compared min(val LSD) after the 4K boundary (5.2024, at 6K) with min(val LSD) up to 4K (5.2162). That is a 0.26% improvement, below 0.003, so it stopped. Two defects make that val signal unfit for a plateau call. (1) The val receivers are a held-out ROW (iy = 3) of the same 8x8 training grid, on training configs: the same spatial process, which Rule 11 forbids as the primary figure. (2) **Bug:** the 2D trainer's `validate()` calls `model.eval()` but never `renderer.eval()`, so every val render uses a randomly jittered ray fan, and the val LSD is noisy at fixed weights. `load_model`-based evaluation is unaffected. No reported P4-5 number uses the trainer's val LSD; P4-5 shape arms set early_stop_warmup = n_iters. The fix is deferred to the chunk end, so that running and requeued arms keep consistent logging. **TB9b stopped the same way at 16K** (best 5.019 at 6K).

(e) **Resumes.** `P4_5_TBdc30.yaml` and `P4_5_TB9b30.yaml` are identical to their parents except `early_stop_warmup: 30000` (= n_iters). The cosine runs over 30K unchanged, and both resume from their last checkpoint in the same directory. TBdc is on tron, TB9b on scavenger.

(f) **D149 label correction.** The A1 row "Track B @14K (no DC head)" (0.743 / 0.029) is **Track B2** @14K (`outputs/p3_3fast/p3_3fast_trackB2/ckpt_iter0014000.pt`: aperture tokens, cond_dim 464). Track B (55-d aperture conditioning, TBdc's actual parent) has no 14K checkpoint on disk (only 20K). **The matched pair is therefore Track B @20K (0.810 / 0.055, D139) against TBdc @20K** from the resume, both on the same 30K cosine. A1 (field-only and all-parameter, labelled) and the coupling law will run on TBdc @20K and @30K. The coupling law of Track B @14K cannot be computed, so the before/after pair is matched at 20K instead. The published 0.172 / 2.46 is Track B @20K, so it IS the matched reference.

(g) **Two-region DC head, pre-registered.** It is implemented IF TBdc @30K's field-only 10-20 Hz A1 share stays >= 0.15 (near D149's 0.197). Design: one complex value per bin for each side of the divider, blended by a soft side indicator sigmoid((x - x0)/tau) of the receiver position, i.e. a lumped two-volume model. Manager's prediction: the room model's 10-20 Hz share falls well below 0.197, and the slope moves further toward 7.61.

(h) **Note on the held-out band.** The original Track B evaluation's held-out band is **a in [0.9, 1.1] m** (sqrt(a) in [0.95, 1.05]), `aaf/data/aperture_configs.py:51`. The deliverable report uses it as defined there.

**D151 — The 60K round (B8, B9, B9b, C250r) and B10 @30K; PB3/W3 interim at 20K. C250r does NOT reproduce C250 at 60K, and the difference is all at 25-100 Hz.** Dense Gate-2, off-lattice, 15 shapes, paired CIs; per-band r is the off-lattice per-bin column Pearson, band-averaged (`p4_5_pooling_paired.per_shape`). `GAP_CI_60K_round3.json`, `GAP_CI_PB3_W3_20K.json`.

| arm | OFF in-slab [CI] | gap in [CI] | r 25-100 | r 100-200 | r 200-300 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B5 @60K (default) | 0.924 [0.909, 0.939] | +0.001 [-0.005, +0.005] | 0.887 | 0.631 | 0.467 |
| B9b @60K | 0.924 [0.915, 0.935] | +0.002 [-0.001, +0.006] | 0.885 | 0.634 | 0.478 |
| B9 @60K | 0.922 [0.911, 0.931] | +0.001 [-0.004, +0.007] | 0.883 | 0.618 | 0.450 |
| B8 @60K | 0.870 [0.854, 0.882] | -0.000 [-0.008, +0.007] | 0.844 | 0.596 | 0.427 |
| B10 @30K | 0.874 [0.866, 0.882] | +0.001 [-0.004, +0.007] | 0.853 | 0.617 | 0.459 |
| **C250r @60K** | **0.775 [0.741, 0.812]** | **+0.099 [+0.084, +0.112]** | 0.648 | 0.162 | 0.106 |
| C250 @60K (published) | 0.611 [0.580, 0.656] | +0.230 [+0.191, +0.277] | 0.512 | 0.149 | 0.118 |

(a) **C250r fails its validation.** It was launched to stand in for C250, whose intermediate checkpoints are gone, "validated by its 60K endpoint". It differs from C250 only in checkpoint bookkeeping and an early-stop warmup that never fired. At 60K it memorises much less (gap +0.099 against +0.230) and its in-slab CI does not overlap C250's. The difference is **all at low frequency**: paired C250r - C250 is +0.137 [+0.123, +0.151] at 25-100 Hz, +0.013 [+0.002, +0.025] at 100-200 Hz and -0.012 [-0.020, -0.003] at 200-300 Hz. **Reading:** the mode-based in-slab score is dominated by the lowest modes, and how far a memorising run memorises that band is run-dependent. tcnn's hash-grid backward uses atomics, so two runs of one config are not bit-identical. Non-memorising arms replicate tightly (B10s1 vs B10 +0.002, D142; B4s1). **Consequences:** (1) C250r's 30K read must not stand in for C250's low-band or in-slab numbers; earlier uses of it are flagged as one run of a high-variance baseline. (2) The 200 Hz tail criterion (arm minus C250 @60K) is NOT affected materially: the two runs agree within 0.012 at 200-300 Hz. (3) Any claim of the size "arm X beats C250 in-slab by d" carries a baseline run-variance of order 0.16 when X does not memorise and C250 does. The DC-fixed arms' lead over C250 (0.92 vs 0.61-0.78) survives it.
(b) **B9b against B5 at 60K** (D137's check): equal on Gate-2 (0.924 vs 0.924, gap 0) and slightly AHEAD at 200-300 Hz, +0.011 [+0.004, +0.017] paired; tied at 25-100 (-0.002 [-0.010, +0.004]) and 100-200 (+0.003 [-0.004, +0.011]). The DC head (B5) and 0-10 Hz loss suppression (B9b) reach the same place. B5 remains the default only because it is the recipe the downstream arms are built on (D137); B9b is not worse on any measured axis.
(c) **PB3 / W3 interim at 20K** (pre-registered comparison D144(d) is scored at 30K and 60K). No gap in either (PB3 -0.003, W3 +0.002). **Training rooms, 200-300 Hz: PB3 r 0.550 / LSD 4.43 dB; W3 r 0.553 / LSD 4.43 dB: identical.** **Test rooms, 200-300 Hz: PB3 0.489 [0.467, 0.512], W3 0.469 [0.443, 0.495]**, paired PB3 - W3 +0.020 [+0.009, +0.031]. B5 is at 0.455 @30K and 0.467 @60K. So at 20K band separation buys a small, significant TRANSFER edge with no FIT edge. Neither is near the 0.52 / 0.75 thresholds yet. B5's matched 30K training-room per-band fit is running (`B5_30Kb`), so that the 30K read has a matched baseline (Rule 12).

**D152 — Speckle thread, diagnostics d1-d6 (D150): the visible speckle is SUPRA-PHYSICAL content (|k| > 1.5 k0), finer than the 64x64 test grid. It is under 1% of the field's energy, and a physical projection at inference removes all of it without blurring. It is NOT where B5's accuracy is lost. Gates: a3 FAILS, a2 NEGATIVE, Helmholtz gate condition 1 FAILS.** 15 dense test shapes, off-lattice (pairs with both nodes off-lattice), shape-bootstrap CIs; `outputs/p4_5/speckle/*.json`, `d5_d1_after_projection.json`, `d6_shape1000_2cm.{png,json}`.

(a) **d1 on the 64x64 grid** (speckle ratio, prediction over FDTD): B5 **1.097 / 1.013 / 0.968** (25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz); B6 1.733 / 1.087 / **0.469**; C250 3.736 / 2.870 / 2.113. B5 is NOT rougher than FDTD at this spacing. **By FDTD amplitude quintile, B5 at 200-300 Hz:** ratio **0.61 / 1.09 / 1.28 / 1.42 / 1.46** (quietest to loudest). Mean excess |dB step| per pair is **-2.04 / +0.22 / +0.47 / +0.51 / +0.39 dB**. B5's nulls are 2 dB SHALLOWER per step than FDTD's (they are filled in), and the excess is roughness in the LOUD regions. **Gate a3 FAILS** (Q1 holds no excess and has the SMALLEST ratio), so the floored-loss arm does not run. Its code (default-off, tested) and `P4_5_B5F.yaml` stay. Note B6's 0.469: a ratio far BELOW 1 is blur, not physicality. The target is a ratio near 1 with a flat quintile profile, so "lowest speckle ratio" (the a1 prediction's wording) must be read as |ratio - 1|.

(b) **d6, 2 cm** (shape 1000, all 61,874 in-room FDTD nodes; the fresh FDTD re-simulation reproduces all 3,753 stored receivers to 2e-10). B5's d1 is **1.45 at 200 Hz and 1.27 at 280 Hz**, against 0.97 and 1.04 on the 64x64 nodes of the same shape. **The test grid aliases the speckle away;** it lives at the 2-4 cm scale. dB-field correlation lengths: FDTD 0.32 / 0.24 m, B5 0.28 / 0.20 m. The ERROR field's correlation length is 0.26 / 0.18 m, i.e. most of B5's dB error is at the field's own scale (a wrong pattern), not speckle. The figure shows this at 200 Hz, where B5's large-scale pattern differs from FDTD's.

(c) **d2, fine-scale coherence.** The mask-aware high-pass uses the least-squares band-limited projection; it is tested to keep a physical plane wave to <= 5e-4 at every frequency 25-300 Hz, with 4x padding. Fine-scale (|k| > 1.5 k0) energy is **0.9% of B5's field and 0.3% of FDTD's** at 200-300 Hz: **2.55x FDTD's** [2.23, 2.98] (1.77x at 25-100 Hz, 2.35x at 100-200 Hz). Complex coherence **|c| = 0.455 [0.429, 0.478]** at 200-300 Hz (Re c 0.452), 0.583 at 100-200 Hz and 0.670 at 25-100 Hz. About half of B5's fine-scale content sits where FDTD's does; the rest is excess. C250: |c| 0.04-0.08 and 51-120x FDTD's fine-scale energy (35% of its field at 200-300 Hz), i.e. memorisation noise. B6: |c| 0.31-0.36, 4-9x. **This revises the premise of the Helmholtz reversal.** S4's zero-fill FFT measure is dominated by mask-edge leakage common to prediction and FDTD, which made the AMOUNT look right. Mask-aware, B5 has 2.5x too much, half of it misplaced. The literal normalised convolution (Gaussian, half-power at 1.5 k0) loses ~10% of a physical plane wave (test), so it high-passes physical content (its |c| 0.685, HP ratio 0.79); it is reported but not used for any verdict. **Helmholtz gate condition 1 (|c| <= 0.1) FAILS.** Condition 2 (B5r / a2 not removing the speckle) is read at B5r @60K.

(d) **d3, hash-level masking** (paired against B5). Masking every level >= 6 cuts the 200-300 Hz ratio by -0.318 [-0.333, -0.301], to 0.650: it OVERSHOOTS into blur and deepens the null filling (Q1 0.37). The loud-quintile excess falls (Q5 1.46 -> 1.19). Off-lattice r falls by **-0.235 / -0.204 / -0.124** (25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300) and LSD rises by +2.5 / +1.7 / +1.2 dB. Levels >= 5 and >= 4 are the same or worse. **Gate a2: NEGATIVE** (r cost 0.12-0.24, far above 0.05), so no a2. Caveat, as the manager anticipated: masking moves the MLP's inputs off-distribution, so d3 cannot separate "the fine levels carry signal" from "masking is off-distribution". A 6-level cap trained from scratch is therefore untested, not refuted. `P4_5_B5L6.yaml` stays unlaunched.

(e) **d4, ray-fan rotation averaging.** The 8 single-angle renders are statistically identical (spread <= 0.001 in d1 and r). Their complex average lowers d1 by **-0.054 [-0.059, -0.050]** at 200-300 Hz (loud-quintile excess -22 to -24%) while r RISES +0.003 [+0.003, +0.004] and LSD falls 0.012 dB. **A quadrature-aliasing component exists and is minor:** about a fifth of the loud-region excess on the 64x64 grid.

(f) **d5, physical projection (renderer-side, no training).** Off-lattice r **+0.0023 [+0.0017, +0.0030]** at 200-300 Hz, +0.0016 at 100-200 Hz, +0.0005 (n.s.) at 25-100 Hz; LSD -0.004 [-0.008, -0.000] dB at 200-300 Hz. The FDTD self-projection ceiling is r 0.995 and LSD 0.29 dB, so the projection is nearly lossless on physics. **Recorded as instructed: a free renderer-side gain, and proof that the removed content was noise** (accuracy rose). **Its effect on the speckle**, a check added beyond the spec:
* On the 2 cm grid, d1 goes **1.45 -> 0.89 at 200 Hz and 1.27 -> 0.94 at 280 Hz**, while FDTD projected the same way stays at **0.99 / 1.00**. The projection removes ALL of B5's visible excess roughness, leaves FDTD's nodal structure intact, and so satisfies the governing principle.
* On the 64x64 grid, the loud-quintile excess falls 0.39-0.51 -> 0.31-0.39 dB.
* Accuracy barely moves (2 cm dB Pearson 0.467 -> 0.469 and 0.483 -> 0.485), because the speckle is under 1% of the energy.

(g) **Reading.** The speckle is a COSMETIC, supra-physical component, below 1% of energy. It is prominent in dB maps of loud regions and sits at 2-4 cm, so the 64x64 test grid cannot see it. A physical projection at render time removes it completely, at zero cost. B5's 200-300 Hz accuracy deficit (r 0.47) is in the physical band: a wrong pattern at the field's own scale, which neither projection, fan averaging nor level masking touches. **Consequences:**
* (1) For figures, render B5 through the projection: physical, not blurred.
* (2) The speckle is not the lever for 200-300 Hz accuracy; the fit-and-transfer levers (D140/D145: density, band separation, schedule) remain the ones that matter.
* (3) A Helmholtz residual would penalise exactly the content the projection removes. Since the projection is free at inference, its case is only for a network that is physical by construction (e.g. for edits or gradients), and its pre-registered gate (|c| <= 0.1) fails. Not queued; the manager's call.
* (4) a1 (B5r) is read on |d1 - 1|, the quintile profile and 2 cm d1, plus off-lattice r/LSD with CIs.

**D153 — Doorway at the matched iteration (D150(f)): Track B @20K -> TBdc @20K takes the slope 2.46 -> 4.57 (0.32 -> 0.60 of GT) and r^2 0.172 -> 0.739, and roughly halves the inter-room level error at every aperture. TBdc does not improve from 14K to 20K. TB9b (0-10 Hz suppression, no head) is weaker than the head. An A1 objective gap is fixed. B5r passes its 4K kill signal.**

(a) **Deliverable terms** (`outputs/p4_5/trackB_dc_eval/DELIVERABLE.md`). Mean |pred - true| inter-room level difference, 6 unseen geometries per aperture, Track B @20K -> TBdc @20K: **a = 0.15 m 7.72 -> 4.86 dB**, 0.30 6.16 -> 3.33, 0.50 5.07 -> 2.45, 0.70 4.24 -> 1.91, held-out band (0.95 / 1.00 / 1.05) 3.47 / 3.32 / 3.18 -> 1.48 / 1.43 / 1.37, 1.5 2.15 -> 0.78, 2.0 1.44 -> 0.64, 2.5 0.85 -> 0.59, open 0.80 -> 0.36. **Slope ratio 0.32 -> 0.60.** Pooled r^2 0.172 -> 0.739 (GT 0.948). Held-out-band RMS residual against the seen-aperture line 2.30 -> 0.79 dB (GT 0.68); mean held-out residual +0.02 -> -0.12 dB (GT +0.44). TBdc @14K and @20K agree within 0.1 dB at every aperture (slope 4.57 at both). Between those two points the coupling law has plateaued; the 30K read (~23:45) is still to come.

(b) **A1, TBdc field-only, 14K -> 20K:** 0-10 Hz 0.033 -> 0.038; **10-20 Hz 0.197 -> 0.117**; >= 100 Hz 0.305 -> 0.340 [+-0.032]. The 10-20 Hz share D149 flagged is falling with training alone. The two-region DC head's gate (D150(g): >= 0.15 at 30K) is already below threshold at 20K. All-parameter shares (head included, labelled): 0-10 Hz 0.995. Track B @20K (no head, D139): 0-10 Hz 0.810, >= 100 Hz 0.055.

(c) **TB9b @20K** (Track B + B9b's 0-10 Hz spec-weight suppression, no head): slope 3.92 (ratio 0.52), r^2 0.632, |LD error| 5.39 dB at a = 0.15 m, open 0.88 dB. That beats Track B at every aperture but is below TBdc at small apertures and in the open configuration (0.88 vs 0.36 dB). On the doorway, unlike the shapes (D151(b)), the DC head is the stronger fix.

(d) **Correction: A1 decomposed the BASELINE objective for spec-weighted arms.** `p4_5_a1_bin_gradient.py` applied the four term weights but not `loss_spec_bin_weights`. For an arm whose intervention IS a per-bin weight (TB9b; the shape arms B1, B1b, B8, B9, B9b, B10), its share was therefore the share of the unweighted loss at that checkpoint, not the step the arm trains on. **Only TB9b @20K was ever run on such an arm** (0-10 Hz 0.991; unpublished until now, labelled here as baseline-objective). Every other A1 in the record (C250, Track B, B2, TBdc, B5) has no spec weights and is unaffected. The script now applies the checkpoint's weights by default (`--objective trained`; `--objective baseline` keeps the old behaviour) and records which was used. The trained-objective rerun of TB9b @20K is running.

(e) **B5r @4K (a1):** D135 kill signal (4K off-lattice training-room LSD 0-100 Hz) **2.56** against non-memorisers <= 4.01 (B5 @4K 2.53). Gap -0.001 [-0.006, +0.004]; off-lattice in-slab 0.796 [0.772, 0.825] (B5 @4K 0.824). It runs on. Note: B5r's training receivers are drawn per room from all FDTD nodes, so about 1.3% of the "off-lattice training-room" probe points can coincide with its training receivers. That is negligible at this scale.

**D154 — PB3 / W3 at 30K, scored against D144's pre-registration: NO pre-registered outcome holds cleanly at 30K. Both widened pathways fit faster than B5 (training rooms at 30K ~ B5 at 60K). PB3 alone holds a small, stable transfer edge at 200-300 Hz (+0.021 over W3). W3 recovers more than half of PB3's gain. Final scoring is at 60K. Also: TB9b's A1 on its trained objective.** Dense, off-lattice, 15 test shapes, paired CIs. `GAP_CI_PB3_W3_30K.json`, `RISK_METRICS_PB3_W3_30K.json`, `seam_check_30K.json`, `train_offlat_fit/{PB3,W3}_30K.json`, `train_offlat_fit/B5_30Kb.json` (matched baseline, Rule 12).
