(a) **D144 as scored at 30K.**
* (i) PB3 lifts training-room 200-300 r to >= 0.75 OR cuts its LSD by >= 0.5 dB: against D144's reference (B5 @60K) **NO** (r 0.639, LSD 3.84 vs 3.83). Against the matched B5 @30K it cuts LSD by 0.51 dB, marginally YES, but W3 cuts it by 0.42 dB.
* (ii) Test-room band r >= 0.52: **NOT YET** (0.500; the CI reaches 0.530).
* (iii) W3 recovers < half of PB3's gain: **NO.** W3 recovers 54% of PB3's test-room 200-300 gain over matched B5 (0.025 / 0.046) and 82% of its training-room LSD gain.
* **"Capacity" (W3 matches PB3 within CI):** on training rooms yes (r 0.633 vs 0.639); on test rooms no at 200-300 Hz (PB3 ahead, CI excludes 0); W3 is slightly ahead at 100-200 Hz.
* **"Neither":** NO. Both beat matched B5 on training and test rooms at every band.

(b) **Reading (provisional; scored again at 60K).** Most of the gain over B5 at 30K is CAPACITY / optimisation speed: both widened pathways reach B5's 60K training-room fit by 30K. Band separation adds a small, specific TRANSFER edge at 200-300 Hz (+0.021 at 20K and +0.021 at 30K, stable) and costs about 0.01 at 100-200 Hz, the middle head's band. Rule 12: the reference condition's absolute level is reported above. Training-room 200-300 r is still only 0.64 for every model, so the fit deficit D145 identified is reduced by speed, not removed. **Seams:** PB3 1.31x at the 173 Hz window (stable since 4K), with W3 at 1.16x in the same window without a seam, so the seam-attributable part is ~0.15x; under the 2x flag. Full-band RIR intact. PB3's off-lattice in-slab 0.937 is the highest measured in P4-5 so far.

(c) **TB9b @20K A1 on its TRAINED objective (D153(d)):** 0-10 Hz **0.084**, 10-20 Hz **0.176**, >= 100 Hz 0.277 +- 0.020; under the baseline objective it read 0.991 / 0.002 / 0.003. The 0-10 Hz suppression takes the near-DC band off TB9b's step about as well as the head does (TBdc field-only @20K: 0.038 / 0.117 / 0.340). But TB9b leaves MORE of the step at 10-20 Hz (0.176 vs 0.117), the band where D146 found narrow-aperture DC non-uniformity, which a 0-10 Hz suppression does not touch. This is consistent with TB9b's weaker coupling law (slope 3.92 vs 4.57, D153(c)).

**D155 — Doorway at 30K (the resumes' endpoints): TBdc's coupling law has plateaued (slope 4.57 @14K -> 4.57 @20K -> 4.75 @30K, ratio 0.62 of GT; r^2 0.757 -> 0.739 -> 0.746). The two-region DC head's gate does NOT fire (field-only 10-20 Hz share 0.135 < 0.15), but it is close, and the field's 0-10 Hz share rose late. TB9b stays behind the head.** `DELIVERABLE.md` (regenerated with 20K and 30K columns), `trackB_eval_{TBdc,TB9b}_30K/`, `a1_{TBdc_30K,TBdc_30K_fieldonly,TB9b_30K}_C250_60K.json`.

(a) **TBdc @30K vs Track B @20K** (the published reference; no Track B 30K exists): slope 2.46 -> **4.75** (GT 7.61, ratio 0.32 -> **0.62**), r^2 0.172 -> **0.746**. Per-aperture |LD error|: 7.72 -> 4.77 dB at a = 0.15 m, 3.32 -> 1.41 dB at 1.0 m, 0.80 -> 0.39 dB open. Held-out RMS residual 2.30 -> 0.85 dB (GT 0.68). Every aperture is within 0.1 dB of the 20K figure. **Reading:** the 14K early stop rested on a flawed criterion (D150(d)), but on the deliverable it was close to a plateau. Sixteen thousand more iterations at a decaying LR bought +0.18 of slope. The remaining shortfall (0.62 of GT) is not a schedule problem at this recipe.

(b) **Two-region DC head gate (D150(g)): NOT MET as pre-registered.** TBdc's field-only A1, 14K -> 20K -> 30K: 10-20 Hz **0.197 -> 0.117 -> 0.135** (gate >= 0.15); 0-10 Hz **0.033 -> 0.038 -> 0.140**; >= 100 Hz 0.305 -> 0.340 -> 0.343. So the head is not implemented. **Flag for the manager:** the gate missed by 0.015, and the field's combined near-DC share (0-20 Hz) at 30K is 0.275, back above its 20K value of 0.155 because the 0-10 Hz share rose late in training. That is the pattern a uniform head that cannot carry a two-room difference would produce once the shared part is fitted, but it is not what the gate measured, so it is recorded, not acted on. (TBdc has no spec weights, so its trained-objective A1 is identical to its baseline one.)

(c) **TB9b @30K:** slope 3.92 (ratio 0.51), r^2 0.604, |LD error| 5.66 dB at a = 0.15 m and 0.57 dB open. A1 on its trained objective: 0-10 Hz 0.069, 10-20 Hz **0.203**, >= 100 Hz 0.299. Its 10-20 Hz share is the highest of any doorway model and its coupling law the weakest of the two fixes, consistent with D154(c). **On the doorway, the DC head (TBdc) is the recipe to carry forward;** 0-10 Hz suppression alone is not enough.

**D156 — a1 (B5r) INTERIM at the matched 30K: accuracy at or above B5, fine-scale content better placed, loud-region speckle ~10% lower but not removed.** B5r = B5's recipe on B6's per-room-receiver corpus (D150). Paired over the 15 dense test shapes, off-lattice, B5r - B5, both @30K. `GAP_CI_B5r_30K.json`, `speckle/{B5r_30K,B5_30K,B5r_20K}.json`, `train_offlat_fit/B5r_{20K,30K}.json`.
* **Accuracy.** Gate-2 off-lattice in-slab 0.915 [0.901, 0.928] vs 0.920 [0.909, 0.931], gap +0.001. Band r -0.004 [-0.011, +0.002] / -0.004 [-0.012, +0.005] / **+0.010 [+0.001, +0.019]** (25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz). Training rooms 200-300 Hz: r 0.581 vs 0.567, LSD 4.29 vs 4.34 dB.
* **Speckle (d1, primary pairs).** Band ratio -0.013 / -0.023 / -0.022 (B5r 0.936 vs B5 0.958 at 200-300 Hz). By quintile (200-300 Hz, mean excess dB per step, quiet -> loud): B5r -2.13 / +0.13 / +0.39 / +0.45 / +0.36, B5 -2.09 / +0.18 / +0.44 / +0.51 / +0.40. So the loud-region excess falls ~10% and null filling is unchanged. That is why the band ratio moves AWAY from 1: the band average mixes the two, so the quintile profile is the reading (D152(g)(4)).
* **Coherence (d2).** |c| **+0.059 [+0.041, +0.078]** at 200-300 Hz (0.547 vs 0.487) and +0.027 [+0.015, +0.040] at 100-200 Hz. Fine-scale energy ratio -0.13 / -0.16 / -0.03 (n.s. at 200-300 Hz).
* **Against the manager's a1 prediction** ("lowest speckle ratio of any arm, accuracy at or above B5"), interim: the accuracy half HOLDS at 30K. The speckle half holds only as a modest REDUCTION: per-room supervision places fine-scale content better and trims loud-region excess, but B5r still carries ~0.4 dB/step of loud-region excess and 2.3x FDTD's fine-scale energy. Scored at 60K (~10:00), with the 2 cm d6 render on B5r.

**D157 — PB3 / W3 FINAL at 60K, scored against D144: "band separation is the lever" is NOT MET as pre-registered. (i) holds, (ii) fails, (iii) splits. The result is two mechanisms: CAPACITY drives the training-room FIT (W3 recovers ~75-80% of PB3's fit gain), and BAND SEPARATION alone drives the 200-300 Hz TRANSFER to unseen rooms (W3 recovers ~17%).** Dense, off-lattice, 15 test shapes, paired CIs. `GAP_CI_PB3_W3_60K.json`, `RISK_METRICS_PB3_W3_60K.json`, `seam_check_60K.json`, `train_offlat_fit/{PB3,W3}_60K.json`, `speckle/{PB3,W3}_60K.json`.

| @60K | train r 200-300 | train LSD 200-300 | test r 25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 | test LSD 200-300 | OFF in-slab [CI] | gap in / out | excess S | full RIR | modal RIR off | seams 173 / 245 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PB3 | **0.772** | **2.88** | 0.895 / 0.647 / **0.496 [0.462, 0.533]** | 4.74 | 0.931 [0.921, 0.942] | -0.002 / +0.003 [+0.000, +0.006] | 0.0091 | 0.9998 | 0.939 | 1.17 / 1.09 |
| W3 | 0.746 | 3.12 | 0.897 / 0.646 / 0.472 [0.432, 0.512] | 4.94 | 0.921 [0.910, 0.931] | +0.002 / +0.004 | 0.0032 | 0.9998 | 0.938 | 1.05 / 1.03 |
| B5 (reference) | 0.648 | 3.83 | 0.887 / 0.631 / 0.467 [0.431, 0.501] | 5.00 | 0.924 [0.909, 0.939] | +0.001 / +0.003 | -- | 0.9998 | 0.935 | -- |

Paired test-room differences (25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300): **PB3 - W3: -0.002 [-0.014, +0.007] / +0.001 [-0.009, +0.011] / +0.024 [+0.013, +0.035]**; PB3 - B5: +0.008 / +0.016 / **+0.029 [+0.018, +0.041]**; W3 - B5: +0.010 / +0.015 / +0.005 [-0.004, +0.015].

(a) **D144 as scored.**
* **(i) HOLDS:** PB3 lifts training-room 200-300 r to 0.772 (>= 0.75) AND cuts its LSD by 0.95 dB (>= 0.5).
* **(ii) FAILS:** test-room 200-300 r is 0.496, below 0.52 (the CI reaches 0.533).
* **(iii) SPLITS.** On training rooms W3 recovers 79% of PB3's r gain (+0.098 of +0.124) and 74% of its LSD gain, so it is NOT under half. On test rooms at 200-300 Hz W3 recovers 17% (+0.005 n.s. of +0.029), which IS under half.
* "Band separation is the lever" requires all three, so it is **NOT MET**. "Capacity" (W3 within CI of PB3) holds on the training-room fit but not on 200-300 Hz transfer. "Neither" is refuted: both beat B5 on training rooms by ~0.1 r.

(b) **Reading.** Extra signal-pathway capacity (W3, PB3) buys FIT: both pass or approach 0.75 on training rooms at 200-300 Hz, against B5's 0.648. Only band separation (PB3) converts that fit into TRANSFER at 200-300 Hz (+0.029 over B5; the edge over W3 is +0.020 / +0.021 / +0.024 at 20K / 30K / 60K, stable). **Rule 12, the absolute levels:** the fit-transfer gap at 200-300 Hz WIDENED from 0.181 (B5: 0.648 vs 0.467) to 0.276 (PB3) and 0.274 (W3). Fit is no longer the dominant deficit at 200-300 Hz; transfer is. This revises D145's "~70% fit" for these recipes: the fit lever has been pulled and transfer did not follow in proportion.
* **Cost.** Seams are mild and shrinking (173 Hz window 1.31 -> 1.17, W3 1.05, so ~0.12 is seam-attributable). Excess supra-physical fraction PB3 0.0091 vs W3 0.0032. Speckle (d2): PB3's fine-scale energy is 2.15x FDTD's against W3's 1.84x and B5's 2.55x. Coherence vs B5 +0.022 [+0.014, +0.031] (PB3) and +0.053 [+0.041, +0.064] (W3). Loud-region excess 0.45 / 0.35 dB per step (PB3), 0.45 / 0.36 (W3), 0.51 / 0.39 (B5). Neither pathway adds speckle relative to B5. PB3 has a tiny out-slab gap, +0.003 [+0.000, +0.006]. Full-band RIR is intact.
* **Implication for the manager** (not acted on): the 200-300 Hz ceiling is now a TRANSFER problem on these recipes. The levers that move transfer are band separation (+0.029) and, from D75, corpus density along the shape parameters. PB3 off-lattice in-slab 0.931 is within CI of B5.

**D158 — B10 (B4's 4x receivers + B1's 1/RMS weights) at 60K: density adds NOTHING on top of B1's loss. B10 equals B1 at every band, is below B4 and B5 below 200 Hz, and, like B1, DECLINES from 30K to 60K. Density alone (B4) is the only arm still climbing at 60K, and B1's weighting removes that climb.** Dense, off-lattice, 15 test shapes, paired CIs. `GAP_CI_B10_60K.json`, `RISK_METRICS_B10_60K.json`, `train_offlat_fit/B10_60K.json`.
* **Level.** Gate-2 off-lattice in-slab 0.870 [0.858, 0.882] PASS, gap -0.000. **Band-mean r (test rooms): 0.848 / 0.602 / 0.443 [0.403, 0.485]** (25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz). Band LSD 2.01 / 4.41 / 5.11 dB. Full-band RIR 0.9997; modal RIR off-lattice 0.922; excess S 0.0083. Training rooms 200-300 Hz: r 0.722, LSD 3.38 (B1 0.727 / 3.32).
* **Complementarity (D141's question).** B10 - B1: -0.006 [-0.011, -0.000] / -0.007 [-0.016, +0.001] / -0.005 [-0.015, +0.005], i.e. equal. B10 - B4: -0.042 / -0.020 / +0.001 [-0.020, +0.022]. B10 - B5: -0.039 / -0.029 / -0.024 [-0.036, -0.012]. **D141/D142's 20K reading holds to 60K: density with B1's loss is a substitute, not a complement.**
* **Trajectory, 30K -> 60K (paired).** B10 -0.005 / -0.015 / **-0.016 [-0.026, -0.005]**; B1 -0.003 / -0.012 / **-0.013 [-0.023, -0.003]**; **B4 +0.013 / +0.029 / +0.054 [+0.038, +0.068]**. The arms carrying B1's 1/RMS weights LOSE transfer in late training at 100-300 Hz; density on the baseline loss gains it. Labelled inference: 1/RMS weighting up-weights the high bins ~5-13x (D96), and late in the schedule that buys training-room fit (B1/B10 train r 0.72-0.73, the highest of the single-change arms) at the cost of transfer. That is the same fit-without-transfer pattern D157 found for capacity. B4's late climb still leaves it at 0.442 at 200-300 Hz, below B5's 0.467.
* **The manager's reset prediction (D137: 0.38-0.42 at the top of the band, 295-300 Hz):** **0.380 [0.319, 0.440]**, inside the range at its lower edge. The 200-300 Hz band value, the figure to quote, is 0.443. For reference at 295-300 Hz: B1 0.374, B4 0.353, B5 0.396.
* **Consequence.** B5 (DC head) remains the best single recipe at 60K across all three bands among the DC-fixed arms without extra capacity. B9b ties it and is slightly ahead at 200-300 Hz (D151). PB3 adds +0.029 at 200-300 Hz on top (D157). The density lever's late gain is real but appears only WITHOUT B1's weights. If density is pursued (D75), pair it with the DC head (B5-style), not with 1/RMS weights. That arm is untested: a candidate for the manager, not launched.

**D159 — a1 (B5r) FINAL at 60K: the manager's prediction holds on accuracy (B5r = B5 within CI at every band) and FAILS on speckle. Per-room supervision places fine-scale content better (coherence +0.071) but does not reduce the visible speckle at 60K: the 30K reduction closed, and the 2 cm ratio is unchanged. Helmholtz gate condition 2 is therefore met ("a1 did not remove the speckle"); condition 1 still fails (D152(c)).** Paired over the 15 dense test shapes, off-lattice, B5r - B5, both @60K. `GAP_CI_B5r_60K.json`, `RISK_METRICS_B5r_60K.json`, `speckle/B5r_60K.json`, `speckle/d6_shape1000_2cm_B5r_60K.{png,json}`, `train_offlat_fit/B5r_60K.json`.
* **Accuracy.** Gate-2 off-lattice in-slab 0.921 [0.907, 0.934] vs 0.924 [0.909, 0.939], gap -0.001. Band r **-0.006 [-0.014, +0.000] / -0.004 [-0.013, +0.005] / -0.003 [-0.013, +0.006]**; band LSD 2.03 / 4.28 / 5.01 dB. Full-band RIR 0.9998, modal RIR off-lattice 0.935. Training rooms 200-300 Hz: r 0.661 vs 0.648, LSD 3.74 vs 3.83. B5r is flat from 30K to 60K (-0.002 / +0.002 / -0.000). **"Accuracy at or above B5": HOLDS as "at".** The +0.010 edge at 200-300 Hz seen at 30K (D156) did not survive to 60K.
* **Speckle, 64x64 grid (d1, primary pairs).** Band ratio -0.018 / -0.032 / -0.020 (B5r 0.948 vs B5 0.968 at 200-300 Hz). Quintile excess (dB per step, quiet -> loud): B5r -2.11 / +0.17 / +0.42 / +0.48 / +0.39, B5 -2.04 / +0.22 / +0.47 / +0.51 / +0.39. The loudest quintile is identical, so the ~10% loud-region reduction at 30K has closed.
* **Speckle, 2 cm (d6, shape 1000; one shape, no CI).** B5r d1 **1.54 at 200 Hz and 1.25 at 280 Hz**, against B5's 1.45 and 1.27. Not reduced.
* **Fine-scale content (d2).** Coherence |c| **+0.071 [+0.051, +0.092]** at 200-300 Hz (0.526 vs 0.455) and +0.034 at 100-200 Hz. Mask-aware fine-scale energy ratio 2.44x vs 2.55x FDTD (-0.105 [-0.297, +0.034], n.s.). The zero-fill excess supra-physical fraction (D108's S4 measure) falls 0.0102 -> 0.0034. The two measures disagree because the zero-fill one includes mask-edge leakage (D152(c)); the mask-aware one is primary for fine-scale content.
* **Against the a1 prediction** ("lowest speckle ratio of any arm, with accuracy at or above B5, because supervision now covers the whole plane"): **accuracy HOLDS; speckle FAILS.** Per-room receivers move the fine-scale content toward the right place but not toward the right amount. The visible 2 cm speckle is set elsewhere. The renderer-side projection (D152(f)) remains the only measured remedy for the speckle itself.
* **Premise correction.** The prediction cited B6 as "the lowest excess fine-scale energy measured (-0.023)". That is B6 @20K. The same measure reads B6 +0.0285 @10K, -0.0226 @20K, +0.0038 @30K and **+0.0301 @60K**, the highest of B5 / B5r / B6 at 60K (0.0102 / 0.0034 / 0.0301). On the mask-aware measure B6 @60K is 4.2x FDTD (D152).
* **Helmholtz gate (D150(b)) after a1:** condition 2 ("a1/a2 did not remove the speckle") is now MET: a1 did not, and a2 was not run (gate negative). Condition 1 (d2 |c| <= 0.1) still FAILS (0.455 for B5, 0.526 for B5r). As pre-registered, the Helmholtz arm is NOT queued. Manager's call (D152(g)(3)).

**D160 — P4-5 CLOSED; P4-6 (geometry transfer) OPENED. The manager's correction to D145 recorded; Step 0 housekeeping done; t1 / arm pre-registrations fixed before any number is seen.**

(a) **The manager's correction, recorded.** D145's "about 70% fit" assumed that fit and transfer add. D157 shows that extra capacity mostly WIDENS the train-to-test gap: training-room 200-300 Hz r went from 0.648 to 0.772, while test went only from 0.467 to 0.496, and the gap from 0.18 to 0.28. **The binding limit at 200-300 Hz is geometry generalisation.** Transfer to new shapes is also the roadmap blocker (U room at 100 Hz, r 0.06). From P4-6 on, every arm reports in-family test metrics AND out-of-family metrics.

(b) **Storage (manager-approved).** Keep-list written to the repo FIRST (`outputs/p4_6/housekeeping/ckpt_keeplist.{json,md}`, `scripts/p4_6_ckpt_keeplist.py`): for each finished P4-5 arm, the final checkpoint, the 30K one, and every checkpoint cited in DECISIONS.md or a figure manifest. Citation forms: explicit path, `ARM @NK`, `ARM_NK`, and arm lists preceding `@NK` / `at NK`, matched generously. **All 47 kept checkpoints load through `load_model` (0 bad, `ckpt_keeplist_verify.json`), then 40.3 GB was deleted** (19 GB of checkpoints remain). Regenerable caches deleted: dense render cache 25 GB, A2 cache 9.2 GB, speckle projection cache 1.3 GB. **One deliberate, temporary exception:** the B5 @60K and PB3 @60K dense-test caches (1.0 GB) are kept until t1 has used them as its test-room spectra, which saves two ~17-minute re-renders; they are deleted after t1. Corpora, the dense test set and D111's dense family rooms were not touched. Free space 32 -> 66 GB.

(c) **validate() fixed** (D150(d)): the trainer now renders validation with `renderer.eval()` (no fan jitter) and restores training mode after. Test (`tests/test_p4_6_trainer_fixes.py`, Rule 8): two validations at fixed weights now agree EXACTLY, the render saw eval mode, training mode is restored, and the same stub render in training mode does NOT repeat (the test's teeth). Val LSD curves of P4-6 arms are therefore deterministic and NOT comparable point-for-point with P4-5 val curves; no reported number uses them.

(d) **Checkpoint retention for new arms:** "every 10K plus the final" via new options `ckpt_keep_every: 10000` and `ckpt_keep_last: 1` (the newest, which becomes the final). **One addition, my judgment:** `ckpt_keep_iters: "4000"`, because the default recipe's early kill signal (D135/D137) reads the 4K checkpoint, which the 10K rule would delete at 6K. Pruning is a tested function (`_prune_ckpts`); with the new options off it is exactly the old rule.

(e) **Render-time physical projection is the default for every figure and deliverable render** (D152(d5)). It now lives in `aaf/eval/physical_projection.py` (`project_field`; least-squares band limit at 1.5 k0, bins >= 25 Hz). It is labelled POST-PROCESSING, and every report shows raw and projected metrics side by side. Test: physical plane waves kept to < 1e-3, bins below 25 Hz bit-identical, and the D152 diagnostics use the same function.

(f) **g2 redefined (user decision).** FAM never held out a family: it trained on all five (rect, L, U, T, DN; 60 each) and held out 10 instances per family. So "FAM's held-out family" did not exist. **g2 holds out U as a whole:** `configs/sweeps_2d_mat/p4_6_family_holdU_manifest.json` is FAM's manifest with every U row's split set to `test_family`, giving 240 train (rect / L / T / DN), 40 in-family test and 70 out-of-family U. No data rebuilt. g2's training-room fit (and its 4K kill signal) needs off-lattice receivers in FAMILY training rooms, which do not exist yet. I will build a small set (FDTD re-simulation of a fixed subset of family training rooms, receivers drawn per room) before g2's 4K read.

(g) **Arms (config-only; corpora verified complete on disk).** Common recipe: PB3 = B5 + per-band heads, or B5 for g4; every change listed in each config's header.
* **g1** `P4_6_G1.yaml`: PB3 on the 500-shape corpus (C500's), 60K. **Pre-registered (manager):** if transfer is data-limited, test-room 200-300 Hz r >= 0.53 (PB3 @250: 0.496) AND the training-to-test gap falls below 0.22. If neither moves, shape count along the current families is not the lever, and the next step is conditioning.
* **g2** `P4_6_G2.yaml`: PB3 on the family corpus with U held out, 60K. Report the held-out family per band beside the in-family families.
* **g3** `P4_6_G3.yaml`: PB3 + 4x receivers (B4's corpus, no spec weights), 120K cosine, val_max_configs 10 as B4, RTX A6000 (D103). Reads at 30K, 60K and 120K; the 60K read against PB3 @60K gives density's contribution. **Pre-registered (manager):** test-room 200-300 Hz r >= 0.52 by 120K.
* **g4** `P4_6_G4.yaml` (= `P4_5_B5L6.yaml`): hash grid capped at 6 levels. **Pre-registered (manager):** training-room 200-300 Hz fit FALLS, and test-room r stays within CI of B5 or rises, so the transfer gap shrinks. Speckle d1 is also reported.
* **Gated, not launched:** the two-region DC head (gate: t3) and inverse-span geometry features (gate: t1 shows misplacement).

(h) **t1 lag test, operationalised before computing** (the manager's criteria; the thresholds are mine). Models: PB3 and B5 @60K. Rooms:
* TEST rooms: the 15 dense test shapes, off-lattice receivers.
* TRAINING rooms: the fixed 23 training rooms of D125 at B6-corpus receivers off the lattice, as in `p4_5_train_offlat_fit`.

Per receiver: dB spectra (prediction, FDTD) over windows of +-25 Hz centred at 125 Hz and 250 Hz, each mean-removed. The lag is the argmax of their normalised cross-correlation over +-10 Hz (+-20 bins), refined by a parabola; positive means the prediction's structure sits ABOVE FDTD's in frequency. Per room: median lag m_r and sign consistency s_r (the fraction of receivers with the sign of m_r). A room is **systematic** if |m_r| >= 0.5 Hz (one bin) and s_r >= 0.70.

**"Mode misplacement" is declared only if ALL of:**
1. >= 2/3 of test rooms are systematic at 250 Hz;
2. across test rooms systematic at both centres, the median of |m_r(250)| / |m_r(125)| lies in [1.5, 2.5];
3. on training rooms, the median |m_r| <= 0.25 Hz at both centres and <= 1/3 of training rooms are systematic.

If declared, report dL_eff = L * lag / f in cm per test room. Otherwise the transfer error is field SHAPE, not placement.

(i) **t3 gate (manager), operationalised.** For TBdc @30K at a = 0.15 m (6 unseen test geometries), the per-band inter-room level difference (as the Track B evaluation: 20 log10 of the mean |H| ratio, room B over room A) over 0-20 / 20-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz, prediction against FDTD. "Most of the error sits in 0-20 Hz" means 0-20 Hz carries > 50% of the summed per-band |LD error|. Then the two-region head is queued. Otherwise the problem is the aperture representation (A0c: apertures want dx <= 0.01), and the head is not the fix.

(j) **Plumbing:** the training script strips any `p4_N_` prefix from run_id (P4-6 arms live in `outputs/p4_6/arms/<G>`), and `p4_5_eval_one.sh` takes `ARMS=` for the arms root. Both are unchanged for P4-5 paths.

**D161 — t3 RESULT: the narrow-doorway error is in the ACOUSTIC band, not near DC. The two-region DC head is NOT the fix and is not queued. The problem is the aperture representation (A0c: apertures want dx <= 0.01).** TBdc @30K, a = 0.15 m, 6 unseen test geometries; inter-room level difference per band, the Track B evaluator's own `band_level_ratio` and rendering (`scripts/p4_6_doorway_bands.py`, `outputs/p4_6/doorway_bands/doorway_bands_a015.json`).

| model | mean abs LD error 0-20 / 20-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz | pooled 20-300 (deliverable) | share of per-band error, 0-20 / 20-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TBdc @30K** | **0.30** / 4.00 / 4.76 / **8.16** dB | 4.77 dB | **0.02** / 0.23 / 0.28 / **0.47** |
| Track B @20K (reference) | 0.26 / 6.99 / 7.58 / 9.87 dB | 7.72 dB | 0.01 / 0.28 / 0.31 / 0.40 |

**Gate (D160(i)): 0-20 Hz must carry > 50% of the per-band error. It carries 2%, so FAILS.** The near-DC inter-room level is already right to 0.3 dB, with or without the head. The error grows with frequency, and the head fixed the 20-300 Hz part that it fixed (D147) by freeing the gradient (D149), not by carrying the room A / room B DC difference. D155(b)'s late rise in the field's 0-20 Hz A1 share is therefore not a sign of a mis-specified head that matters to the deliverable. **Consequence:** the two-region DC head is closed. The narrow-doorway deficit is an acoustic-band representation problem (the aperture is 0.15 m against a 0.02 m training grid and the 64-ray / 64-point renderer; A0c's note that apertures want dx <= 0.01). That is the manager's call for a later chunk.

**D162 — t1 and t2 RESULTS: (t1) the transfer error is field SHAPE, not mode placement, so inverse-span geometry features are NOT launched. (t2) Out of family, the model fails most at LOW frequency (25-100 Hz r falls by ~0.30; LSD rises from 1.8 to 4.6-4.8 dB), and band separation does not help there.** Both on PB3 and B5 @60K; `outputs/p4_6/t1_lag/t1_lag.json`, `outputs/p4_6/family_oof/{PB3_60K,B5_60K}.json`.

(a) **t1 lag test** (D160(h), as pre-registered). Values are PB3 / B5; "room-median lag" is the median over rooms of each room's median lag.

| set | centre | room-median lag | systematic rooms | sign consistency | per-receiver mean abs lag | peak corr |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| test (15) | 125 Hz | +0.04 / -0.26 Hz | 5/15 / 5/15 | 0.65 / 0.65 | 2.1 / 2.1 Hz | 0.72 / 0.72 |
| test (15) | 250 Hz | +0.04 / -0.09 Hz | **0/15 / 0/15** | 0.59 / 0.57 | **3.7 / 4.2 Hz** | 0.58 / 0.55 |
| train (23) | 125 Hz | -0.07 / -0.07 Hz | 0 / 0 | 0.61 / 0.61 | 0.5 / 0.6 Hz | 0.89 / 0.86 |
| train (23) | 250 Hz | -0.02 / -0.06 Hz | 0 / 0 | 0.56 / 0.57 | 1.0 / 2.0 Hz | 0.78 / 0.67 |

**Verdict: NOT mode misplacement** for either model. Condition (1) fails: 0/15 test rooms are systematic at 250 Hz. Condition (2) is undefined (no room is systematic at both centres). Condition (3) holds for PB3 and narrowly fails for B5 (training median 0.29 Hz at 250 Hz). In test rooms the per-receiver spectral misalignment is large (3.7-4.2 Hz at 250 Hz, about 4x the training rooms') but its SIGN is near-random within a room (consistency 0.57-0.59, against 0.5 for chance). A single effective room-size error would shift every receiver the same way. **Per the pre-registration, the transfer error is field shape, not placement.** No dL_eff is reported, and the gated inverse-span geometry features are not launched.

(b) **t2 out-of-family baseline** (D111's dense family rooms, two pre-registered median rooms per family; off-lattice; raw, with the render-time projection beside it).

| model | group | r 25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 | LSD 25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 (dB) | projected r 25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PB3 | in-family reference (rect, L) | 0.902 / 0.658 / 0.455 | 1.84 / 4.20 / 5.03 | 0.901 / 0.659 / 0.457 |
| PB3 | **out of family (T, DN, U)** | **0.600 / 0.347 / 0.329** | **4.83 / 5.87 / 5.67** | 0.601 / 0.346 / 0.331 |
| B5 | in-family reference | 0.897 / 0.639 / 0.458 | 1.90 / 4.30 / 5.12 | 0.898 / 0.640 / 0.460 |

Per family (PB3 raw, 25-100 / 200-300): rect 0.888 / 0.400, L 0.916 / 0.509, T 0.591 / 0.337, DN 0.580 / 0.315, U 0.631 / 0.336. **U at 100 Hz:** PB3 +0.112 (rooms +0.196 and +0.028), B5 +0.235 (+0.235 and +0.235), consistent with the roadmap's "U at 100 Hz, r 0.06".

(c) **Reading.**
* (1) **In family, the deficit is at 200-300 Hz** (transfer gap 0.28, D157). **Out of family, the collapse is at LOW frequency:** 25-100 Hz r falls by 0.27-0.30 and LSD rises ~3 dB. That is the band the DC fix made excellent in family. An unseen corner topology breaks the low-order modal field, not just the dense high band.
* (2) PB3's band separation does not transfer across families (25-100 0.600 vs B5's 0.624; 200-300 0.329 vs 0.314). What it bought in family (+0.029 at 200-300 Hz, D157) is a within-family gain.
* (3) Projection changes nothing measurable here (+-0.003): the out-of-family error is not supra-physical content.
* (4) Together with t1: across the family boundary the field SHAPE is wrong, and conditioning on the new topology does not reach the low-order modes. This bears on g2 (U held out) and points to conditioning or representation, not data volume along the current families. g1 tests the data volume directly.

(d) **Housekeeping.** The B5 / PB3 @60K dense caches kept for t1 (D160(b)) are now deleted.

G2 is a family-corpus model scored on the 15 L test shapes. No arm opens a gap. All run on. `outputs/p4_6/GAP_CI_4K.json`.

G3 was moved to a scavenger **L40S** (48 GB, Ada). tinycudann's sm52 build JIT-runs there with CutlassMLP, as on the A5000 / A6000. Throughput is 0.64-0.67 s/it and the loss trajectory is normal (1.31 -> 1.02 by 800 its, as the other arms).

**D164 — P4-7 PREP (physics priors for unseen geometry), PRE-REGISTERED before any p1-p3 number. No training launches.** The manager's spec; operationalisations are mine where noted.
* **(p1) Orientation audit.** Notch corner / side counts per corpus and split (C250, C500, the 15 test shapes, family), plus every other asymmetric feature: source position, receiver-lattice anchoring, wall alphas, L > W. If notches sit predominantly in one corner, that is recorded as a likely contributor to the out-of-family failure (D162).
* **(p2) Scale invariance, verified before use.**
  * **Wall model.** The FDTD wall model is the frequency-INDEPENDENT Kowalczyk-van Walstijn boundary (`fdtd_2d.py:162-196`: real scalar admittance xi = (1-R)/(1+R), R = sqrt(1-alpha)). In 2D, H_alpha(f; alpha x) = H(alpha f; x) for the pulse-free transfer function, i.e. h_alpha(t) = h(t/alpha)/alpha, with no amplitude factor in frequency.
  * **Rooms.** 3 C250 training rooms, chosen by rule: the smallest notch-snapping error on the scaled grids, over alpha in {0.8, 0.9}.
  * **Simulations.** Each room simulated directly at alpha = 0.8 and 0.9 (corpus dx / fs / n, source at alpha*SRC).
  * **Comparison.** Against the augmentation of the original's `H_deconv`: time-domain h(t/alpha)/alpha with zero padding to the record length. ~200 receivers per room per alpha, at original nodes whose scaled images are within 1 mm of a scaled-grid node. Bands 0-20 (split 0-10 / 10-20), 20-100, 100-200, 200-300 Hz.
  * **PASS (thresholds mine):** in every band above 20 Hz, for all 3 rooms x 2 alphas, relative complex L2 <= 2x D94's dx 0.02-vs-0.01 floor (<= 1.6% / 3.8% / 4.6% for 20-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz) AND dB spatial r >= 0.99 (D94's criterion).
  * **Control.** alpha = 1 must give ~0.
  * **Expected.** Deviations confined to near 0 Hz (the 2 s window), reported with the original record's energy in its last (1-alpha)T.
  * **If it fails above 20 Hz:** stop, do not implement scale augmentation, and report why.
* **(p3) Geometry sensitivity.**
  * **Room.** D111's U room `UL5.00_W5.26_NEd0.14w0.48x0.00_NWd1.22w1.86x0.00` with its 14 cm NE notch removed, an L with the same NW notch. Simulated at the U's own 3680 receivers.
  * **Metrics.** At 50 / 100 / 200 Hz (single bins 100 / 200 / 400, and +-2.5 Hz means), over off-lattice receivers: GT change dT = mean |dB T_L - dB T_U|, model change dP = mean |dB P_L - dB P_U| for PB3 and B5 @60K, the ratio dP/dT, and the spatial Pearson between the change fields.
  * **Verdict (threshold mine):** "the encoder reacts to segment count, not physical size" if dP/dT >= 3 at >= 2 of the 3 frequencies, for either model. Then arc-length boundary sampling is the recommended fix.
  * **Confound, reported:** the L version's d_hat 0.515 lies inside C250's held-out slab, and L < W.
* **(p4) Loader work, behind flags, default off and default path untouched.**
  * **D4.** All 8 elements are supported. The DEFAULT is the flips {fx, fy, r180}, because the transposing elements map notch width to depth and leak C250 / C500 / holdU's held-out d_hat slab (measured in design: 88/500 and 43/240 rooms). They are refused on a slab corpus unless the manager decides otherwise.
  * **Reciprocity swaps.** Exact in rectangles, approximate in notched rooms (the D65 wall-tip residual). A corpus-resolution pre-flight measures it.
  * **Scale.** Only if p2 passes.
  * **Test.** The manager's: a 90 deg rotation (and all 8 elements) of a room maps its FDTD field exactly.
* **(p5)** Wall impedance beta per material, from the FDTD boundary model, recorded for a later wall-condition loss.

**D165 — P4-7 PREP RESULTS, scored against D164.**
* **p1:** every notch in the L corpora is NW, and every room has the same source and a SW-anchored receiver lattice. Recorded as a likely contributor to the out-of-family failure.
* **p2: FAILS above 20 Hz**, because the FDTD record carries a non-decaying compliance term. Scale augmentation is NOT implemented.
* **p3:** the encoder is NOT segment-count sensitive by the pre-registered rule, but its response to a geometry edit lands in the wrong places.
* **p5:** the wall model is frequency-independent, with beta = tanh(m/4).

(a) **p1 orientation audit** (`outputs/p4_7/p1_orientation.{json,md}`).
* **C250, C500 and the 15 test shapes:** notch at **NW in 100%** of notched rooms (242 / 492 / 15; the rest are 8 rectangles).
* **Family corpus:** NW 180 / NE 60 / midN 60 / SE 60 notch sides in train. There is no SW, W-mid, E-mid or S-mid (by construction, so the source stays in air; `multi_notch.py:16-28`).
* **Every room, every corpus:**
  * source at (0.5, 1.6), in the SW quadrant, never adjacent to a notch (nearest reflex corner >= 0.66 m, median 2.4-2.5 m);
  * receivers 100% on the 0.12 + 0.06 k lattice anchored at the SW origin;
  * every wall alpha 0.15; L > W in 94-100%.
* **Recorded as a likely contributor to the out-of-family failure (D162):** an L-corpus model has never seen a reflex corner outside NW, nor any source-relative geometry other than "source SW, notch NW", and T, DN and U all place notches where it has not.

(b) **p2 scale invariance: FAILS as pre-registered** (`outputs/p4_7/p2_scale.json`). Above 20 Hz, relative L2 is **1.22-2.01** (threshold <= 0.016-0.046) and dB r ~0.2-0.36, in all 3 rooms x 2 alphas. The alpha = 1 control is exactly 0, so the pipeline is correct. **Why** (`p2_scale_diagnostics.json`, labelled diagnostics; they do not change the verdict):
* (1) The pulse-free FDTD response carries a **spatially uniform static (compliance) offset that never decays**: c0 x N ~ 920-1100, the D96/D97 near-DC term. It is 27-41% of the record's energy beyond alpha T. With it removed, only 0.1-0.6% remains, so the propagating field decays well inside the window. The zero-padded stretch truncates the offset into a step at alpha T, and the step's ~1/f leakage swamps the modal field at every frequency.
* (2) Removing the offset (estimated from the last 0.25 s) leaves 25-36% error. Matching the windows (the direct record also cut at alpha T) leaves **16-18% at alpha = 0.9 (r 0.93-0.95) and 29-34% at alpha = 0.8 (r 0.85-0.88)**, a residual roughly proportional to the scale change.
* **Labelled inference:** the static term's level is set by the room's compliance, not by the geometric law, so it cannot be stretched. The residual is consistent with that mismatch.
* **Consequence (as pre-registered: "if it fails above 20 Hz, stop and report why"):** scale augmentation is NOT implemented and no scale flags are added. A future attempt would have to remove the compliance term first, e.g. on the field after the DC head's 0-20 Hz term, and re-verify on the propagating part only.

(c) **p3 geometry sensitivity** (`outputs/p4_7/p3_sensitivity.json`). D111's U room against the same room minus its 14 cm NE notch (8 -> 6 tokens), at the U's 3680 receivers (3285 off-lattice). Ground-truth change: 0.54 / 1.58 / 2.67 dB at 50 / 100 / 200 Hz. Model change ratio dP/dT: PB3 **3.10** / 1.13 / 0.76; B5 1.73 / 1.56 / 1.22.
* **Verdict: NOT segment-count sensitive** for either model (>= 3 at >= 2 of 3 frequencies is required; PB3 meets it only at 50 Hz). Arc-length boundary sampling is not indicated by this test.
* **The finding: the spatial correlation between the model's change field and the true change field is ~0 (-0.10 to +0.18)** at every frequency, for both models, and unchanged far from the notch. The models change the field by about the right AMOUNT when a corner is removed, but not in the right PLACES. This is the same "field shape" signature as t1 and t2 (D162).
* The ground truth itself is more sensitive than the notch's size suggests (1.6-2.7 dB at 100-200 Hz over the whole room).
* Confound, reported: the L version is in C250's held-out slab and has L < W.

(d) **p5 wall impedance** (`outputs/p4_7/p5_wall_impedance.{json,md}`).
* The FDTD wall is locally reacting and **frequency-independent**: beta = xi = (1-R)/(1+R) = **tanh(m/4)**, with R = sqrt(1-alpha) and m = -ln(1-alpha).
* Robin condition in the repo's rfft convention: **dp/dn_out = -i k beta p**.
* beta = 0.0406 for every P4 wall (alpha 0.15); 0.0128 concrete; 0.172 curtain; 0.292 absorber; 0.58-0.63 window.

(e) **p4 status.**
* **Phase A:** `aaf/data/augment_2d.py` + 24 CPU tests pass. Includes the manager's FDTD test over all 8 D4 elements (e / fx bit-exact, the rest <= 1e-11), with teeth; reciprocity is exact in a rectangle, bounded in a notched room (the D65 residual), and off by a factor of 2 at wall nodes.
* **Phase B:** trainer wiring behind default-off flags (`aug_d4_p`, `aug_d4_elems` default "fx,fy,r180", `aug_recip_p`, `aug_seed`). Being tested in a worktree, including a frozen-copy check that the OFF path of `_step` is bit-identical. It merges only when the full suite passes.
* **Phase C (scale): not implemented** (p2 failed).

**D166 — p4 COMPLETE (phases A + B; scale dropped per D165) and the P4-6 30K interim.**

(a) **p4 phase B, merged** (c9e49da). Trainer flags, all default-off:
* `aug_d4_p`;
* `aug_d4_elems`, default "fx,fy,r180";
* `aug_recip_p`;
* `aug_seed`.

When every probability is 0, `_step` runs its original lines, nothing is imported or drawn, and the default train_meta is unchanged. The draws use a private CPU generator, and per-config transforms keep the AABB group count unchanged.

**Refusals:**
* sigma_aux;
* aperture configs;
* a non-token cond_source;
* transposing elements that leak a corpus's held-out d_hat slab;
* wall-node receivers with swaps on;
* a per-file source that differs from the global one.

The resume guard covers the new keys.

**Tests:** 72/73 pass across the trainer, augmentation, P4-6 and P4-5 suites. The one skip is the dense cache deleted in D160(b). They include a **frozen verbatim copy of the pre-change `_step`**: with flags off, three steps on a CPU stub give identical losses, parameters and CPU / CUDA RNG states (teeth: one extra global draw is detected). Also tested:
* identity draws reproduce `_render` bit-for-bit;
* the swap exchanges rx / tx with an unchanged target;
* transformed rows lie inside their transformed boxes (teeth: untransposed boxes fail);
* the integration test: off and on trainers on the family corpus hold identical default tensors.

The worktree was removed without touching corpora or checkpoints (verified).

(b) **Pre-flights.**
* **Smoke run** (G2's config, 200 its, aug_d4_p 0.5 with {fx, fy, r180}, aug_recip_p 0.25): runs at **0.76 s/it**, against 0.80 for the unaugmented G2, so there is no throughput cost. `outputs/p4_7/p4_smoke_aug_log.txt`.
* **Reciprocity at corpus resolution** (one D111 room per family; source (0.5, 1.6) against 10 interior receivers each; `outputs/p4_7/p4_reciprocity.json`):
  * rectangle exact (in-band relative L2 ~5e-13);
  * notched rooms (L, T, DN, U): median 2e-4 to 5e-3 per band, max 1.0e-2.
  * The swap's label error is therefore <= 1%, two orders below the model's error (LSD 4-5 dB). Swaps are usable.

The render-time projection changes every out-of-family r by <= 0.002 (raw and projected in the JSONs).

(a) **g1, pre-registered: "data-limited" if test 200-300 Hz r >= 0.53 AND the train-to-test gap < 0.22. BOTH HOLD:** 0.581 and 0.094.
* Paired G1 - PB3: **+0.036 [+0.022, +0.055] / +0.074 [+0.050, +0.099] / +0.085 [+0.069, +0.100].**
* Doubling the shape count (250 -> 500 L rooms, still all NW-notched) lowers the training-room fit (0.772 -> 0.675) and raises test transfer: the gap closes by two thirds.
* g1 is the best model in the project: Gate-2 0.955, LSD < 100 Hz 1.17 dB, 200-300 Hz 4.28 dB, modal RIR 0.956.
* **Out of family it moves only slightly:** T / DN / U 0.628 / 0.372 / 0.358 vs PB3 0.600 / 0.347 / 0.329 (+0.03 per band), though U at 100 Hz rises from +0.11 to +0.42. More shapes ALONG the family fix in-family transfer; they barely touch the cross-family deficit (25-100 Hz: in-family 0.922 vs out-of-family 0.628).

(b) **g4, pre-registered: training-room fit falls, test r within CI of B5 or higher, gap shrinks. REFUTED.**
* Training-room 200-300 r 0.644 vs B5 0.648: unchanged.
* Test r is slightly LOWER: G4 - B5 -0.004 [-0.012, +0.003] / -0.013 [-0.021, -0.004] / -0.010 [-0.019, -0.002].
* Gap 0.188 vs 0.181: unchanged.
* Speckle d1 is unchanged too (0.946 vs 0.968 raw; loud-quintile excess 0.48 / 0.38 vs 0.51 / 0.39). A 6-level grid (finest cell 1.65 cm) can still carry the 2-4 cm speckle, and it neither cures it nor helps transfer.
* The fine hash levels are not where geometry generalisation is lost. Together with D152(d), the 6-level cap is closed.

(c) **g2, the roadmap test (does the fixed recipe transfer to a family it never saw?): NO.**
* **Held-out U:** 0.554 / 0.257 / 0.294 at 25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz; U at 100 Hz +0.16.
* **In-family held-out instances:** rect 0.961 / 0.846 / 0.701, L 0.789 / 0.441 / 0.257, T 0.808 / 0.596 / 0.508, DN 0.780 / 0.422 / 0.276; mean **0.835 / 0.576 / 0.436**.
* U sits 0.28 / 0.32 / 0.14 below the in-family mean. It is WORSE than PB3, which never saw any two-notch room (U 0.631 / 0.320 / 0.336), despite G2 having trained on two-notch families (T, DN).
* Within-family coverage helps strongly: G2 on T 0.808 vs PB3 0.591; rect 0.961 vs 0.888. Unseen-family transfer does not follow from seen two-notch families.
* G2 on the 15 L test shapes (0.774 / 0.475 / 0.309; Gate-2 0.832) is below PB3, consistent with its 60 L rooms against C250's 242. Its train-to-test gap there (0.48) mixes family rooms with L-corpus test shapes, so it is reported, not interpreted.

(d) **Reading for the chunk** (labelled inference, the manager's call).
* Along a family, transfer is data-limited and doubling the corpus closes most of the 200-300 Hz gap (g1).
* Across families it is not: neither more L shapes (g1) nor seen two-notch families (g2) transfer to U. This agrees with t1 / t2 / p3 (the field SHAPE is wrong out of family) and p1 (the corpora never place a notch outside a fixed set of positions relative to the fixed source).
* The P4-7 lever this points to is orientation / configuration diversity (D4 flips, now in the loader, D166) and conditioning, not representation capacity (g4) or density (g3 at 30K = PB3; scored at 60K and 120K).

**D168 — G3 (PB3 + 4x receivers, 120K cosine) at 60K: density adds NOTHING to band separation's transfer (G3 - PB3 +0.002 / -0.005 / -0.005, all n.s.); it raises training-room fit (0.798 vs 0.772) and so WIDENS the train-to-test gap (0.307 vs 0.276), the same pattern as capacity (D157).** The pre-registered threshold (test 200-300 Hz r >= 0.52) is scored at 120K. G3 @60K reads 0.491, so it needs +0.03 from the remaining half of the schedule.
* Gate-2 0.929 [0.920, 0.938], gap -0.000. Test r 0.897 / 0.642 / 0.491, LSD 1.88 / 4.17 / 4.79 dB. Modal RIR off 0.940, full RIR 0.9998.
* Out of family: T / DN / U 0.593 / 0.374 / 0.327; rect / L 0.899 / 0.651 / 0.473. U at 100 Hz +0.33.
* Speckle d1 0.963 raw / 0.910 projected; abs c 0.447.
* G3 is mid-schedule (LR 1.28e-4 of a 120K cosine), so the 60K comparison with PB3's END-of-schedule 60K favours PB3. The density contribution the manager asked for ("the 60K read against PB3 @60K") is nonetheless nil within CI.
* With D158 (B4 vs B10) and D167 (G1): along-family transfer responds to more SHAPES (G1 +0.085), not to more RECEIVERS per shape (G3 ~0).

**D169 — P4-7 opens (manager, 2026-10-16): configuration diversity (m2, m4) and a physics modal prior (m1, gated m3). PRE-REGISTRATION, written before any m1 / m2 / m4 number exists.** The premise (manager): in family, 200-300 Hz transfer is data-limited (g1, D167); out of family, the 25-100 Hz field SHAPE is wrong (t1, t2, p3, g2), and the corpora fix the source at (0.5, 1.6) and the L notch at NW (p1, D165). G3 continues to 120K (D168). Parked: the doorway aperture (D161; revisit with the modal prior), scale augmentation (retry only with the zero mode subtracted analytically, D165 p2), plane-wave / Helmholtz losses (behind m1).

(a) **m1 -- physics-only modal baseline, CPU, no training** (`scripts/p4_7_m1_modal.py`).
* **Rooms:** the 15 frozen test shapes (dense, `data/track_p4_5_testdense`); D111's dense family rooms, two per family (`family_test_rooms()` first + second pick, `data/track_p4_5_famdense`); 5 training rooms from C250 with per-room off-lattice receivers (B6, `data/track_p4_5_offlat`), chosen by rule: the calibration rectangle is the C250 training rectangle of median area, and the 4 L rooms sit at the 10 / 35 / 65 / 90th d_hat percentiles of C250's training L rooms.
* **Operator:** the solver's own discrete Laplacian, rebuilt from `fdtd_2d.build_geometry` / `build_coefficients` (dx 0.02 fitted per axis, the solver's solid mask, per-direction weights w_d, so walls are the solver's image-node Neumann condition). The discrete operator is self-adjoint in the mu-weighted inner product (mu 1 / 1/2 / 1/4) EXCEPT at reflex-corner nodes (the D65 residual: no diagonal symmetriser exists there). Primary: the mu-weighted symmetrised operator; its relative asymmetry is reported per room. Modes: every eigenpair with f_n <= 600 Hz including the zero mode, normalised sum_j mu_j psi_n(j)^2 dA = 1 (the grid quadrature of the integral of psi^2 dA = 1). Eigen-solve time reported per room.
* **Transfer function:** H(f) = C S(f) e^{i w dt} sum_n psi_n(x_r) psi_n(x_s) / (k_n^2 - k^2 + i k eta_n), eta_n = sum_j mu_j 2 (sum of blocked-face xi / dx_axis) psi_n(j)^2 dA, the grid form of beta times the boundary integral of psi_n^2 (first-order Robin perturbation, diagonal; beta = xi = tanh(m/4), 0.0406 at alpha 0.15, D165 p5). The sign is "+ i k eta" for the repo's rfft convention (e^{+i w t}; poles in the upper half plane, i.e. decaying modes), CHECKED on the calibration rectangle by fitting C under both signs. S(f) is the solver's known source spectrum (the stored target includes the pulse: a 0.04 s delay that no complex scalar can absorb) and e^{i w dt} the documented one-step alignment (`ir[:, k] = p^{k+1}`): both are source convention, not fitted. Receivers and the source are the solver's snapped nodes.
* **Calibration:** one global complex C, least squares over bins 40-600 and all receivers of the calibration rectangle, then frozen for every other room. Reported beside the analytic prediction C = dA / (c dt)^2 (from the semi-discrete form).
* **Scoring, above 20 Hz, off-lattice** (Rule 11): per-band r (per-bin column Pearson of dB, band-mean: exactly `p4_6_family_oof.scores` / `p4_5_speckle` colr) and LSD over 25-100 / 100-200 / 200-300 Hz, in family (15 test shapes; rect, L family rooms) and out of family (T, DN, U), beside PB3 @60K and G1 @60K from their existing JSONs (same receivers, same metric).
* **Zero-mode sanity check vs D101:** its amplitude psi_0^2 = 1/A and decay c eta_0 = c beta P / A (analytic); across the 30 rooms, report corr(|H_modal| near DC, 1/A) and corr(decay, P/A) against D101's 0.936 / 0.976, and the zero mode's exact time-domain response to the actual source over the 2 s record against the continuous-frequency term above 20 Hz (the record truncation D101(b) and D165 p2 found).
* **Pre-registered (manager):** if the OUT-OF-FAMILY 25-100 Hz r >= 0.85 (mean of T, DN, U; the network reads 0.60-0.63), physics carries the field shape the network cannot extrapolate, and m3 launches. **Operationalised (mine): "poor even in family" = in-family 25-100 Hz r < 0.85 on the 15 test shapes; then diagnose damping, truncation, operator mismatch and source convention before anything else** (each separately: eta x {0.5, 2}; modes to 800 Hz; the exact time-domain discrete synthesis per mode; the sign / S(f) / alignment factors), and m3 is not pre-registered until that is understood.

(b) **m2 -- configuration diversity, two arms at 60K, config-only** (`configs/sweep_2d_mat/P4_7_AFAM.yaml`, `P4_7_A500.yaml`, generated from G2 / G1 and differing only in run_id and three flags: `aug_d4_p 0.5`, `aug_recip_p 0.25`, `aug_d4_elems fx,fy,r180`).
* **Leakage pre-flight, run BEFORE launch** (`scripts/p4_7_m2_leak_preflight.py` -> `outputs/p4_7/m2_leak_preflight.json`). Topology read from each image polygon itself (1 cm raster of bbox minus polygon, solid components classified by the bbox sides they touch; U = two corner notches on the same wall, in any orientation). Every D4 image (all 8 elements) of every training room: **0 U-topology images in either corpus** (family holdU: 240 rooms -> rect / L / T / DN, 60 each, under every element; C500: 500 -> 8 rect / 492 L under every element), so 0 inside the U family's parameter ranges. Identity images reproduce every row's own family. Slab leaks: the four transposing elements carry 43 / 240 (holdU) and 88 / 500 (C500) rooms into the held-out d_hat slab [0.45, 0.60]; the flips carry 0.
* **Element choice by the manager's rule:** the holdU family corpus HAS a held-out d_hat slab (its 40 test rooms include 3 in-slab rooms per notched family), so A-fam uses the default flips; A-500 uses the flips (slab corpus). No element leaks the U family; none is dropped beyond the transposes the rule already excludes.
* **Pre-registered (manager):** configuration bias is a main cause of the out-of-family failure if **A-fam's held-out U at 25-100 Hz r rises by >= 0.10 over G2 (0.554)** AND **A-500's out-of-family mean (T, DN, U) at 25-100 Hz r rises by >= 0.10 over G1 (0.628)**. In-family 15-shape metrics should stay within the paired-bootstrap CI of the parents (G1 for A-500: test r 0.931 / 0.721 / 0.581; G2 for A-fam: in-family family rooms rect / L / T / DN); any drop is reported as the cost of dilution. Standard report per arm (D160), including the 4K kill signal.

(c) **m4 -- diverse corpus extension, CPU array, started now** (storage checked first: 86 GB free of 500 GB; C500's 515 rooms occupy 1.8 GB, so ~1.8 GB expected).
* **500 new rooms** ("CX500", `data/track_p4_7_cx500`): bbox L in [5, 7], W in [4, 5.5] (the corpora's ranges), quantised to the 0.02 m grid; 1 or 2 rectangular notches (p = 1/2 each) at random positions among the 4 corners and the 4 side midpoints (a side notch keeps >= 0.8 m shoulders, MIN_STEM_M). Notch depth d = u x 0.45 x (the room dimension normal to its wall), width w = v x 0.45 x (the dimension along it), u ~ U(0, 1), v ~ U(0.15, 1), as `multi_notch._draw_family`. **Excluded by redraw:** the U topology (two corner notches on the same wall, any orientation: everything in the U family's configuration and parameter ranges), any notch with d_hat in the held-out slab [0.45, 0.60] (so no room, or any flip of one, enters either frozen test set's slab), notches closer than 0.8 m to each other (MIN_PASSAGE_M) or overlapping, and any geometry failing the existing `geometry_ok`-style checks (bbox intact, connected, simple ring, no zero-width channel).
* **Source:** uniform in air, >= 0.5 m from every wall and every reflex corner. **Receivers:** 800 per room drawn from the room's own 0.02 m FDTD nodes (B6 style, off any shared lattice), >= 0.12 m from walls and 0.15 m from reflex corners (as B6), and >= 0.10 m from the source (mine: keeps receivers off the source node's near-field singularity). Walls alpha 0.15. FDTD at the corpus settings (dx 0.02, fs 30720, n 61440).
* **The trainer needs a per-room source** (it currently keeps only the LAST file's `source_pos`, silently): added behind a default-off flag, with a refusal when sources differ and the flag is off, and Rule-8 behavioural tests (default path bit-identical; per-room path renders each row with its own source).
* **Then:** G1's recipe on C500 + CX500 (1000 rooms) at 60K, standard report in family and out of family. No threshold was given; reported against G1.
